Rests on Harappa alone (data/derived/dark/loop5_cycle2.txt) | | S-DARK-5.3 | Signs as numbers, arrow 3: the short-stroke count written before W390/W405 (3-8; S204) predicted by the value (sum / product / positional) of the rest of the text, the numeral itself removed. 91 texts (3:28, 4:28, 5:13, 6:14, 7:5, 8:3); train Mohenjo-daro (49), test Harappa + Dholavira + Kalibangan + Lothal + Banawali + Chanhu-daro (42). Same annealer and controls as S-DARK-5.1; stroke-count rule scored | **Null.** Held-out rho of the best-train restart: sum 0.05 (seq_raw), -0.05 (strong/all); product 0.01; positional 0.00. Shuffled-sign control reaches 0.11 (sum) and 0.19 (product) over the same restarts; train rho up to 0.44 is pure overfit at n = 49. Stroke-count rule: test 0.01 / 0.04 / -0.03. No stable values. Planted control recovers 0.27 of a 0.28 truth, so the test had power for a moderate effect | The number counted before W390 is not computed from the other signs; it is an independent quantity (data/derived/dark/loop5_cycle3.txt) | | S-DARK-5.4 | Signs as numbers, arrow 4: seal thickness log(th) from inscriptions.csv (523 seals with thickness; rho(len, th) = 0.26), residualised on text length pooled across sites; train Mohenjo-daro + Harappa (470), test Dholavira, Kalibangan, Allahdino, Nausharo, Lothal, Banawali (53). Same annealer, controls and stroke-count rule as S-DARK-5.1 | **Null after the length leak is closed.** Nominal held-out rho of the sum rule 0.16 / 0.20 / 0.24 (raw/strong/all; best of 30 restarts 0.30-0.37) against shuffled-sign control 0.22 and permuted control -0.04, so it looked marginal. But the held-out seals keep a +0.13 length residual (pooled residualisation), the annealed values correlate 0.5-0.6 with text length, and an all-ones assignment already scores 0.12. Re-scored with thickness residualised on length WITHIN the held-out set: rho 0.01 / 0.08 / 0.08 (P = 0.9 / 0.6 / 0.6); within site x length 0.08-0.11. Product 0.05, positional 0.06. Stroke-count rule test 0.11-0.21 before, 0 after the same correction. No stable values (0/60). Planted: truth 0.15 -> recovered 0.12, values rho 0.44 | Thickness, like face area (S-DARK-5.1), tracks text length only. The all-positive sum rule is a text-length detector in disguise; any future 'numeric value' claim must beat the all-ones assignment on held-out sites (data/derived/dark/loop5_cycle4.txt) | | S-DARK-5.4b | Signs as numbers, arrow 5: do seal texts sum (or read positionally in base 10) to Indus weight-series values {1,2,4,8,16,32,64,160,200,320,640}? Anneal values 1-12 on the 60 commonest signs to maximise the hit rate on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa seals (1,232), score the hit rate on held-out-site seals (309). Nulls: the same annealing on a sign-shuffled corpus (lengths kept) and on 3 random 8-11-value sets drawn log-uniformly from 1-640; chance = random assignments. Stroke-count rule scored (tools/dark_loop5_weights.py) | **Null.** Real held-out hit rate 0.265 (sum; best restart 0.285) vs 0.278 (max 0.324) for the SHUFFLED corpus and 0.148 for random values: the annealer tunes any corpus to the series equally well, because small sums (2, 4, 8, 16, 32) are easy to hit by choosing values. Positional base-10: 0.074 real vs 0.065 shuffled (chance 0.024). Random weight sets reach 0.19-0.43 by the same mechanism. Stroke-count rule 0.230 (sum). Only W740 (jar) is stable across top-10 restarts, and in the shuffled corpus too (an artefact of its frequency) | No weight-series arithmetic in the texts. Closes the family: across 22 real arrows in five arms (size, thickness, voucher count, W390 count, weight series) and 4 combination rules, no numeric assignment beats its own null; nothing passes to the S107/S113 controls (data/derived/dark/loop5_cycle4b.txt) |

| S365 | **Arrow-in-the-dark machine** (`tools/strat_dark.py`, `tools/strat_dark_strict.py`): 3,000 random hypotheses per run = (random READING of a text: sign at a random position, random pair at distance d, text as a number in base b mod m, even/odd positions, first/last shape families, numeral sums, run lengths, etc.) × (random OUTSIDE FACT from data/raw/inscriptions.csv: type, emblem, material, colour, shape, cross-section, boss, direction, sides, find-area, period, size bins); MI with a permutation null; train on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa (500 perms, zero exceedances), replicate on held-out sites (1,000 perms, p < 0.01) and on seq_strong/seq_all. Three runs: scrambled facts (within site); real with site-stratified null; real with site × object-type-stratified null and emblem restricted to seals | Scrambled control: 0 of 3,000 replicate. First real run exposed a site confound (facts and texts both differ by city) and then a type confound. **Strict run: 1,471 training hits, 274 replicate, of which 216 are object type (opener→seal, suffix→tablet), 28 shape (square vs bar-seal format, S157), 11 size (text length → seal size, S330), 10 material within seals, 2 find-area (closers by quarter, S62–S63), 7 direction (weak, MI ≤ 0.05).** No reading × fact survives that is not already a known frame or format rule | **Null beyond rediscovery.** Random text-to-fact arrows at this corpus size only rediscover the frame. The machine is validated (clean control, recovers known rules) and is kept for new facts and new sites. Lesson logged: nulls must be stratified by site AND object type, or both confounds produce hundreds of false survivors |
| S-DARK-12.1 | Stock texts as denominations (a): do copies per stock text (complete text on >=3 objects; K=163, 1,288 copies at seq_raw) fall on a structured series (powers of 2, weight series 1-2-4-8-16-32-64), or conserve value (count x copies constant)? Nulls: copies reassigned multinomially among stock texts; log-normal fitted to log(copies); counts permuted across texts (5,000x each). All three seq levels; Harappa and Mohenjo-daro separately (`data/derived/dark/loop12_cycle1.txt`) | **Null everywhere.** Powers of 2: 36/163 vs 33.2 expected (P=0.32; seq_strong P=0.60, seq_all P=0.63; Harappa alone 10 vs 15.4, P=0.96; Mohenjo-daro 9 vs 10.7, P=0.79). Weight series: same. Count x copies among the 52 stock texts with one numeral: Spearman rho=-0.12 (p=0.40), CV 1.97 vs null 1.90 (P=0.62). The three voucher faces give 2x89=178, 3x120=360, 4x106=424 (rising). Copy counts form a smooth heavy tail (62 texts at 3, 26 at 4, ... 89, 106, 120) with no peaks | **Copy numbers are not denominations.** A stock text's number of surviving copies behaves like a Zipf tail (popularity), not an issued series; the voucher count does not trade off against how many were issued. 4 arrows fired, none below P=0.30 |
| S-DARK-12.2 | Stock texts as denominations (b, c). (b) Harappa two-faced tablets with one count face (tall 2-4 + W700) and one complete text face (292; TAB:B 160, TAB:I 132): does a text face with more copies carry a smaller count (value conservation), and do moulds with higher counts have fewer copies? Null: counts permuted among tablets within type (3,000x). (c) Do copies of one stock text (complete text on >=3 objects; 737 objects) share a find area / house block / period more than copies of different stock texts? Null: text labels permuted among objects within site x object type (2,000x). All seq levels (`data/derived/dark/loop12_cycle2.txt`) | **(b) Null:** Spearman(copies, mean count) +0.05 TAB:B (P=0.50), +0.01 TAB:I (P=0.95); mean count 2.94 for single-copy texts, 2.95 for 2-4 copies, 3.35 for 5+ copies (the most-copied texts carry the higher counts, not lower). Mould groups (identical text + count, 139): mean size 1.16 / 1.11 / 1.23 for counts 2 / 3 / 4, rho=+0.06 (p=0.52). **(c) Strong:** same-text pairs share a find area 0.519 vs 0.248 null (z=15.8, P<0.0005); Harappa 0.564 vs 0.191; Mohenjo-daro 0.393 vs 0.314 (P=0.03) and same house block 0.145 vs 0.076 (P=0.002); same period 0.572 vs 0.361 (z=6.6). Transcription levels agree within 0.01 | **The voucher count is not a denomination tied to issue size** (kills the value-conservation reading of S93-S95). **Copies of a stock text do cluster spatially**, but Harappa areas are also period-specific, so cycle 4 must separate batch from chronology before this counts |
| S-DARK-12.3 | Stock texts as denominations (d, e). (d) Are stock texts (complete text on >=3 objects) city-specific? Share of stock texts at a single site vs text labels permuted among objects within object type (1,000x), with the 2-copy sharing rate as baseline; all objects, seals only, tablets only, non-Harappa. (e) Do one-sign pot labels (103 complete) form a count series? Values of numeral-alone pot labels vs tablet count faces (`data/derived/dark/loop12_cycle3.txt`) | **(d) The split is by medium.** Tablet stock texts (78): 0.897 single-site vs 0.664 null (P<0.001; seq_all 0.909 vs 0.657); only 8 are shared, all Harappa + Mohenjo-daro. Seal stock texts (27): 0.111 single-site vs 0.206 null (more shared than chance, one-sided P~0.07); 24 of 27 occur at 2-5 sites (61-740 at 4 sites, 817-2-3-390 at 3, 820-2-3-390 at Nausharo). All objects 0.576 vs 0.403 (P<0.001). **(e) No series:** numeral-alone pot labels tall 1/2/3/4 = 5/7/10/6; tablets 3/119/177/137; same shape over 2-4 (chi2=0.32, p=0.85), a hump at 3, not a ladder; tall 1 is 18% of pot numerals but 0.7% of tablet counts (p~0.0002) | **Tablet stock texts are local issues, seal stock texts are pan-Indus formulae** (sharpens S336/S344 with a sharp 0.90 vs 0.11 single-site contrast). Pot numerals reuse the Harappa voucher values plus '1'; no denomination series anywhere. Rests on Harappa + Mohenjo-daro; 'single-site' is an upper bound (< 3/n) CORRECTION (S-DARK-23.4): counting every sherd in inscriptions.csv, lone pot tall values 1/2/3/4 = 8/39/19/8 (mode 2) against tablets' mode 3 (chi2 20.6, p < 0.0005); pot numerals are not voucher copies (gloss C: batch/lot number) |
| S-DARK-12.4 | Confirmatory for S-DARK-12.2(c): does the find-area clustering of stock-text copies survive forcing same period (strata = type x time), dropping the 5 and 15 most-copied texts, and splitting by medium; held-out city Mohenjo-daro (`data/derived/dark/loop12_cycle4.txt`) | **Harappa survives:** same-area rate within type x period 0.800 vs 0.581 null (ratio 1.38, P<0.001); top 5 texts dropped 0.655 vs 0.520 (P=0.002); top 15 dropped 0.543 vs 0.393 (P=0.004); moulded tablets 2.1x, incised 1.3x (both P<0.001); 22 of 46 texts cluster above their own null. Reverse: same period given same area 1.23x (P<0.001). **Mohenjo-daro:** area level weak, 1.14x (P=0.09-0.12); house-block level 1.9x (P=0.002-0.004); seals 1.8x (P=0.003-0.017, 6-9 texts). seq_all agrees throughout | **Copies of a stock text were deposited in batches** (same area, same period), beyond what period alone explains; sharpens S274 (same objects, so transcription-robust rather than replicated; the Mohenjo-daro house-block result is the partial held-out confirmation). Fits locally issued chits (S-DARK-12.3), not a value series. Rests mainly on Harappa |
| S-DARK-11.1 | Scribal confusions as plaintext. All pairs of distinct complete texts (>=3 signs) at edit distance 1 where one member is a frequent text (>=3 objects); the differing sign pair (a,b) is a candidate confusion. Null: keep the frame and sign a, redraw partner b from the sign distribution at the same position, length, site group and object class (1000x); indels: random-position null (2000x). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all, also minfreq 2 and fragments included (tools/dark11_confusions.py) | 189 / 221 / 228 substitution frames, 55 / 71 / 71 indel frames. (a) Recurrence of specific (a,b) pairs is NOT above chance: pairs recurring in >=2 frames 12 / 15 / 16 vs null 17 / 22 / 23 (P = 0.98-1.0); max frames per pair 3 vs null 4-5. (b) Slot agreement 0.93 / 0.90 / 0.90 vs null 0.51 / 0.43 / 0.42 (P = 0.001); pairs already merged or in a variant class 20 / 16 / 16 vs null 3.9 / 3.3 / 3.7 (P = 0.001); glyph similarity 0.60 / 0.58 / 0.58 vs 0.55 / 0.53 / 0.53 (P = 0.001). (c) Confusion rate per sign vs log frequency: rho 0.10 / 0.04 / 0.05 vs null 0.23 (P = 0.007 / 0.003 / 0.003), i.e. frequent signs are swapped less than their frequency predicts; not robust to including fragments (P = 0.28). (d) Same site 0.63 / 0.59 / 0.59 vs null 0.57 / 0.53 / 0.52 (P = 0.007-0.013); same find area 0.17 / 0.16 / 0.16 vs 0.11 / 0.12 / 0.12 (P = 0.003-0.013); needs cycle 2 (Harappa-tablet confound). (e) Deletable signs: W400 (10 of 55 indel frames, all final), W240 (6, all initial), W235 (3, initial), W2, W33, W803; the known deletable/qualifier set carries 20 of 55 deletions vs 11 expected (P = 0.0015). Recurring pairs not yet merged: 3<->4 and 4<->5 (stroke numerals), 176<->717 / 440 / 220 (seated-man title slot on Harappa tablets), 636<->904, 28<->840. | **Confusions are not scribal errors.** Specific substitutions recur no more than a frequency-weighted draw predicts, so there is no hidden set of systematic miscopies or missed allographs; the one-slot swaps respect the frame slots (93%), recover the known Wells merges blind (20 of 189 vs 4 expected) and otherwise exchange shape-unrelated words in one slot (S320 confirmed, transcription-robust). The frequency effect (c) and the optional-sign result (e: suffix W400 and the fish qualifiers W240/W235 drop out) fit a learned writing system with a fixed frame and optional modifiers, not a code copied from a model text. The new recurrent pairs are paradigm members (numerals, the pre-jar title class), not variants. |
| S-DARK-3.1 | Transformation screen: 16 transformations (reverse, interleave, middle-out, sort, shape families, numerals only, non-numerals, drop frame, BPE 5-80, split at numerals, rotate, emblem/object type as sign, relabel, random deletion) x 4 tests (name-calibration ratio, repetition vs shuffle, slot-entropy edge drop, arithmetic closure), 20 random parameterisations each, train = Mohenjo-daro + Harappa, seq_raw | Null: 20 sign-shuffles with the same transformation; identity and relabel arrows give z in [-2.1, 0.4]; Ur III names and legends under the same transformations (loop3_ur3_control.txt) | Frame-destroying transformations 'hit' the slot test trivially (re-states S17-S40). Non-trivial shifts: shape-family collapse makes the corpus look like khipu (uniqueness ratio 0.67, repetition 0.97 of chance, flat slots); reverse raises the ratio 0.78 -> 0.83 (direction asymmetry); numeral strings are more unique than their bigram model (1.36); random 10-30% deletion lifts the ratio to 0.92 (recurrence is carried by a few exact formulae). Nothing moves whole texts to the Ur III name value (0.43-0.62). All sent to cycle 2 with the random-class control. 64 arrows fired. | screen only |
| S-DARK-11.2 | Confounds and replication for S-DARK-11.1. (i) Site/area clustering of confusion pairs re-tested without Harappa tablets, on seals only, and with area labels permuted within site x object class x length (400x). (ii) Held-out replication: (a,b) substitution pairs fitted on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa (152 frames), tested on all other sites (312 complete texts, 28 frames, Lothal / Dholavira / Kalibangan / Chanhu-daro / Nausharo), random-partner null 500x. (iii) Missed-allograph scan: substitution pairs not in any Wells merge or variant tier, glyph similarity vs the same null (tools/dark11_cycle2.py; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all) | (i) The same-site excess vanishes without Harappa tablets: 0.42 / 0.44 / 0.44 vs null 0.47 / 0.46 / 0.46 (P = 0.89 / 0.67 / 0.66); seals only P = 0.61 / 0.17 / 0.17. The same-find-area excess is Harappa only (0.16 vs 0.10, P = 0.0025) and absent at Mohenjo-daro (0.20 vs 0.17, P = 0.18-0.29); identical-text duplicates share a find area 42% of the time. (ii) Held-out sites: frames whose (a,b) was seen at MD+H 8 / 6 / 6 vs null 2.1 / 1.3 / 1.5 (P = 0.002); already-merged pairs 7 / 3 / 3 vs 1.6 / 0.5 / 0.5 (P = 0.002-0.008); glyph similarity 0.65 / 0.58 / 0.58 vs 0.54 / 0.52 / 0.52 (P = 0.002); slot agreement 0.89 of 9 vs 0.57 (P = 0.05; seq_strong 0.67, P = 0.13). Replicated pairs: 817/820/861 openers, 3<->4, 705<->706, 705<->33, 390<->407, 233<->235. (iii) Non-merged frames 302 / 377 / 382; glyph similarity 0.56 / 0.55 / 0.55 vs null 0.535 / 0.524 / 0.523 (P = 0.002); frames with similarity >= 0.85: 20 / 22 / 22 vs null 9.3 / 10.1 / 9.4, max 19-20 (P = 0.002). Unclassed high-similarity pairs: 384<->388, 27<->28 (both M119), 850<->851, 61<->63, 525<->526, 783<->785 | **Site clustering was the Harappa-tablet confound** (tablet duplicates and near-duplicates deposited together; S63b), not local scribal habits: no site or quarter signal survives on seals or at Mohenjo-daro. **The substitution structure itself replicates at held-out sites** (transcription-robust and site-robust): the same openers, numerals, U-stroke variants and tree variants swap at Lothal, Dholavira, Kalibangan and Chanhu-daro. There is a small residual of shape-similar unmerged pairs (about 10 frames above chance), tested one by one in S-DARK-11.3. |
| S-DARK-11.3 | (A) Allograph test for the unmerged shape-similar substitution pairs of S-DARK-11.2: context sharing (mean cosine of left and right neighbour profiles, whole corpus) vs 200 frequency-matched partners, plus site and medium split; calibrated on the 16 strong Wells merges. (B) Optional signs: indel frames (one text = the other plus one sign, one member frequent), deleted sign vs a null matched on position class and object class (1000x); direction (is the longer or the shorter text the commoner?). (C) Confusion rate per sign vs log frequency with a position-matched null (tools/dark11_cycle3.py; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all) | (A) Calibration: 12 of 16 strong merges pass the context test (P <= 0.05); site/medium split does not separate them (5 of 16 are split), so split is not diagnostic. Candidates: **384<->388** ctx 0.82 vs null 0.39 (P = 0.005 at all three levels, glyph 0.87, same site and medium distribution, 29/58 tokens) = allograph-like; **27<->28** ctx 0.97 vs 0.32 (P = 0.04, glyph 0.93, 6/5 tokens; Mahadevan already has both as M119); 390<->407 ctx 0.70 vs 0.33 (P = 0.005-0.035) with a medium split (0.35, P = 0.03) = medium variant of the tree, like 390/405; 525<->526 ctx P = 0.02 (11/13 tokens); 233<->235 and 235<->240 score as allographs on context (P = 0.035-0.065) but are the S296 fish word family; 850<->851 is site-split (0.77, P = 0.02; 851 = 7 tokens) = local form or local word; 61<->63, 32<->240, 220<->235, 17<->615, 140<->233 fail (different words). (B) 55 / 71 / 71 indel frames; the shorter text is the commoner one in 37 / 49 / 49 (longer texts are expansions of a base formula); the extra sign is initial in 32 / 39 / 39 vs 13 / 17 / 17 expected, medial in 8 / 14 / 14 vs 29 / 38 / 38. Optional signs: W400 (M176) 10 / 12 / 12 frames, all final, vs 2.7-3.1 expected (P = 0.001); W240 (M67) 6 / 8 / 8, initial, vs 1.5-1.8 (P = 0.001); W235, W803, W2, W33 2-4 each (P = 0.07-0.3); W519 2 (7 tokens, P = 0.002-0.005). The known deletable/qualifier set carries 28 / 36 / 36 of the deletions vs 10.4 / 13.7 / 13.7 (max 23-26; P = 0.0005). The jar is deleted in 1 frame vs 6-7 expected and the opener in 1 vs 4.5-6. (C) rho 0.10 / 0.04 / 0.05 vs position-matched null 0.19 / 0.16 / 0.15 (P = 0.13 / 0.09 / 0.13): not significant, though confusion events per 100 tokens fall from 5.6-6.2 (10-30 tokens) to 0.3-0.8 (>= 300 tokens) | **Two likely missed merges: W384 = W388 (B) and W27 = W28 (B, Mahadevan agrees); W407 is a medium variant of the tree W390 (B); W525 may belong to the W526/527 box class (C).** Otherwise the Wells merge list is complete at the one-slot level: there is no pool of unrecognised allographs. **Optional signs are exactly the grammar's qualifiers**: the suffix W400 after the jar and the fish qualifier W240 (then W235, W803, W33) before a base formula; frame members (opener, jar) are not optional. Frequent-text variants are expansions (base + one prefixed qualifier), not omissions, so the near-duplicates of a formula are qualified copies, not miscopies. The learned-script frequency effect is suggestive only (fails the position-matched null). |
| S-DARK-4.1 | **Are the units wrong? Search over segmentations.** (a) BPE merges of adjacent signs learned on MD+Harappa (k = 5–60, greedy or random tie-break among top-5, 20 segmentations); (b) sign-identity level: seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all plus 8 random subsets of the allograph list and 8 shape-blind frequency-matched FAKE merge lists; (c) sub-sign: marked sign → base + MARK (41 containment pairs), ligature → A + B (13), 9 variants. Per segmentation, on the held-out named sites (943 texts, 2,480 sign tokens): bits per original sign (interpolated bigram, unseen units spelled by the sign model), text uniqueness / bigram-null uniqueness (S321 statistic), within-text repetition ratio, share of sign tokens in slot-pure units (S310 parser). Arrows-fired null: the whole search re-run on 10 corpora generated from the sign-level bigram of the fit set; best delta per statistic recorded. Controls: Ur III seal legends (word boundaries known) and a planted Tamil logo-syllabic corpus (`loop4_seg.py`, `loop4_controls.py`) | **Nothing beats the sign level on predictability AND name/lexicon-likeness.** Best BPE −0.045 bits/sign (−0.6%; seq_strong −0.048, seq_all −0.046), but it raises the uniqueness ratio (1.03 → 1.05, texts LESS lexicon-like) and cuts grammar coverage from 0.444 to 0.287 (merged units straddle slots). Its merges are the known frame: 100+740, 817+2, 760+740, 705+33+520, 920+60 (M8+M342, M267+M99, M347+M342, M336+M89+M211, M293+M123). Bigram-null: BPE never helps there (best +0.048 to +0.076), so the −0.045 is real beyond-bigram structure, i.e. the frame phrases already in GRAMMAR.md. Sub-sign decomposition hurts (median +0.08 bits; ligatures-only neutral, −0.002). Allograph merging lowers bits trivially (lossy): real list −0.150, FAKE frequency-matched lists −0.068 median / −0.097 best, same list on bigram-null data −0.110; the real list wins by ~0.04–0.05 bits, a small vote for the allograph list, not for a hidden lexicon. 'Joint' winners (bits and rep) 7 of 29 on seq_raw, but the null gives 3–7 too. Controls: Ur III sign level 4.672 → BPE 4.517 ≈ true words 4.513 bits (−3.3%, 77% of merges word-internal, rep 0.61 → 0.06 at the word level); planted 6.772 → BPE 6.636, true words 6.247 (−7.8%, 83% word-internal). Indus −0.6% is a fifth of the Ur III gain and the Indus uniqueness ratio stays at 1.0 under every segmentation (Ur III words 1.13, planted words 1.18, Indus 1.01–1.05) | **No hidden lexicon under a wrong segmentation. The best units are the ones GRAMMAR.md already lists.** Robust across seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all. The method would have found the word level in Ur III (and does). Caveat: the planted control shows the test is weak for a large Zipf lexicon on 13k tokens (BPE recovers only 26% of the true-word gain there), so a lexicon of thousands of rare names would not be seen; a lexicon of a few hundred recurring words would. | | S-DARK-4.2 | **Direct hidden-lexicon test: do sign chunks recur across sites more than chaining predicts?** Distinct pairs and triples shared between the fit sites and a held-out set (other sites; Dholavira; Lothal; Kalibangan; MD→Harappa swap), in all signs / NAME residue / frame signs, three levels (90 arrows). Null A: both sets regenerated from the fit set's bigram model, same lengths (100×); null B: signs shuffled across texts within each set (`loop4_chunks.py`) | **All 90 rows fall BELOW the bigram null (0 above; Bonferroni threshold P < 0.0006, none significant).** Mean observed/bigram-median: all signs 0.69, frame 0.85, NAME residue 0.54 (pairs 0.47–0.65 across the five splits, triples 0.34–0.73). All far above the shuffle null (NAME 11×), so the bigram structure itself is real. Example: NAME pairs shared MD+H ↔ other sites 223 vs bigram median 405 (seq_raw), 228 vs 398 (seq_strong), 228 vs 400 (seq_all) | **Opposite of a hidden lexicon: name chunks recur across cities LESS than sign-by-sign chaining predicts.** Consistent with S17/S19 (middles unique to one city) and S311 (name-internal grammar). Caveat: null A assumes one vocabulary for all sites, so part of the deficit is site vocabulary difference (the MD↔Harappa swap shows the same 0.54, so it is not only small-site sampling). Replicated on three held-out sites and the city swap; rests on MD+Harappa for the fit side as always. | | S-DARK-4.3 | **Segmentation search inside the NAME residue only** (S310 parser learned on MD+Harappa; 2,493 fit name runs, 695 held-out, mean 2.3 signs): BPE (20) + sub-sign (9) segmentations, same statistics; arrows-fired null on 10 bigram-generated name corpora. Positive control: Ur III seal-owner names at the syllable level (`seal_line1.txt`, 23,029 names, 3,364 distinct), run twice: with repeats (a real onomasticon) and distinct only (one copy per name, as the Indus corpus is deduplicated by site + text) (`loop4_names.py`) | **Indus names: best bits −0.024 (seq_raw; −0.019 strong/all), null best +0.009 to +0.025, so marginally real but tiny; uniqueness 1.04–1.07 at the sign level; sub-sign decomposition lowers the uniqueness ratio by 0.06–0.08 but the null lowers it by 0.08–0.12 (an artefact of splitting signs); joint winners 0 of 29 on seq_strong and seq_all (3 of 29 on seq_raw, null 0).** The name-residue BPE merges are 920+60, 692+60, 920+60+741, 2+803, 861+2 — the S331/S334 opener units the S310 parser does not yet strip. **Ur III names with repeats: uniqueness 0.278 (names recur 3.6× more than bigram), BPE −0.239 bits (−5.4%).** **Ur III DISTINCT names: uniqueness 1.105, BPE −0.053 bits, rep 1.5 — statistically the same picture as the Indus residue.** | **The Indus name residue looks exactly like a deduplicated onomasticon, and a deduplicated onomasticon shows no lexicon signal either.** So this family of tests cannot distinguish 'names built from a hidden syllabary' from 'names written sign-for-sign' once each text is counted once — the duplicates (same seal impressed many times, same owner on several seals) are what carry the lexicon signal in Ur III, and the Indus corpus has been stripped of them (S268/S312). Lead for another loop: redo on the undeduplicated impression counts (Lothal/Kalibangan sealings, Harappa tablets) where the same text recurs. Do not promote; the S310 parser should add the S331 openers (920/692 + 60). | | S-DARK-4.4 | **Minimum description length: does any lexicon pay for itself?** Two-part code (corpus under a unigram or bigram code with (V−1)/2·log₂N parameter cost + lexicon spelled with the sign unigram code), greedy BPE k = 1–80 on the whole corpus; minimum of the DL curve. Indus three levels, MD+Harappa alone; 10 bigram-generated Indus nulls; Ur III distinct legends and the planted corpus with their true word segmentations (`loop4_mdl.py`) | **Indus, unigram code: gain rises monotonically to k = 80 (+5.5–5.8%), no interior minimum; bigram null +4.0 to +4.5% (max 4.51). Bigram code: best k = 0–10, gain 0.00–0.09%; null 0.00.** Ur III: unigram +16.7% (true words +10.4%), bigram +0.5% (true words −12.7%, the lexicon of 2,588 words is too dear under a bigram code). Planted (3,000-word Zipf lexicon): unigram +1.9%, true words −6.5%: a large rare lexicon does not pay at 13k tokens | **No lexicon size at which chunking pays under a bigram code; under a unigram code the Indus exceeds the bigram null by only ~1 point and shows a fifth of the Ur III gain, with no natural lexicon size.** The ~1 point is again the frame phrases. Same on all three levels and on MD+Harappa alone. The planted control shows MDL cannot see a large Zipf lexicon on a corpus this size, so this is evidence against a SMALL hidden lexicon (hundreds of recurring words), not against thousands of rare names. |

| S-DARK-14.2 | Workshop signatures (b): do allograph variants (raw Wells form vs merged form, 20 strong+probable sets of `sign_allographs_levels.json`, seq_raw by necessity) track physical workshop clusters (Gower/Ward on boss, material, colour, shape, cross-section, log h/v/th; k=6; no emblem, no text) more than site? Statistic: sum of G(variant x grouping); nulls: labels permuted within site x object type (and also within coarse emblem class); 3,000x; 14 arrows, Holm (`tools/dark_loop14_c2.py`) | **Variants do not follow physical clusters** (MD z +1.3 P 0.09; Harappa z +0.9 P 0.18; other sites z +1.0 P 0.16; same with emblem held fixed). **Variants follow SITE** (G 86 vs 51, z +3.7, P 0.001, Holm 0.014), carried by W390/405 (Harappa 405:390 = 23:27 vs MD 23:117) and W803/806 (Harappa 806:803 = 29:11 vs MD 39:67), plus W705/706. Harappa only: variant x find-area z +3.1 P 0.002 (Holm 0.028) and variant x unicorn style letter z +2.9 P 0.005 (Holm 0.05); neither replicates at MD (P 0.73, 0.07) or the other sites (P 0.04, 0.39) | **Variant choice is a city habit, not a workshop or a seal-type habit.** Caveat: the site split is partly built in, since S268 condition 3 accepted merges that show complementary distribution over site x medium; so the site result confirms the construction rather than testing it. New content is the negative: within a city, seals that look alike physically (boss, stone, size) do not share a sign form. The Harappa area/style hint is one city, two arrows, not replicated: noted, not claimed |
| S-DARK-10.4 | Random ontologies, cycle 4, sequence levels: the full-corpus search re-run on `seq_raw` (original Wells numbers) and `seq_strong` (strong merges only), 40 ontologies each with P and S controls; MDL co-rates checked for the S268 allograph pairs that are separate signs in seq_raw | **Cross-criterion stable pairs: 1 (raw: W405–W900, 0.70) and 1 (strong: W31–W55, 0.70); neither recurs in any other run (Jaccard 0.00 between all 7 runs; S-DARK-10.1's W156–W154 and these are three different pairs).** Held-out facts over all 4 full-corpus runs (177 ontologies): only object type is above the order-shuffled control (0.28 vs 0.24 bits/text; 2 of 14 above the control maximum), emblem 0/11, period 0/6, size 2/11, material 2/10, quantity consistency 0/46, cross-site reuse 0/37. **Blind merge recovery:** on `seq_raw` the MDL criterion puts every strong S268 pair among the top 60 in one class in 13 of 13 ontologies: 861–817 (S control 0.85), 820–817 (0.77), 803–806 (0.54), 705–706 (0.77), 390–405 (0.54); the doubtful pair 156–151 scores 0.46 (control 0.38). Pooled MDL classes over 53 ontologies (all levels): {817, 861, 820, 920, 692, 550}, {240, 235, 231, 803, 806, 31, 140, 3}, {400, 90}, {390, 405, 407, 900}, {690, 760}, {1, 2, 368}, {60, 527} | **The result does not depend on the merge level, and the sequence objective independently supports the S268 allograph list** (strong merges cluster at 1.00, the doubtful W156/W151 does not). The only ontology-free structure in the sign list is the slot structure already in GRAMMAR.md; the random-ontology family is closed as a null. Leads to carry: W803/W806 as the pre-fish element; W550 as a fourth opener companion (920-550, 692-550, 817-550 at 0.92–0.94); W368 between opener and connective |
| S-DARK-13.1 | **Arrows in the dark, TRADE-BALANCE family (loop 13, cycle 1): is a site's sealing vocabulary complementary to its own seal vocabulary (what arrives = what it does not issue)? 5 sites with >= 5 seals and >= 5 sealings; cosine and Jaccard of sign frequencies and cosine of closer distributions, seals vs sealings; null = sealing site labels permuted within length class 2,000x; with and without collapsing identical impressions; seq_raw/strong/all (`data/derived/dark/loop13_engine.py 1`)** | All impressions counted: Kalibangan sealings are far LESS like Kalibangan seals than chance (cos 0.52 vs null 0.76, p < 0.001; closer-cos 0.50 vs 0.75, p = 0.017), Harappa cos 0.59 vs 0.75 (p = 0.02), summed z over sites -10.4. **After collapsing identical impressions the effect disappears: Kalibangan cos 0.73 vs 0.70 (p = 0.63), summed z -1.4 (cos), -1.4 (closers), +3.9 (Jaccard, i.e. own sealings share slightly MORE sign types with own seals).** The whole non-dedup signal is 8 copies of one Kalibangan impression (91-56-2-417-890-892) and the 10 Lothal bale sealings. Same on all three seq levels; no result depends on a merge | **Null.** No site receives a sign, closer or commodity vocabulary complementary to what it issues; if anything a site's sealings and seals share types a little more than chance (local dialect, not trade balance). Lesson: repeated impressions of one seal must be collapsed before any sealing-vs-seal vocabulary test, or one bale shipment produces a p < 0.001 'effect' |
| S-DARK-13.2 | **Loop 13, cycle 2: do the counted items (N·tree, N·bracket, fish classes) on a site's sealings differ from those on its seals in one consistent direction (imports vs exports) at Lothal, Kalibangan and Harappa, with Dholavira and Mohenjo-daro held out? 12 text features; label (seal/sealing) permuted within site 2,000x; Bonferroni over 60 tests; dedup of identical impressions; seq_raw/strong/all (`loop13_engine.py 2`)** | **0 of 60 tests survive Bonferroni at any seq level, dedup or not.** Raw p < 0.05 only: Dholavira sealings have fewer tree signs (-0.10, p = 0.04) and fewer numerals (-0.17, p = 0.035), Kalibangan sealings have no fish (0 of 15 vs 25% of seals, p = 0.05; upper bound < 20%). The only feature with the same sign at L, K, H and both held-out sites is text length: sealings are 0.5-1.6 signs shorter than seals everywhere (Dholavira p = 0.001, Lothal p = 0.014), which is impression breakage, not content. N·tree: Lothal -0.045, Kalibangan +0.013, Harappa -0.046, Mohenjo-daro +0.023, i.e. no direction. N·bracket is absent from all 224 sealings except Dholavira/Harappa (upper bound < 1.3%). Identical at seq_strong and seq_all | **Null.** Counted goods on sealings are the same mix as on seals at every site; no import/export asymmetry is visible. With 15-61 distinct sealing texts per site the test could only have seen a shift of about 20 percentage points, so a weaker asymmetry remains possible but untested. Absences (no fish on Kalibangan sealings, no N·bracket on any sealing) are upper bounds, not evidence |
| S-DARK-13.3 | **Loop 13, cycle 3: site x site flow graph from matched seal->sealing texts (exact, S324, plus edit-distance 1); is flow reciprocal (A->B implies B->A) or hierarchical (hub -> periphery)? Near-match graph (47 sealings, 45 edge-weights): reciprocity 0.15 vs null A 0.09 (p = 0.08), corr(out, in) +0.16 vs 0.13 (p = 0.30); no cell deviates from null A at p < 0.07 (Lothal -> Dholavira 1.25 vs 0.5, p = 0.09; Kalibangan -> Dholavira 0.67 vs 0.17, p = 0.08). Null B is degenerate on a 6-edge star (no alternative wiring). Without Mohenjo-daro seals: 1-3 exact matches (Harappa -> Lothal), near graph led by Harappa -> Lothal 9-10 (Harappa holds 49% of remaining seals) | **Null for structure; a correction to S324/S326.** 'All 18 matches are cross-site' is what seal counts predict (93%) because the sealings come from Lothal, Kalibangan and small sites, not from the cities that own the seals. 'Flow only outward from Mohenjo-daro' is mostly its 63% share of long seal texts; the residual Mohenjo-daro excess (8 of 9 distinct texts, p = 0.012, uncorrected over about 150 arrows this loop) is suggestive, not established. No reciprocity and no hierarchy beyond seal counts can be shown with 9 distinct matched texts; the Lothal bales (S325, S330) stay the one physical case |

Non-dedup Kalibangan p = 0.004 and Lothal p = 0.004 are again the 8 Kalibangan copies and 10 Lothal bale copies. Fastening type at Lothal: MI(fastening, jar-final) 0.21 vs 0.07 (p < 0.001) and MI(fastening, opener-first) p = 0.02 with all impressions, **both null after collapsing identical texts (n = 67: p = 0.36, 0.92)**; the 'wooden boxes' class is 10 of 15 the one bale text 817-2-48-740; the 8-sign Lothal text 705-500-741-1-55-220-740-90 sits on pottery, boxes, straw, leather sacks, structures and pegs (11 impressions, 6 fastening types). Same at all seq levels | **Null.** Pot labels do not name arriving consignments; the same seal text is impressed on every container type at Lothal, so the sealing text names the sealer, not the goods or the packaging (fits S325, S329). Container type is not recoverable from the text |
| S-DARK-15.3 | Quantity gaps / minimum-lot rule: for 46 items counted >= 10 times (numeral immediately before the item, all sites, seq_raw/strong/all), does the value distribution lack 1 and 2 beyond a null in which numeral values are permuted among items within series (short/tall) x region x object class (2,000x)? '2 tree' exists 13x at home as TALL 2 (32-407 in the seven-copy tablet 231-17-585-95-520-32-407; 32-390 x4), 'short 2 tree' only as the W2 marker (M-1851; K-78, K-431, Ns-67 after an opener). Persons: home 100/728 numbered, held-out 18/138; plain person W90/91 2/196 at home, 2/… abroad; the numbered figures are fixed terms (3-156 x35, 2/3-140 x14, 1-142 x10 = W1 marker) | **Writing rule, not lot size.** Short-stroke counts start at 3 for every item because the short 1, short 2 and tall 1 glyphs are grammar markers; 1 and 2 are written only with tall strokes and only in fixed terms. The '1 tree never / 2 tree once' hole dissolves (2-tree is written 32-390/407). S98 refined: figure signs take fixed numerals; only the plain person is never counted (same at held-out sites). Prediction: no new text with short 1 or 2 before a counted good **DEMOTED by S-DARK-21.1: on 210 IM77-only texts, 3 of 19 counted goods carry a short single stroke before the good (16% vs the frozen 5% kill rate, prereg 5); status now 'strong tendency on seals', not a rule.** |
| S-DARK-17.1 | **Institution generator + ABC design.** Agent-based credential corpus (authorities A, offices O, commodities C, holders H, credentials/holder k, middle shared per holder or fresh per credential, tablet re-issue, batch deposit, travelling seals, 21 parameters) laid over the real excavation skeleton (site, class, find-area recorded) of 3,520 complete texts; 34 data-driven summary statistics (nesting, cross-site runs, middle uniqueness vs bigram, stock-text copies and area clustering, tablet/seal locality, closer inventory and Zipf, quantity seals, sealing->seal cross-site, lengths by class, sign richness). Control: prior-predictive coverage of the observed vector (`tools/dark_loop17.py`, `data/derived/dark/loop17_cycle1.txt`) | Observed vector stable across seq_raw/strong/all (e.g. nest 0.12-0.13, miduniq 0.75-0.76, area_ratio 1.95-2.11, loc_tab 0.84, loc_seal 0.14-0.16, tag_cross 0.96, signs/1000 48-49). First pilot: 4 of 34 statistics unreachable (richness, seal duplicates, pot and suffix mixes) -> 3 nuisance parameters added, H and V_mid widened; then 33 of 34 inside the prior range (tag_cross needs p_travel ~ 1) | **Set-up only; no verdict on the institution yet.** The model can reach the real corpus; fitting follows in 17.2. Everything rests on Harappa + Mohenjo-daro (76% of texts) and on the skeleton of complete objects |
| S-DARK-16.1 | Usage intensity (a): how many impressions per distinct sealing text, and do heavy-use texts differ from singletons? 224 TAG lines; spectrum per site vs seal texts of the same site sub-sampled to TAG n (1000x); features of repeated vs singleton texts with copy counts shuffled within site (3000x, 24 arrows, Bonferroni 0.0021); seq_raw/strong/all | Lothal 70 lines -> 47 distinct, two seals account for 21 impressions (705-500-741-1-55-220-740-90 x11, 817-2-48-740 x10); Kalibangan 19 -> 12, one seal x8; Dholavira 35 -> 35 and Harappa 11 -> 11 (every sealing a different seal); Mohenjo-daro 15 -> 13. Sealings repeat texts more than same-site seals at Lothal, Kalibangan, Mohenjo-daro (p < 0.001) but not more than tablets (p 0.4-1.0). Spectrum is 'one or two heavy users + singletons', not Zipf. No text feature marks the heavy tail after correction; openers lead the repeated texts (ALL p_hi 0.031 raw, same sign Lothal and Mohenjo-daro). Identical at all three merge levels | **Null-leaning, descriptive.** Heavy use is a deposit effect (one bale seal stamped 8-11 times in one warehouse), not a class of credential; with 11-35 distinct texts per site anything subtler is below the floor. 'Dholavira 35 of 35 distinct' is an upper bound (< 3/35 repeated) |
| S-DARK-16.2 | Usage intensity (b): 'ghost issuers'. Distinct sealing texts (>= 2 signs, 133; >= 3 signs, 87) with no exact seal match anywhere; share per site; chance control = seal texts scrambled within text (300x); Chapman capture-recapture of the seal-text population; ghost vs matched features with the label permuted within site and within site x length (3000x, 31 arrows, Bonferroni 0.0016); seq_raw/strong/all | 111 of 133 sealing texts (83%) have no seal anywhere; at >= 3 signs 78 of 87 (90%), and 67 occur nowhere else at all. Only 5 of 22 matches are same-site, all 2-sign texts; scrambling predicts 8.6 of the 22 by chance (1.1 of the 9 at >= 3 signs). Ghost share by site 75-91%, Dholavira 15/15 and Mohenjo-daro 7/7 at >= 3 signs. Capture-recapture: S=1898, T=133, m=22 -> ~11,000 distinct seal texts (within-site m = 0-3, so sites give lower bounds only, ~950 Dholavira). Matched texts are jar-final 59% vs ghosts 30% (length-controlled p 0.007, same sign at Lothal and Dholavira) and never contain W32 (0/22 vs 18/111); nothing passes Bonferroni. Identical at all merge levels | **Descriptive, agrees with S309/S324.** The seal record behind the sealings is almost entirely missing: the seals that were actually used at Lothal, Dholavira and Kalibangan are not in the excavated corpus (lost, abroad, or at unexcavated sites). A capture-recapture figure of order 10^4 distinct seal texts is a rough upper-type bound that matches S309's '95% of new texts are new'. Office (jar) texts are the ones that do recur on surviving seals; W32-containing sealing texts have no seal counterpart (upper bound < 3/22) |
| S-DARK-16.5 | Usage intensity (e): do multi-sided tablets and sealings repeat one text on several sides (counting device) or carry different texts (ledger)? 793 objects with >= 2 inscribed lines; identical-side and shared-sign rates vs random side pairs from the same site x object type (1000-2000x); repeated with voucher 'N . W700' sides and single-sign sides removed; seq_raw/strong/all | 0 of 793 objects repeat a text (TAB:B 0/342 vs 4.6 expected, p 0.008; TAB:I 0/340 vs 9.2, p 0.001; upper bound < 3/793). Sides share a sign far less than random pairs (TAB:B 36 vs 97.5, TAB:I 46 vs 84, TAB:C 1 vs 15.4, all p < 0.001) because 415 of 682 Harappa tablets pair one count side with one text side (176-740-400 x29, 3-861 x22, 3-156 x21). With count sides removed the two text sides are at chance at Harappa (TAB:B 31 vs 35.6; TAB:I above chance only through W3 in 3-861 | 3-156) and still complementary on Mohenjo-daro TAB:C (1 of 16 share a sign vs 4.8, p 0.014). Multi-sided Lothal tags carry one constant bale text (x11) plus a different short text on four tags. Same at all merge levels | **Strong for 'ledger, not tally'.** A tablet is never two impressions of one text; it is one count plus one title/office text (Harappa), or two unrelated texts (Mohenjo-daro TAB:C). The Lothal two-seal bales (constant shipper text + variable second seal) read like a sender/receiver pair, which is what a sealing system needs and what S29's 'suffix = act of sealing' predicted. Transcription-robust only; rests on Harappa (86% of multi-sided objects) |
| S-DARK-18.1 | **S321/S322 redone at matched granularity.** The Indus corpus is one row per OBJECT and is NOT deduplicated (5,369 rows, 3,599 distinct site+text; seals 2,250 rows, 2,121 distinct texts), so S321's Indus side already counted every object; the Ur III side (CDLI) is one row per tablet impression. Three matched levels: (1) object level: every Indus SEAL* row (1,744 middles) vs every Ur III `@seal` section on an Ur III tablet plus every seal object (21,047 impressions, 1,795 distinct names); (2) deduplicated: distinct Indus site+text (2,663 middles) vs distinct full Ur III legends (5,490, one per seal); (3) one physical seal: Indus SEAL* rows one per cisi+text (1,744) vs distinct Ur III legends, and separately vs CDLI physical seal objects (4,356 'seal (not impression)' records). Name = line 1 of the legend before a title/kin word (S322's whole-legend rule run as a check). Statistic: share of unique strings / bigram-null share trained on the same sample, same lengths, SIZE-MATCHED to the smaller side, 50 draws; Zipf slope of unit (sign / syllable) frequencies in the name slot (`loop18_matched.py 1`) | **Indus 0.97 vs Ur III 0.43 at object level (n=1,744 each); Indus 1.01 vs Ur III 0.56 deduplicated (n=2,663); Indus 0.97 vs Ur III 0.60 (distinct legends) / 0.62 (physical seal objects) one-per-seal (n=1,744).** Ur III unique share at the seal level 0.33–0.35 vs its bigram 0.55–0.57; Indus 0.84 vs 0.87. Including all Indus object types (tablets, pots) at object level gives 0.83, because Harappa tablets repeat whole texts (33-700 ×123), not because middles repeat. Zipf slope, top-100 units: Indus −0.88, Ur III −0.87 (all ranks −1.36 vs −1.61): the unit frequencies are equally Zipfian, so the difference is in whole-string reuse, not in the sign inventory. Name rule makes no difference (S322 rule: 0.44 / 0.63 / 0.64) | **S321/S322 survive at every matched granularity: the 0.62 was not an impression-count effect.** Counting impressions pulls Ur III down to 0.43, but one-legend-per-seal still gives 0.60–0.62 against Indus 0.97, a gap of 0.35 at identical n. The Indus middle population does not reuse strings the way a population of seal-owner names does. |
| S-DARK-18.2 | **Person-level statistic independent of impressions: how many distinct SEALS share one name / one middle (homonymy).** Ur III: distinct legends and physical seal objects grouped by line-1 name; Indus: distinct seals (cisi+text) grouped by middle; same at n=1,744 with each side's own bigram null (50 draws). Planted control: 3,000-name stock generated from the Ur III syllable bigram with Zipf weights (a = 0.6 / 1.0 / 1.3), 8,000 seals, each impressed k ~ geometric (mean 3) with 5% heavy users ×20; measured at impression, seal and distinct-string level (`loop18_matched.py 2`) | **Ur III: 78% of distinct legends carry a name found on another seal (3.06 seals per name; ur-Baba on 118 seals, lu2-dingir-ra 84, ur-Lamma 81); physical seal objects 74% (2.69 per name, ur-Baba 98). Indus: 15.6% of seals share their middle with another seal (1.11 seals per middle; max 14 for 3-390, then 590-390 ×9, 4-390 ×9, 32-220 ×8, 415-100 ×8).** Size-matched: Ur III legends 0.67 shared vs bigram null 0.45 (1.49×); Indus seals 0.156 vs 0.124 (1.25×, i.e. the Indus excess over chance is 32 seals, concentrated in numeral + W390 / W220 strings that are probably count phrases, not names). Planted: impressions 0.42 / 0.36 / 0.31, seals 0.64 / 0.57 / 0.46, distinct strings 1.31 / 1.31 / 1.23 regardless of homonymy. Real Ur III names deduplicated by NAME STRING: 1.175 | **Ur III name populations are name-like because the same name sits on 2.7–3.1 different seals; Indus middles sit on 1.11 seals each, within 25% of a random sign generator.** The loop-4 caveat ('Ur III distinct names give 1.105, like the Indus residue 1.04–1.07') is a construction artefact: deduplicating by the name string forces uniqueness to 1.0 and the ratio to 1.2–1.3 for ANY population (planted control), so it says nothing about homonymy. The correct dedup unit is the seal, and at that unit Ur III stays at 0.6. The planted control also shows the impression → seal step moves the ratio by only ~0.1–0.2, matching the real Ur III 0.43 → 0.60. |
| S-DARK-18.3 | **Replication: Mohenjo-daro vs Harappa vs Lothal+Kalibangan+Dholavira+Chanhu-daro; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all; Ur III legends resampled to each Indus n; Indus sealings (TAG*) as the impression analogue** (`loop18_matched.py 3`) | Indus physical-seal ratio: MD 0.97–0.98 (n=1,081), Harappa 1.02–1.03 (n=319), other sites 1.00–1.01 (n=229); Ur III legends at the same n: 0.66, 0.81–0.83, 0.84–0.88 (the statistic drifts toward 1 at small n, which is why matching matters; the gap is 0.31 at MD, 0.20 at Harappa, 0.13–0.16 at the small sites, all > 10 sd). seq_strong / seq_all shift Indus by ≤ 0.015. Indus seals per middle: MD 1.09, Harappa 1.06, other 1.04 (Ur III 2.7–3.1). Indus sealings (112 with a middle): 12.5% match a known seal; 28% share a middle with another sealing, almost all two Lothal/Kalibangan cases (705-500-741-1-55-220 ×11, 91-56-2-417-890-892 ×8), the S149 twins | **Holds at every site and sign level; the whole 'not names' gap is transcription- and merge-robust, and largest where n is largest.** Sealings behave like Ur III impressions (one seal re-used), seals do not behave like Ur III seals. Caveats: Harappa has only 319 seals with a middle (its corpus is tablets), and 76% of the material is MD+Harappa as always; the Ur III comparison population is Umma/Girsu administrators, a narrower social set than seal owners in general. |
| S-DARK-14.1 | Workshop signatures (a, d): seals clustered within each city by PHYSICAL features only (boss code, material, colour, shape, cross-section, log h/v/th; Gower distance, Ward, k = 4/6/8; 2,248 of 2,291 seals joined to the canonical corpus). Do clusters differ in sign vocabulary (G over the 40 commonest signs), closer (MI with last sign), text length (Kruskal-Wallis) and find area (MI) more than random clusters of the same sizes? Null: labels permuted within site x object type (1,000x), and again with the coarse emblem class also held fixed (/E). seq_raw/strong/all; 63 arrows, Holm (`tools/dark_loop14_c1.py`) | **Vocabulary: null everywhere** (MD P 0.18-0.84, Harappa 0.10-0.45, other sites 0.17-0.56, all k, all levels, with or without emblem held fixed). **Closer: null** (best MD k=4 P 0.003 raw, 0.011 /E, Holm 0.17; Harappa and others P > 0.12). **Length: MD k=8 only** (z +7.7, P 0.001, Holm 0.063 = permutation floor; k=6 P 0.04, k=4 P 0.07; Harappa P 0.97, others P 0.29): PBSG-boss seals carry longer texts than PN-boss seals at MD; cycle 4 direct test fails to replicate at Harappa (reversed) and elsewhere. **Find area: MD and Harappa P 0.001, all k, other sites P > 0.95**, but at MD it is an excavator effect (Marshall's DK- seals lack h for 88% and DKG seals lack colour for 97%, so the clusters follow recording practice, which follows area), and at Harappa it is emblem-less cuboid-convex seals concentrating on Mound AB | **No scribal schools: seals that look alike physically within a city do not share a sign repertoire, a closer or a length beyond what emblem and seal type already give. Central standard holds.** The one text-independent positive (physical clusters map onto areas) is explained by recording regime at MD and by seal type by mound at Harappa, not by workshops. Rests on MD + Harappa (80% of joined seals); the pooled other sites are too few per city to cluster separately |
| S-DARK-14.3 | Workshop signatures (c): do a workshop's clients share text? Style letters also tested for their own vocabulary, closer, length and find area (null: style permuted within site x type x emblem, so only the carving style moves). 1,000x; 30 arrows, Holm (`tools/dark_loop14_c3.py`) | **All null after correction.** Physical clusters: within-minus-across Jaccard MD +0.002 (P 0.23), Harappa +0.007 (P 0.03 raw, 0.08 with emblem fixed, Holm 0.84), others P 0.55; identical middles P 0.37-0.79. Style letters: Jaccard P 0.51/0.90/0.87, identical middles P 0.11/0.05/0.48 (Holm 1.0); vocabulary MD P 0.04 raw (Holm 1.0), Harappa 0.73, others 0.40; closer P 0.75/0.61/0.39; length P 0.09/0.50/0.54; style x find area P 0.16/0.36/0.08. Closer tables by style are the same list in the same order everywhere (jar W740 first, then W520, W390, W90) | **Clients of one carving style, or of one physical seal type, do not share more text than clients of another: no house styles in the writing.** Taken with S-DARK-14.1 and 14.2, the carving workshop and the text are independent layers: the cutter supplies the stone, boss and animal; the text comes from a common standard (fits S329, S352) |
| S-DARK-14.4 | Follow-up of the one surviving cycle-1 arrow: boss TYPE (PB plain boss, PBSG boss with groove, PN perforated no boss, P, NN, other PB variants) vs text length, vocabulary (G, 40 commonest signs) and closer (MI), complete seals; null: boss labels permuted within site x object type x coarse emblem class, 2,000x; three levels; replication MD -> Harappa -> other sites (`loop14_cycle4.txt`) | MD: length KW 17.1 vs 8.0 (z +2.3-2.5, P 0.02-0.03 at all levels; PBSG 5.7 and P 5.7 signs vs PB 4.8, PN 4.5, NN 3.7), closer MI P 0.02, vocabulary P 0.08. **Harappa: reversed and null** (PBSG 4.6 vs PB 4.8, P 0.32; vocabulary P 0.78; closer P 0.27). **Other sites: null** (P 0.80, 0.46, 0.16). PBSG share is 9-14% in every MD area, so it is not a recording artefact; the effect is simply MD-only and weak (9 arrows, none Holm-safe) | **Boss type does not predict the text.** The MD hint (grooved-boss seals carry a sign more) fails held-out replication and the direction flips at Harappa. Treat as noise unless new material shows it again |
| S-DARK-20.1 | **Allograph clock (a): does the share of each variant form drift with depth or period within Mohenjo-daro and within Harappa, in the same direction?** Variant sets = the 23 S268 merge classes (strong + probable + doubtful, seq_raw forms) + 5 S-DARK-11.3 candidate pairs; token level, each non-canonical form vs the rest of its set; sets with >= 30 dated tokens and >= 5 of each form in the city; Spearman(form indicator, time residualised on site x area x object type); permutation 2,000x within the same strata; BH per city x axis; replication = q < 0.1 same sign in the other city; whole-machine control = time labels shuffled within strata, 3 seeds (`tools/dark_loop20.py 1`, `1 control seed=N`) | 25 arrows fired (MD depth 9, MD period 7, HP depth 5, HP period 4; only 390/405/406, 705/706, 803/806/838, 861/817, 156/151, 384/388, 390/407 have enough tokens). Survivors q < 0.1: MD depth **W706 later** (rho +0.19, p 0.004, q 0.036; HP +0.12, p 0.35); HP depth **W407 later** (rho +0.54, n 49, 16 form tokens, p 0.0005, q 0.002; MD +0.04, p 0.95); MD period 0, HP period 0. **Both-city same-sign survivors: 0.** Sign agreement 4 of 5 (depth, P 0.19), 1 of 4 (period). Controls: 7 of 66 control arrows p < 0.05 (real: 2 of 25), 0 both-city | **Null for a shared drift.** No variant form moves with depth or period in both cities. Two single-city hits, each unreplicated at the other city and each inside what the shuffled-label control produces; the W407 one rests on 16 tokens of an unmerged candidate pair at Harappa (within-area, within-type). Variant choice is a city habit (S-DARK-14.2), not a date marker |
| S-DARK-20.2 | **Allograph clock (b): build it anyway and test it out of sample.** Per text one feature per set (+1 variant form present, -1 canonical only, 0 absent; 28 sets), ridge (lambda 1) to stratum-residualised time; 5-fold CV within each city and train-one-city/test-the-other, depth and period; statistic = held-out Spearman(prediction, residual time); control = shuffled-variant clock (features permuted within stratum, 500x) (`tools/dark_loop20.py 2`) | Within-city CV: MD depth +0.015 (shuffled clock -0.01 +/- 0.04, P 0.26), MD period -0.02 (P 0.50), HP depth -0.02 (P 0.49), HP period -0.11 (P 0.87), pooled depth -0.01 (P 0.45). Cross-city: MD -> HP depth +0.065 (P 0.018, Holm over 8 = 0.14), HP -> MD depth +0.03 (P 0.16), period +0.01 / -0.03 (P 0.35 / 0.74). The two cross-city weight vectors do not agree (MD-trained: 812 -2.7, 371 +2.6, 154 -2.3; HP-trained: 772 +2.0, 390x407 +1.6, 527 -1.4) | **The clock has no out-of-sample power.** It predicts held-out depth or period no better than a clock built from shuffled variants in either city, and the one cross-city arrow fails correction and is not mirrored in the reverse direction. There is no letterform clock in the Wells variant structure |
| S-DARK-20.3 | **Allograph clock (c): the pooled clock applied to 860 UNDATED texts (MD 278, HP 582) with at least one variant-set token; arrows = W390 count (clock refitted without the 390 and 390/407 sets), text length (full and per-token clock), jar-final, arrow-final, other closer, tablet text reuse; permutation 2,000x of the score within site x area x object type; dated texts as sanity (`tools/dark_loop20.py 3`)** | Undated: arrow-final rho +0.23 (p 0.0005), length +0.10 / +0.12 (p 0.03 / 0.02), W390 count +0.04 (p 0.12), jar-final +0.04 (p 0.37), other closer -0.03, tablet reuse +0.05 (p 0.34). Dated: jar-final +0.08 (p 0.006), arrow-final +0.09 (p 0.002), length +0.03 ns. Decomposition: the arrow-final association is carried by the fish set W240 (weight -0.56, canonical-only texts score high; 29% of W240 texts end in W520 = the S296 fish + arrow formula, vs 6.5% overall) and W390 presence (24% arrow-final) | **Nothing moves in time along the clock, because the clock is not time.** With zero out-of-sample power (20.2) its score is a formula detector (fish -> arrow), and that is what the only 'trend' is. W390 quota, text length, closer mix and tablet reuse remain null in time, as in S-DARK-9 |

| S-DARK-19.2 | Sequence or set, part 2. (b) For every sign multiset (>= 3 distinct signs) on >= 2 objects, the share of extra copies that bring a new order, split by object type and site; nulls: shuffle every text (free set) and shuffle within frame slots; pots vs seals by type-label permutation within site. (c) Every unordered sign pair co-occurring in >= 5 distinct texts: A-before-B vs B-before-A at any distance; fixed = one-sided binomial with BH(0.05); null counts from whole-text and within-slot shuffles; the fixed pairs assembled into a partial order (cycle check, longest chain); each fixed edge classed by the per-text frame slots (frame-explained / same slot / against); held-out: fixed pairs fitted on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa, direction tested on the other sites. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (tools/dark_loop19.py 2) | (b) 186-200 multiset groups, 717-760 objects: new-order share 0.012-0.013 vs 0.79 if the text were a set and 0.35-0.38 if order were free inside the frame slots; tablets 0.011, seals 0.012-0.018, Mohenjo-daro 0.000, Harappa 0.017. Pots: 0 testable groups (80 complete direction-recorded pots), P = 0.70. (c) 783-794 pairs tested; BH-fixed 395-399 (null 0-1 for a set; 261-273 with the frame kept), free 63-68, ambiguous 208-220. Fixed edges: 74% follow the frame slot order, 26% (102-114) order two signs INSIDE one slot, 0 go against the frame. Same-slot fixed pairs 84-98 vs a frame-only null max of 27-31. 40-44 of the same-slot edges are documented rules (fish chain, arrow phrase, frozen titles, numeral before item); 62-70 are new, mostly name-name: leaf-tree 803 first in the middle (27:4 before 233, 26:6 before 220, 20:4 before 390, 16:4 before 32), marked jar 741 first (13:0 before 803, 14:2 before 220), 415 -> 220 39:0, 798 -> 415 14:2, fish -> 590 (16:3, 13:3), 550 -> 60 21:5, 125 -> 2 18:5. Partial order: 110-117 signs, one cycle (741/820, 6 texts), longest chain 16-19 signs: 320 -> 920 -> 60 -> 741 -> 2 -> 803 -> 32 -> 350 -> 798 -> 415 -> 220 -> 233 -> 705 -> 255 -> 435 -> 690 -> 740 -> 400. The free residue (4% of pair instances) sits in the numerals (1/31/32/2), plain fish vs modified fish, and W2's two uses. The pot prediction is untestable here (no multiset of >= 3 signs recurs on direction-recorded pots). Caveat: 43-46% of pair instances are too rare to classify, and 'fixed' is an upper bound on the pairs seen (S309). |
| S-DARK-23.1 | **Loop 23, cycle 1: if a lone numeral scratched on a pot is a capacity or lot label it should track the pot's size within a site. Wall thickness (a vessel-size proxy) exists for 52 sherds, 48 of them Kalibangan. Lone numeral vs thickness: n = 1. Numeral-first text vs thickness: n = 4, rho +0.33, p = 0.84. Against sherd H: pooled n = 9, rho +0.41, p = 0.035, but this is 1 hit in 18 arrows (Bonferroni 0.63) and sherd H is the break, not the pot. Jar alone, tree alone, fish alone: 0-2 pots with a dimension each, all p > 0.18. The 2,000x permutation returns nothing at any merge level. What the capacity reading needs is the excavators' vessel data: Harappa HARP pot forms behind the H98/H99 numbers (Meadow and Kenoyer figure references in `excavation-idno`) and the Kalibangan sherd report; without them this family stays grade C with no evidence either way |

All Harappa stamped pots with a period are Period 3. **(b2) W440 impressions are one size:** 14-17 mm, sd 1.1 mm over 7 measurements, vs sd 5.7 for all stamped pots; p = 0.006 (0.04 after the 7-arrow correction); W13 sd 0.6 (n = 3), W416 sd 3.0. **(b3) Pre-firing marks are opener signs:** 8 of 19 carry W820/W861/W920 (820 x5, 861 x2, 920 x1; Surkotada x2, Shikarpur x3, Kanmer, Bala-kot, Mohenjo-daro) vs 5% of graffiti, difference +0.37, p = 0.008 (0.056 corrected); numeral share -0.27 (p = 0.53), jar family +0.01 (p = 1.0). Same at all merge levels | **The stamped pots are one lot, not W440 alone**: the whole Harappa stamped class is a Trench 43, Period 3 deposit (CISI 3.1: pointed-base goblets), so 'one product line' is true of the class and cannot be tested for the sign. The W440 impressions are consistent with a single die (or a standard 15 mm seal), which fits S355's workshop-stamp gloss but does not sharpen it. New, grade C: **potters' pre-firing marks use the opener sign family** (the leaf/diamond/wheel openers), not numerals: a potter's mark is the seal-text opener standing alone, at six small sites. Would support: new pre-firing marks at Harappa or Kalibangan being 820/861/920. Would kill: ten new pre-firing marks with no opener. Form/size tests need the excavators' vessel typology, which inscriptions.csv does not hold |

One-sign labels at Harappa are 70% numerals (tall 2 x36, tall 3 x17, W440 x12, W2 x11), at Kalibangan 43%, elsewhere 28% (jar 7%). The one-sign numeral labels are a Harappa Period 3 sherd phenomenon (HARP trenches 39N, 43, 54, 11), the multi-sign texts are the older museum vessels: a difference in how the two collections were gathered, not evidence of two pot forms. Numerals on pots stand alone or next to W999/W700/W480, never before a tree or a fish, so they are not the seal-type 'n trees' counts |

Held-out: the mode-2 shape is Harappa (36/17/5); Kalibangan 1/1/0, rest 2/1/3 (Nausharo, Mohenjo-daro), too few to replicate. S-DARK-12.3(e) counted 28 lone numerals (5/7/10/6) from the canonical file, which was built from an earlier inscriptions.csv (5,369 vs 5,680 rows) and lacks about 92 later-added pots, mostly Harappa HARP sherds (267 vs 347 Harappa pots; 12 vs 38 lone W32). Numeral + item pairs on pots: tall before W700 (4), W999 (6), W480 (2); short before W480 (3), W370 (2); none before a tree, arrow or fish. Lone W1/W2 (the 'markers' of S234 and S-DARK-15.3) stand alone on 15 Harappa pots. The two media use the same tall series with different value profiles, so the pot numeral is not a copy of the voucher. A capacity reading would want value to scale with vessel size (untestable, S-DARK-23.1); a lot or batch reading would want a few repeated values, which is what is seen (2 and 3 are 58 of 77 tall marks). Grade C gloss for the lone tall numeral on a pot: batch or lot number, not capacity. The canonical corpus should be rebuilt from the current inscriptions.csv (it is 311 rows short, 92 of them pots) before any further pot count is quoted from it |
| S-DARK-19.1 | Sequence or set, part 1: anagram census. 3,155 complete direction-recorded objects (>= 2 signs) grouped by sign multiset; among multisets on >= 2 objects, how many recur in more than one order, and which slot pairs swap. Held-out sites: 0 of 15-20 groups, 0 of 145-152 pairs (< 3/150) vs 0.55 / 0.93-0.95. 13 of 15 anagram order-pairs are single adjacent transpositions (235-240 vs 240-235 fish, 740-760 vs 760-740 before the suffix, 220-700 vs 700-220); the others are a full reversal (413-892-824 x11 vs 824-892-413 x1) and a suffix moved to the front, i.e. recording errors. The only loose order is the Harappa two-sign voucher N + W700 (15% reversed, more on moulds), read as a label whose count and unit can stand on either side, or as mirrored moulds; it needs a photo check of H-tablets reading 700-33.
