| S-DARK-16.4 | Usage intensity (d): does the CISI condition grade (Poor/Fair/Good/Fine) or preservation field behave like wear, i.e. do office (jar-final) or long texts show more wear than quantity (numeral+tree) texts? 2125 seals; text class or text length permuted within site x material x seal type (3000x, 10 arrows); control = only seals recorded as fully preserved with complete text (1235); seq_raw/strong/all | Reversed and then null. Longer texts are in BETTER condition (r +0.24, p < 0.001) and less damaged (r -0.18); stock seals are the least damaged (16% vs 35%, p < 0.001); the short 'other' class is the worst (46% damaged). Among fully preserved seals every class effect vanishes (office +0.005 p 0.40; stock -0.055 p 0.23) and the length effect shrinks to r +0.12 (p 0.001; same sign at Mohenjo-daro +0.10 and Harappa +0.12). Identical at all merge levels | **Null; the field is not wear.** Damage shortens the readable text and strips the class markers, so condition predicts text class, not the other way round. The residual 'long texts are better preserved' most likely reflects selection (fine, well-cut seals get long texts and survive better) rather than use. The condition field cannot serve as a usage proxy; drop it from the usage family |
| S-DARK-22.1 | **Fixed-width fields (a): is each sign anchored at a fixed offset from the START or from the END?** For every sign with >= 30 tokens (78 signs), entropy of its position counted from the start vs from the end over all 3,155 complete direction-recorded texts; d = H_end - H_start; null = each text shuffled internally (300 reps; keeps length, sign multiset, site, object type), Bonferroni over 2 x 78; by frame slot (S310 parser), seals vs tablets, held-out sites (not MD/HP); controls 6,675 Ur III seal legends (syllables) and 2,643 Linear B tablet lines (words, ideograms, numbers); seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop22.py 1`) | 43 signs tighter from the end, 34 from the start (P = 0.18); 20 end-anchored and 10 start-anchored after Bonferroni. Token-weighted entropy gain over the null: 0.35 bits from the start, 0.59 from the end (ratio 1.7; seals 1.7, tablets 1.6, held-out sites 1.3). By slot: closers 8/8 end-tighter (mean d -1.2), suffix 2/2 (-1.6), titles 10/11 (-0.75), openers 0/4 (+1.4), markers 0/2 (+1.3), NAME residue 18/42 (+0.04, no preference). Signs under 1 bit of positional entropy from one edge: W752 (0.41), W400/M176 (0.60), W527/M254 (0.61), W151/M12 (0.84) from the end, W60/M123 (0.96) from the start, all known edge grammar. Controls: Ur III ratio 0.5 (start-anchored names, end-anchored ARAD2-zu and titles); Linear B ratio 4.8 (NUM and ideograms counted from the end, entry-head words from the start). Same at all three merge levels. | **Null for a one-edged record format; the two-edged frame confirmed as positional entropy.** Each closed class is anchored at its own edge (openers/connectives at the start, closers/suffix/titles at the end) and the name residue floats. Indus end-anchoring (1.7) is weaker than a real tabular record (Linear B 4.8) and the opposite of a name-first legend (Ur III 0.5). Nothing beyond the two edge fields is at a fixed offset. |
| S-DARK-22.2 | **Fixed-width fields (b): does an 'offset grammar' (P(sign | offset from the end, text length)) beat the order-2 Markov chain and the slot grammar at predicting held-out signs?** Seven models with Witten-Bell backoff, all given L, scored per sign token without an end token: Markov-1/2, Markov-2 + remaining length, offset-from-end, offset-from-start, slot-class bigram (S310 slots), slot-class + end offset. Fit MD + Harappa (2,751 texts), test the other sites (404 texts); 5-fold on all; paired bootstrap over held-out texts; seals and tablets separately; controls Ur III legends and Linear B lines with a crude first/mid/last class; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop22.py 2`) | Held-out nats/token, Indus: Markov-1 4.51, Markov-2 4.65, slot-class 4.78, offset-from-end 5.10, offset-from-start 5.07. Offset-end minus Markov-2 +0.45 [+0.35, +0.55]; minus slot grammar +0.33 [+0.26, +0.40]; end vs start offset tie (+0.03 [-0.04, +0.10]). Adding the remaining length to the chain or the slot model makes both worse (+0.10 to +0.11). Seals: same (+0.37 over the chain). Tablets: same in 5-fold (3.67 vs 2.65). Controls: offset-end loses to the chain by +1.79 in Ur III and +0.86 in Linear B (a real field record, but with variable-width fields). Relative cost offset/chain: Indus 1.10, Linear B 1.18, Ur III 1.71. Per sign: the offset model wins only for a few edge signs (W151/M12, W820, W920, W590) and loses most for the connectives W2/W60 and the suffix W90, which are predicted by their left neighbour. Identical at all three merge levels. | **Null: an end-anchored (or start-anchored) offset model beats neither the chain nor the slot grammar, in any subset.** The sign is predicted by its neighbour, not by its distance from an edge. Indus is the most positional of the three corpora (relative cost 1.10 vs 1.18 and 1.71), because its closed-class edge signs are frequent, but it does not cross into fixed-offset territory even relative to Linear B. The test's power is limited: no known administrative format of the period has token-level fixed widths, so the offset model can only win for a strictly fixed-width code; Indus is not one. |
| S-DARK-16.3 | Usage intensity (c): 'dead seals'. Seal lines (>= 2 signs, 2028) whose text never appears on a sealing or tablet (1849, 91%) vs those with attested use (179): size, material, condition, emblem, text class; used label permuted within site x seal type (1000x, 16 arrows) and again within site x type x text length (15 arrows; Bonferroni over 31 = 0.0016); held-out by Mohenjo-daro / Harappa / other; seq_raw/strong/all | Used share is flat across cities (8-12%). Naively, used seals are shorter-texted (-2.2 signs), smaller (-215 mm2), in worse condition (-0.26) and poorer in numerals and fish (all p 0.001) - but every physical difference vanishes when text length is held (area p 0.05, condition p 0.34, emblem p 0.45, material p 0.18). What remains: used seals end in the jar +0.12 vs -0.07 expected (p 0.001, same at seq_strong; Mohenjo-daro +0.16, other sites +0.13, Harappa 0.00) and in the arrow (p 0.003), and the raw deficit of openers among used seals is entirely a length effect (length-matched they have slightly MORE openers, p 0.002) | **Mostly null; one modest survivor.** Dead and used seals are the same objects physically - no sign that unused seals were a different class of thing (votive, blank-stock, foreign). The text does matter a little: closer-bearing (jar/arrow) short texts are the ones that get copied onto tablets, which restates S344 (tablets reproduce closer phrases) rather than measuring seal wear. 'Use' here is 90% tablet copies, so this is a scribal-copying result, not a sealing result; 91% dead is an upper bound on non-use given S309 sampling |
| S-DARK-21.1 | **True replication on material Wells never transcribed, part 1: which IM77 texts are absent from Wells/ICIT, and the frame / fish order / minimum-lot rule on them.** Matching extends `tools/audit_overlap.py`: one text = all lines of a text_no+side; bridge W→M; site-aware (big-5 sites must agree; 'Other'/'West Asian' match any other site); unbridged W signs are wildcards, capped at 2 per text with ≥ 3 concrete agreeing positions (0 wildcards for texts ≤ 3 signs); NEAR = edit distance 1 or exact reverse under the same caps. Rule tuned on a shuffle control (texts ≥ 4 signs shuffled within themselves): unlimited wildcards give 9.7% false exact matches, the chosen rule 0.3% (near 1.1%). IM77-only = fully bridgeable complete text with no exact or near match; texts with an unbridged M sign and no match are 'indeterminate'. Tests on the IM77-only set, in M numbers, each with its own control on that set: (1) opener M267/391/293/150 first, connective M99/100/123 never initial, closer M342/211/12/15/254/60/328 last (within-text shuffle, 200×), closer exclusivity (sign tokens permuted across texts, 200×); (2) fish order M65→M67→M72→M70 (binomial); (3) numeral before a counted good (trees M161–169, arrow M211, bracket M287, M407, M296, M216), null = numeral values permuted among numeral slots (2,000×). Same tests on a length-matched OVERLAP sample (3 per new text) and on all OVERLAP texts (`data/derived/dark/loop21_engine.py 1`) | **Overlap: of 2,742 complete IM77 texts, 1,685 (61.5%) match a Wells text exactly, 157 nearly, 690 are indeterminate (unbridged sign), and only 210 (7.7%) are certainly absent from Wells** (MD 91, Harappa 67, Lothal 27, Kalibangan 12, Chanhu-daro 7, other 3, West Asia 3; 117 seals, 51 sealings, 16 tablets, 15 pots; mean 3.6 signs, 123 of them 2–3 signs). The audit's 69% was depressed by bridge gaps: with wildcards the audit-style figure is 82%. Spot checks confirm the new texts are absent from the merged corpus at any site (e.g. M48-342-130 = W176-740-435, M54-54-342). **(1) Frame REPLICATED on the 210: opener first 34/209 (shuffled 12.7, 95% ≤ 19); connective initial 4/46 tokens (shuffled 10.0); opener+connective 24 (shuffled 1.8, max 5); closer last 102/209 = 49% (shuffled 39.8, max 49); opener before closer 25/28; texts with two closer-class signs 3 vs 17.0 permuted (P = 0.005), jar with another closer 2 vs 13.6 (P = 0.005); per-sign finality M12 10/11, M15 12/13, M211 14/16, M254 4/4, M60 3/3, M342 57/73; M328 only 2/10 (it is the Harappa tablet closer and the new set has 16 tablets).** Length-matched overlap sample: opener 16%, closer 77%, exclusivity 3 vs 72.6; new set is weaker on closers (49% vs 77%) because 30 of its texts are pots, rods, implements and 'miscellaneous'. **(2) Fish order UNDERPOWERED: 4 predicted : 1 reverse (P = 0.38); 5 texts carry two modified fish** (overlap 63:10). **(3) Minimum lot NOT replicated at n = 19: 3 of 19 counted goods take a short single stroke (M97/98) directly before them** (2929:3 pot 87-343-97-169; 3044:0 seal 391-99-98-296-342; 1471:2 misc 98-287) **plus one tall-1 + arrow (2516:0); counts 3–8 = 14/19; depletion vs the permutation null only marginal (3 vs 6.5, P = 0.056)**, whereas the overlap set gives 5/184 short-2 (all the W2 connective after an opener, read the same by Wells) and 0 short-1 (P = 0.0005). Glyphs verified from the IM77 sign drawings: M97/M98 are short single strokes, M86 tall. | **Two of three parts hold on material Wells never read; the minimum-lot rule does not.** The slot frame (openers, the never-initial connective, the closer paradigm and its mutual exclusivity) is confirmed on 210 texts with no Wells counterpart, which is the first replication that is not a re-reading of the same objects. The fish-qualifier order cannot be tested at this n. The minimum-lot rule meets its own frozen kill rate (prereg 5, P5.2: > 5% of new counted goods with a short 1/2; observed 16%) though on 19 goods rather than the 200 the preregistration assumed; two of the three cases are not seals. Demote S-DARK-15.3 from 'writing rule' to 'strong tendency on seals' until Rakhigarhi/Binjor material is read. Caveats: 'absent from Wells' can also mean Wells read the same object with ≥ 2 signs the bridge does not cover (690 texts are indeterminate for this reason); the IM77-only set is short-text-heavy and 14% of it is non-seal; the M inventory splits strokes by placement (M97 vs M98) where Wells does not. |
| S-DARK-22.3 | **Fixed-width fields (c, d): are the slots in front of the closer of fixed width, and is an empty field skipped or filled with a placeholder?** (c) Width of every S310 slot (OPENER, MARKER, NAME, COUNT, TITLE, SUFFIX) in the 2,095 closer-bearing texts; texts counted by width signature; null = slot-class bigram generative model (200 corpora, same lengths, re-parsed); seals, tablets, held-out sites. (d) For each field and each sign not of that field (374 tests), odds of the sign in texts where the field is EMPTY vs FILLED; null = permute the empty label within (site, type, length) strata, 200 reps, max-statistic per field, Bonferroni over 6 fields; hits re-counted at held-out sites; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop22.py 3`) | (c) TITLE width 0/1/2+: 0.36/0.54/0.10 (null 0.81/0.18/0.02); COUNT 0/1/2: 0.80/0.07/0.11; NAME 0-10 signs with a smooth fall-off 0.32, 0.26, 0.19, 0.11, 0.07, 0.03; 198 distinct signatures vs null 168 [158, 180] (more, not fewer); commonest signature TITLE + CLOSER alone 16% (26% of tablets). The only excess over the class null is 'exactly one title sign', which is the closer-specific left partner (S229, S289). (d) Fit-set hits: W320, W388 when TITLE is empty (23:1, 23:5; not in seq_raw), W34 when NAME or COUNT is empty (106:2, 107:1), W700 when SUFFIX is empty (357:1); none for OPENER or MARKER. Held-out: W320 0:0, W388 4:1, W34 0:0, W700 3:0, none replicates. W34 and W700 are the Harappa voucher '3 + W700' (the parser calls 34 a title, so NAME/COUNT are empty by construction; the voucher closer excludes the tablet suffix, S216-S218); W320/W388 are name signs of title-less Mohenjo-daro seals. Same at all merge levels except the W320/W388 pair (seq_strong/all only). | **Null for fixed widths and null for placeholders.** Every field except the closer is optional and variable: the name runs 0-10 signs, the title 0-2+, the count 0 or 2. An empty field shortens the text; no sign stands in for a missing opener, marker, title, count or suffix. The one code-design signature a record format would leave (a filler sign) is absent at every merge level and at the held-out sites. |
| S-DARK-24.1 | Transcriber disagreement as data. Wells (seq_raw + 000 lost tokens) and Mahadevan IM77 readings aligned object by object within site by bridge-aware edit distance (sub 0 if M in the W sign's correspondence set, else 1; unknown 0.5; lost 0.25); accepted if cost <= 1 + 0.15 L, object type compatible, field symbol not in conflict, unique best. Correspondence = bridge + learned dominant partners for 57 unbridged Wells signs + systematic mappings. Control: same procedure against wrong-site Wells objects. Validation: Mahadevan's own doubtful marks (`tools/dark24_align.py`, `dark24_analyze.py`) | **1,363 IM77 texts matched (823 identical readings, 540 with differences) vs 119 at wrong sites (4.3%); field-symbol agreement 90% at cost 0, ~50% at cost >= 1.75, so only one-difference pairs are clean.** 7,390 aligned positions: substitutions 109 / 6,527 sign-to-sign positions = 1.7% (1.5% deduplicated); Mahadevan reads a sign where Wells marks lost 186, the reverse 34; one-sided extra sign 38 (M) / 9 (W); doubling-segmentation 49 (W617 = M245 245, W519 = M153 153, W36 = M89 89). Doubtful marks: 1.1% at agreements, 7.3% at substitutions, 26% at Wells-lost positions (control 1.6%). Last sign 0.4% substitutions vs first 3.1%, middle 1.7%. **Two systematic bridge errors found: W798 = M53 (51/51 tokens; bridge said M216 = W550) and W806 = M389 (31/34; bridge said M387 = W803).** After removing them, residual confused pairs are shape-similar (glyph 0.73 vs null 0.56, P = 0.001), recur above chance (12 vs 7, P = 0.011) and are mostly jar/marked-jar (740/741), fish/marked-fish (220/233/231/235), 803/806, 550/798, 900/920, 31/32. Mahadevan SPLITS 384 (M326) / 388 (M327), 803 (M387) / 806 (M389), 390 (M162) / 405-406-407 (M169), 151 (M12) / 156 (M15); LUMPS 154/156/158 = M15, 615/617 = M245, 510/511 = M253, 904/927 = M294 | **The two transcriptions agree on 98.3% of sign-to-sign positions; the disagreement load sits in lost-vs-read positions (condition) and in a small set of shape-similar sign pairs, not in random noise.** Corrections: (1) the bridge's W798 -> M216 and W806 -> M387 are wrong (fix in bridge_extended.json: W798 = M53, W806 = M389); (2) the S268 'strong' merge 803/806 loses its Mahadevan support (he splits them on 129 tokens) and 390/405 likewise (M162 vs M169); (3) S-DARK-11.3's W384 = W388 is contradicted by Mahadevan (M326 vs M327). W154/156/158 = M15 and W615/617 = M245 are new lump candidates for the interchangeability test (cycle 2). Caveat: 76% of pairs are Mohenjo-daro; Harappa's rate is one duplicated tablet decision. |
| S-DARK-21.2 | **True replication part 2, same 210 IM77-only texts (S-DARK-21.1): (4) name calibration, (5) quantity-seal type, (6) nesting, plus prereg-4 P2 reuse.** (4) middles = text minus suffix M176/M1, minus one closer sign, minus opener and connective (loop18 rule in M numbers); share unique vs a bigram model trained on the same middles, size-matched 50 draws; Ur III seal legends and CDLI physical seal objects (loop18 cache) resampled to the same n; homonymy = share of objects whose middle recurs. (5) complete texts opener·connective·N(3–8)·tree and the same run inside longer texts, vs within-text shuffle 200×. (6) distinct 3–5-sign texts occurring whole and contiguous inside a longer text, three pools: new shorts in new hosts; new shorts in all 1,201 complete IM77 hosts; known (overlap) shorts in new hosts; within-text shuffle 50×. P2: share of new texts containing a ≥ 3-sign run attested in the 1,685 overlap texts, vs shuffle 100× (`loop21_engine.py 2`) | **(4) UNDERPOWERED: new seals n = 94 middles, Indus ratio 1.08 (sd 0.05) vs Ur III 1.02 / 1.00 at the same n; all objects n = 168: 1.04 vs 0.91 / 0.92.** At n < 200 both populations sit near 1 (the drift S-DARK-18.3 warned of), so the test cannot separate them; the length-matched overlap sample (n = 261) already shows the gap re-opening (0.99 vs 0.84) and the full overlap (n = 785) gives 0.93 vs 0.70. Homonymy on the new seals: 9.6% share a middle (1.07 objects per middle; M182-149 ×5, i.e. a Lothal-type title, not a name) vs Ur III 14–21% at the same n. **(5) One case, consistent: Kalibangan sealing 8106:1 = M267-99-104-169 ('opener · of · 4 · tree'), shuffle expectation 0.04**; no run inside a longer new text. **(6) REPLICATED: new shorts inside new longer texts 7/135 = 5.2% vs 0.8% shuffled (max 2.2%), 6.5×; new shorts inside any IM77 text 24/135 = 17.8% vs 3.2%, 5.5×; known shorts inside new long texts 17/555 = 3.1% vs 0.5%, 5.9×** (prereg-4 P1 threshold 2×; S347 gave 4×, S349 4.1×). Examples: M48-342-176 inside 150-123-48-342-176; 325-347-342 inside 86-325-347-342; 102-59-342 inside 102-59-342-12. **P2 REPLICATED: 64/210 = 30.5% of new texts contain a ≥ 3-sign run known from the overlap set vs 5.9% shuffled (max 10.5%), 5.2×** (length-matched known texts 46%, 6.4×); the reused runs are the arrow phrase 336-89-211 / 72-336-89, opener phrases 267-99-59/98/70, and closer units 325-347-342, 136-193-342, 193-342-176. | **The credential structure (texts built from shared parts that nest) is confirmed on texts Wells never read; the 'not a name stock' claim and the quantity-seal type are not testable at this n.** Nesting and reuse are the two prereg-4 predictions and both pass at 5–6× on 210 texts with no Wells counterpart, so they are now replicated in the strict sense (new material, not a second transcription of the same objects). The name calibration needs several hundred new middles; the quantity-seal type needs new 4-sign seals. Caveats: the new set is short-text-heavy, so many 'shorts' are the closer unit alone (X-342, X-342-176) and nesting partly measures the closer paradigm again; hosts for the 17.8% figure include overlap texts; non-seal objects are 44% of the set. |
| S-DARK-21.3 | **Robustness of S-DARK-21.1–21.2: (a) the STRICT IM77-only set (no exact match, near matches kept: 1,057 texts, 993 with ≥ 2 signs; near matches are probably the same objects read with one sign different, so this set is only half new); (b) the conservative set split seals (117) vs non-seals (93), and Mohenjo-daro (91) vs Harappa (67) vs small sites (52). Same tests and controls (`loop21_engine.py 3`)** | **(a) STRICT: opener first 115/993 (shuffled 41), connective initial 6/262 tokens, closer last 48%, two closers in one text 30 vs 84 permuted (P = 0.01); fish order 28 : 4 (P < 10⁻⁴); minimum lot: 8 short-1/2 of 54 numeral+good pairs vs 22.8 permuted (P = 0.001); names: seals n = 479 Indus 0.94 vs Ur III 0.76 (sd 0.04), all objects n = 828 0.80 vs 0.69; nesting 36/406 = 8.9% vs 1.3%, 7.0×; reuse 28% vs 3.9%, 7.1×. (b) Conservative set: opener first seals 19/116 vs shuffled 7.2, non-seals 15/93 vs 5.9 (the non-seal openers are mostly Harappa sealings and tablets); closer last 50% / 47%; exclusivity P = 0.01 / 0.02; Mohenjo-daro connective initial 0/91 texts, Harappa closer last 39% (tablets), small sites opener 27% and closer 61%; nesting 5× / 10× / 4.3× / ∞ / 15× (all on 1–2 cases); minimum lot: the three short-1 cases are all Mohenjo-daro (2 non-seal), Harappa 0/5, small sites 0/4.** | **The frame, exclusivity, nesting and reuse hold in every split of the new material; the fish order and the Indus-vs-Ur III name gap re-appear only when the near-matched texts (half of which are re-readings) are added, so they remain transcription-robust rather than replicated.** The minimum-lot exceptions are confined to Mohenjo-daro and mostly to non-seal objects; on new seals the depletion is P = 0.008 with one exception (3044:0). |

| S-DARK-15.1 | Negative space: patterns the slot grammar's own generative model (order-2 KN chain per object class, fitted on MD+H; 100 synthetic corpora) expects >= 5 times but the corpus never shows: co-occurrence, ordered pair with gap, skip-bigram, ordered triple over the 105-119 commonest signs. Controls: 100 synthetic corpora scored leave-one-out (chance gaps), within-text shuffle + refit, held-out sites with their own model (E_held >= 2), and one copy per distinct text per site x object class (`data/derived/dark/loop15_engine.py`; seq_raw/strong/all) | All texts: 34/27/27 gaps vs synthetic max 3-7 per kind, but the within-text shuffle gives 43-55 (tri 32-38): triple gaps are co-membership, not order. Dedup: 17/12/15 gaps; the W501-jar (29 copies of 501-405-2-240-520), W850-W240 (14 copies), 705-700, 235-33-923 and all other triple gaps vanish. Survivors in all three levels: W2 twice (E 26-28, O 0; held-out E 11, O 1), W2 ... marked jar W741 (E 11-13, O 0; held-out E 3.5, O 0, persistent; chance persistence max 2), fish marks W240/W233 never before W2 with a gap (E 9-10 / 5-6, reverse 29+40 / 24+18), jar never before W2 (E 5-6, reverse 263), W240 twice (E 6). IM77 (1,809 dedup lines, stratified permutation 300x): M99 twice 1 vs 55.7, M99 .. M343 3 vs 16.2, M67 ... M99 0 vs 13.4, M67 twice 0 vs 7.2 (all P(0) < 0.001) | **The negative space of the common signs is one sign's grammar.** Beyond pairwise exclusion (S36/S37/S360) the only gaps that survive deduplication, chance and held-out controls are rules about the marker W2 (M99): written once per text, always before the words it introduces (fish marks, the jar), never with the marked jars of the 920-60-741 phrase. The repetition ban is the general S88 effect at its strongest single sign; the 2 .. 741 exclusion extends S333/S334 from adjacency to the whole text. No new paradigm class and no triple rule. Method lesson: gap searches must deduplicate the moulded tablets or they report ghosts |
| S-DARK-15.2 | Classify the cycle-1 gaps (REPEAT / PARADIGM / ORDER / BOUND / TRIPLE) and test each against a model-free null (sign tokens permuted across texts within site x object class, 200x; `data/derived/dark/loop15_classify.py`); check the surviving rules in IM77 (Mahadevan numbers via the bridge, 1,809 dedup lines, stratified permutation 300x; `loop15_im77.py`) | Dedup, robust under both nulls: REPEAT 5, ORDER 4-5, PARADIGM 0-2, BOUND 0-1, TRIPLE 0 (every triple gap has E_perm < 2.5). Rules: (R1) W2 never twice (E 28 / 39, O 0; held-out 1 of 842; IM77 M99 x2 1 vs 55.7), W240 never twice (E 6 / 9; IM77 0 vs 7.2); (R2) fish marks W240/W233 and the jar never precede W2 with a gap (0 vs 10-15 / 61; reverse 69 / 42 / 263; IM77 M67 ... M99 0 vs 13.4); (R3) W2 never in a text with the marked jars W741/W742 (0 vs 11-12 / 5-8; held-out 0 vs 3.5, persistent in all three levels; IM77 3 vs 16.2); (R4) 233 before 220 only adjacent (E 5, weak). The all-texts PARADIGM gaps {501,740}, {240,850}, {235,700}, {405,482} and all 10-11 TRIPLE gaps are single moulded-tablet series | **Four small rules, all about the frame, no new paradigm, no triple rule.** The negative space beyond pairwise exclusion is the syntax of the marker W2: once per text, before its fish/jar hosts, and in a frame that excludes the 920-60-marked-jar opening (extends S333/S334 to the whole text, held-out and IM77 agree). The same picture holds in seq_raw, seq_strong and seq_all. A code with so little forbidden beyond its frame words is consistent with short formulae whose middles are nearly free (S321) |
| S-DARK-17.2 | **Recovery control for the institution ABC** (bank of 8,000 prior simulations; leave-one-out coverage over 60 pseudo-observed corpora; three planted worlds 'institution', 'names' (every holder one personal middle on all seals, p_share=0.95), 'bureau' (6 authorities, 200 offices, 30,000 holders); `tools/dark_loop17.py control/truth`, `data/derived/dark/loop17_cycle2.txt`) | Rejection posterior calibrated (90% CI coverage 0.87-1.00) but uninformative for A, O, k, p_share, p_batch, p_travel (width 0.9-1.1 of prior); regression-adjusted posterior 2-4x tighter but over-confident (coverage 0.65-0.92). Planted worlds: 'institution' 20/21 recovered (miss p_travel; 69 complete sealings); 'names' 14/21 (A, p_share missed: 0.52 for a planted 0.95); 'bureau' 14/21 (A 1.3 for 6, O 63 for 200, H x k traded: 52,000 vs 33,000 credentials) | **Identifiable: tablet re-issue rate and popularity slope, batch deposit, the display probabilities, and the credential stock H x k to within a factor ~2. NOT identifiable: the number of authorities, holders vs credentials per holder, whether the middle is a personal name, and O beyond a factor ~3.** The ABC on the real corpus (17.3) can only claim what this control recovers |
| S-DARK-24.2 | Per-sign reliability (agreement rate per Wells sign on the 1,234 one-difference-or-identical pairs, binomial vs corpus rate, Bonferroni over 76 signs with >= 15 tokens) and grammatical interchangeability of every disagreement pair, Mahadevan lump and Mahadevan split: mean left/right context cosine in the whole Wells corpus vs 300 frequency-matched partners, calibrated on the S268 strong merges (12/18 pass), plus site/medium split (`tools/dark24_cycle2.py`) | **Agreement 99.1% per token; one unreliable sign, W388 (0.47 of 43, one duplicated Harappa tablet read as M48); frame signs and numerals agree at 99.9-100% (jar 677/678, W2 378/378, numerals 1,031/1,048 with 2 short-vs-tall confusions).** Residual confusions split cleanly: interchangeable = fish marks 231/233 (0.82), marked jars 741/742 (0.59), strokes 31/32 (0.53), markers 1/2/31 (0.4); NOT interchangeable = jar/marked jar 740/741 (0.11, 6 texts: true noise), 550/798, 900/920, 368/520. Mahadevan's lumps 154/156/158 = M15 (ctx 0.56-0.94, P <= 0.017), 405/406/407 = M169, 904/927 pass; his lump 615/617 fails (0.08) because W617 is his doubled '245 245' (segmentation). His splits: 384/388 same slot (0.76, P = 0.003), 390/405 same slot (0.73), 803/806 only weak (0.61, P = 0.10, different right partners), 151/156 moderate (0.41, P = 0.01) | **Transcription noise is confined to shape-similar pairs, and most of those are grammatically interchangeable, i.e. the two readings disagree about which member of a word family or variant class was cut, not about the slot.** The only frequent true-noise swap is jar vs marked jar (740/741), which touches closer counts at the 0.5% level. Merge-list consequences: add W156 to the 154/158 class (B, Mahadevan M15 + context); demote 803/806 from strong to probable (bridge error removed its Mahadevan support, context weak); W384/W388 stays B- (one slot, two shapes by an independent reader); W617 = W615 doubled (segmentation convention, not an allograph); W27 = W28 and 525/526/527 confirmed. Findings that depend on 740 vs 741, on 803 vs 806, or on W388 at Harappa are the transcription-fragile ones. |
| S-DARK-24.3 | Fragility audit: S289 closer paradigm, S296 fish family, S-DARK-15.3 minimum-lot, S321/S-DARK-18 name ratio and S29 opener/suffix exclusion recomputed on MATCHED (1,223 objects read by both transcribers), AGREED (642 identical readings) and DIFF (581 with a difference) vs FULL, each with its own null; disagreement flags per object vs Wells condition / preservation / material / site / object type / IM77 source volume, permutation null stratified by site x object class (`tools/dark24_cycle3.py`) | **All five results hold on identical-reading objects:** closers 151/154/156/520/527/226 pass on MATCHED (6 vs null max 2) and AGREED (4 vs max 2), W617 and W700 fail (617 = Mahadevan's '245 245'; 700 = Harappa tablets, unsampled); fish family cosine 0.32 vs null 0.11 on AGREED (P = 0.0025); numerals read identically on 1,031/1,048 tokens so the minimum-lot rule cannot be a transcription artefact; name ratio 0.98 on AGREED vs size-matched 1.01 (random-string-like everywhere); suffix after opener 6.7% vs 12.3% without on AGREED (P = 0.02; FULL 4.3% vs 17.3%). Condition: any difference Poor 59% vs Fine 33% (P = 0.002), fragments 76% vs complete 38%; lost-vs-read Poor 25% vs Good 3% (P = 0.001), clay sealings 50%; substitutions depend on condition only weakly (P = 0.04-0.05) and more on object class (tablets 18% vs seals 6%, P = 0.045) and site (Harappa 15%, P = 0.04); Mahadevan's doubtful marks track Wells's condition labels (19% Poor vs 0% Fine, P = 0.001) | **None of the audited findings is transcription-fragile; the transcription-fragile elements are specific signs, not results: W617 as a closer (tokenization differs), W700 and the whole tablet half of S29 (Wells-only Harappa material, no second reader), W388 at Harappa, and any count that separates jar 740 from marked jar 741 at the 0.5% level.** The 'read by both' set is 90% seals, so tablet-based results (S29's 28.7% suffix on tablets, S-DARK-18's Harappa tablet repeats) have one transcription only. Condition drives presence/absence disagreement, not identity disagreement: use the Wells 'condition' field as a weight when counting lost signs, and the agreement rate table (loop24_sign_reliability.json) when a result hangs on a fish-mark, marked-jar or leaf-tree distinction. |
| S-DARK-6.3 | Geography as plaintext, the shared-text network: pairs of sites carrying the same text (>= 3 signs, exact match; 52 texts, 74 links on seq_raw; 60/94 on seq_strong) vs great-circle distance, coast-distance difference and river change. Sharing normalised by n_i x n_j; Mantel test with site labels permuted 5,000x; linked vs unlinked pair distance; S324 seal -> sealing flows; power by simulation. `data/derived/dark/loop6_c3_network.py` | **Null. Sharing ~ km rho -0.03 (p 0.73); coast -0.01 (p 0.92); river change -0.07 (p 0.29). Linked pairs 548 km apart vs unlinked 567 km (P 0.40).** Harappa-Lothal (904 km) and Dholavira-Harappa (794 km) share 4 texts each, as many as Chanhu-daro-MD (130 km) and Harappa-Kalibangan (177 km). Without MD + HP 3 links remain. S324 flows: 5 of 7 sealings lie nearer the sea than their seal but all four source sites are MD/Harappa. Power 0.50 for a 300-km decay, 1.00 for a < 400-km network. IM77 (cycle 5) agrees: links follow n_i x n_j. | **Shared texts do not follow the map.** Identical seal texts turn up at any distance, with no river-order or coastward gradient beyond the size of Mohenjo-daro and Harappa. Consistent with S17/S324/S344: the recurring texts are short title-like formulae and copied voucher texts belonging to a region-wide system, not neighbour-to-neighbour contact. Caveat: 17 linked pairs; a gradual decay (half-sharing every 300 km) has only even odds of detection. |
| S-DARK-25.1 | **Phonetic-complement signature without sound values.** For every sign >= 30 tokens, right/left neighbour sets; a SMALL set = <= 4 signs cover 80%; a HUB = a neighbour shared by the small sets of >= 3 hosts (a complement inventory); for each hub the free-use fraction, far-side profile and position attached vs free, host-slot entropy, and mobility (pair offset entropy / uniform). Null: stratified token shuffle (site x type x length, 200x) and, for Indus, a frame-kept (within-slot) shuffle. Same code on Ur III legends, Linear B lines, Proto-Elamite lines, a planted logo-syllabic corpus (12 complements + 3 determinatives) and a planted frame-only corpus. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop25.py 1`) | Indus: 29 / 28 / 28 small sets, **3 / 3 / 3 hubs, all frame signs** (jar W740/M342 right of 12-13 title hosts; W2/M99 right of the openers; W33/M89 left of W700/W845; W400/M176 right of the closers W156/W154/W142); null 0, frame-kept null 0.0 (max 1). Hub free fraction 0.62 but the 'free' jar keeps the attached profile (cos 1.00, position JSD 0.04). Mobility 0.40 / 0.37 / 0.37. The 13 hub-less small sets are lexicalised bigrams (34-700, 550/555-527, 33-700, 413-575, 845-407 ...). Controls: planted logo-syllabic 10 hubs, free 0.86, mobility 0.74 (recovers the 4 frequent complements + all 3 determinatives); planted frame-only 8 hubs, free 0.39, mobility 0.16; Ur III 28 hubs, mobility 0.62 (d, ki, dumu, ARAD2-zu, syllables ba/a/na/ma); Linear B 9 hubs, mobility 0.62 (NUM, ideograms); Proto-Elamite 2 hubs (numerals), mobility 0.00. | **Indus shows the frame signature, not the complement signature.** The machine finds planted complements (shared hubs with 70-90% free use as name syllables, mobile) but in Indus every shared neighbour is a closer, connective, suffix or fixed-term numeral, its 'free' tokens are the same frame use, and host+hub pairs sit at fixed offsets (mobility nearer the planted frame than any language). Upper bound only: a flat or low-rate complement inventory would be missed (the detector sees 4 of 12 planted complements). Transcription-robust at all three levels. |
| S-DARK-27.1 | **Completing the W<->M bridge from aligned object pairs, part 1: candidate table.** Core = the loop 24 aligner (Wells seq_raw + lost tokens vs IM77 text, bridge-aware edit distance, site-aware, type and field-symbol compatible, unique best, cost <= 1 + 0.15 L). Every aligned sign-to-sign position whose (W, M) pair is not in `bridge_extended.json` is a candidate; k = co-aligned distinct text pairs, consistency = k / all aligned positions of the W sign, confidence = Wilson 90% lower bound of that consistency, gate = binomial null (M drawn at its background frequency) P < 0.01 and k >= 2. Control: the same statistic on wrong-site pairs (IM77 text aligned to another site's Wells objects). Two passes: provisional mappings (lb >= 0.5) are added and the corpus re-aligned (`data/derived/dark/loop27_engine.py 1`) | **Pass 1: 1,178 accepted object pairs, 5,660 aligned positions, 391 candidate pairs (223 both sides unbridged, 116 unbridged W to an M another W already covers, 19 bridged W to an uncovered M, 33 disagreements between known signs). Wrong-site null: 106 pairs, 99 candidate rows. At lb >= 0.2 the home set has 113 pairs vs 1 in the null (0.9%); at lb >= 0.4, 109 vs 0.** Pass 2 (105 provisional mappings added): 1,369 accepted pairs (767 identical readings, vs 459 before), 435 candidate rows. Top of the table: W798 = M53 (78/78), W585 = M194 (29/29), W617 = M245 (22/22), W892 = M307 (20/20), W832 = M375 (16/16), W165 = M319 (16/16), W154 = M15 (15/15), W806 = M389 (53/56), W742 = M344 (25/26), W615 = M245 (24/25), W752 = M358, W384 = M326, W243 = M75, W463 = M230, W921 = M301 (all 9-10/9-10), W904 = M294 (18/19). The largest gaps left untouched by alignment: W34 (177 tokens, 2 aligned) and M95 (64 tokens, 2 aligned), both the Harappa tablet opener before W700 = M328 | **The co-alignment statistic separates real correspondences from chance almost perfectly (one null pair above lb 0.2, none above 0.4); 105-113 new mappings are on the table before validation.** The weakness is coverage, not precision: signs confined to short duplicated tablets (W34/M95) never enter the aligner because it rejects texts under 3 signs and tied candidates. |
| S-DARK-27.2 | **Bridge completion part 2: hold-out validation.** The 142 non-empty bridge entries split into 5 folds; each fold (28-29 Wells signs, 20%) removed from the correspondence, corpus re-aligned, and the fold's signs recovered from the candidate table (top M by confidence). Precision = share of recovered top candidates that are in the true bridge set; recall against all held-out signs and against those that aligned at all. Threshold rule fixed in advance: lowest lb with precision >= 0.95 AND zero wrong-site null pairs passing (null from cycle 1) (`loop27_engine.py 2`) | **135 of 142 held-out signs aligned at least once; the top candidate was right for 132 of them. Precision 0.976 at lb >= 0, 0.984 at lb >= 0.3-0.5, 0.968 at 0.8; recall 86% of all held-out entries (90% of those that align) at lb >= 0.35.** The three 'errors' are W798 -> M53 (43/43) and W806 -> M389 (29/32), i.e. the two bridge mistakes S-DARK-24.1 found, now re-found blind with the entries removed, and W711 -> M342 (5/12, lb 0.26), the only genuine confusion (marked jar read as jar), which lb >= 0.3 removes. Seven signs never aligned (W868, W27, W28, W104, W421, W712, W215: all rare). Chosen threshold lb >= 0.35 (k >= 2, P < 0.01): 124 proposed, 122 correct + 2 corrections, 0 null pairs | **The alignment recovers the existing bridge at 98-100% precision and 86% recall, so the same procedure applied to unbridged signs is trustworthy at lb >= 0.35; its two failures are corrections, not errors.** Caveat: the held-out entries are mostly frequent signs; rare-sign proposals (k = 2) are validated only through the null, not through hold-out. |

| S-DARK-28.1 | Graphic-derivation graph of the Wells sign list. Every sign rendered from the lipi font (S164 mapping, 64 px); parts found by connected components, hole filling and empty-column/row splits, normalised to 32 px and matched to every sign by the symmetric dilated overlap of glyph_sim. Types: base + enclosure (box/oval/ring), base + roof or baseline, base + 1-4 detached strokes (with position), base doubled or mirrored, base + another whole sign (ligature). Threshold = 99.5th percentile of a part's score against wrong bases. Controls: 60 planted derivations per type built from random glyphs must return the right base AND type; checks against Wells' own shape-family numbering and against glyph_sim_fine. A subtractive template search for attached (touching) modifications was tried and dropped (coverage of a wrong base reaches 0.9+ on random pairs; misassigns W741, W240). Cruder proxy for attached families: same Wells 20-block + glyph_sim_fine >= 0.6 ('family'). tools/dark_loop28_c1.py, c1fix.py, c1b.py; data/derived/dark/loop28_edges*.json, loop28_edges_sheet.png | Planted recall at threshold 0.863: box 59/60, oval 57/60, roof 58/60, strokes 55/60, doubled 59/60, mirrored 59/60, ligature 47/60; a wrong base passes in 0.5% of comparisons. Real graph: 203 component edges (strokes 105, enclosure 27, doubling 27, roof 14, ligature 15; 20 of them re-pointed to a frequent base such as W741 <- W740), 139 at mid/high confidence; ΔW <= 20 in 55% of edges vs 4% of random pairs; glyph_sim_fine 0.60 vs 0.40 random (50% above the random 95th pct). Only 50 component edges have both signs >= 5 tokens (seq_raw); derived members are mostly hapax/rare (median 2 tokens). Family proxy: 207 more edges, 85 with the derived sign >= 5 tokens | The sign list has a thick graphic-derivation layer: about 30% of non-numeral Wells signs are detectably base + mark, base + frame, base doubled, or two bases fused, found blind with >= 92% planted recall for every type but ligatures (78%). But the derived signs are overwhelmingly rare, so the operator question can be tested on only ~50 pairs (108 with the family proxy); touching modifications such as the fish qualifiers are beyond the component detector and enter only through the proxy. |
| S-DARK-28.4 | Proto-Elamite comparison (same test as S-DARK-28.2 on the Dahl transliteration, other-scripts/proto-elamite/data/pe_corpus.json, 7,110 entries): graphic derivations are given by the sign list itself, variant forms X~a..X~j (added marks, hatching) and compounds |A+B|. Profiles: slot in the entry (alone/first/middle/last), numeral-system class of the entry (capacity / sexagesimal / bisexagesimal / fraction / N23 / none), left and right neighbours; JSD vs 1,000 frequency-matched random signs not of the same base; min 5 tokens each (tools/dark_loop28_c4_pe.py) | 149 pairs (90 variants, 36 compound-first, 23 compound-later). Variants: mean percentile 0.20 on slot, 0.33 on numeral system and on both neighbour profiles (all P < 0.05); 70% keep the base's modal slot, 81% its modal numeral system; relative-position shift -0.03 vs stranger -0.02; co-occurrence with the base 9 vs 17.7 expected. Compounds: the first element predicts slot (0.31*) and numeral class (0.37*), the later element predicts the numeral class (0.21*) but not slot or neighbours | **In Proto-Elamite a graphic variant is functionally the same sign (same slot, same commodity class, half the expected co-occurrence with its base), and a compound takes its slot from its first element: an allograph/determinative layer, not an operator layer.** The Indus 'strokes' and 'family' pairs behave the same way (S-DARK-28.2: derived keeps the base's slot and neighbours, no fixed positional shift, substitutes for the base in minimal pairs), so on this measure the two scripts' derived signs are alike; neither shows added marks acting as a grammatical operator. Indus doubling (slot change, S-DARK-28.2) has no PE counterpart in this test (PE reduplications |M296+M296| etc. are too few). |
| S-DARK-30.2 | **Loop 30, cycle 2: do anomalous-direction seals (L/R, BUS, T/B, SYM; 60 lines >= 2 signs) differ in text from ordinary R/L seals (1,956)?** 14 features (length, opener, closer class, number of closer-class signs, numerals, stock-text status, nested credential = opener + closer + L >= 6, frame-only short = closer, no opener, L <= 3, area, boss); label permuted within site x material (1,000x) and again within site x material x length; Mohenjo-daro alone, other sites alone, home square/rectangular only; seq_raw/strong/all. `tools/dark_loop30.py 2` | Anomalous seals are shorter: 3.57 vs 4.53 signs (null -0.50, p = 0.014-0.024 at the three merge levels; Mohenjo-daro alone -1.14, p = 0.006-0.014; other sites -0.75, p = 0.29). Their closer classes are the ordinary mix (none 50%, jar 27%, other 22%, arrow 2% vs 43/36/14/8%). Frame-only short texts 37% vs 18% (p = 0.03) and fewer openers (10% vs 19%, p = 0.07) and arrow closers (p = 0.05) are all length effects: with length in the strata every text feature sits on its null (p 0.34-0.89), including stock-text status (13% vs 7%, p = 0.6), nested credentials (1.7% vs 5.9%, p = 0.6) and numerals (p = 0.9). Area (1.60 vs 1.41 cm2, p = 0.8) and boss (87% vs 84%, p = 0.6) do not differ. Nothing passes Bonferroni over 14 features (0.0036). Identical at seq_raw, seq_strong, seq_all | **Null beyond length.** Seals recorded with a non-standard direction carry ordinary texts, only shorter, and the length deficit itself is the expected signature of direction misjudged on 2-sign texts (33 of 61 flagged lines), not of a different object class. No support for a 'display seal' text register; the length result rests on Mohenjo-daro (22 of 60) |

With length held only two survive: 'no closer' (-0.20 vs null +0.05, p 0.001; i.e. among short texts the closer-bearing ones get copied, = S-DARK-16.3/S344) and area (1.12 vs 1.44 cm2, p 0.001; S-DARK-16.3 had p 0.05). Opener, jar, numerals, nested credentials all go to p >= 0.14 when length is held. TAG-only working seals (50): shorter (p 0.001), otherwise null (jar p 0.67, numerals p 0.24, nested 0 of 50 vs 6%, p 0.09). Anomalous seals: length sits halfway (pos +0.51, CI 0.26-0.74), stock-text rate 13% (pos +0.22) and frame-only 37% (pos +0.43) between the groups; standardized distance to the dead mean 1.1 vs 2.0-2.1 to the working mean at all merge levels (TAG-only 1.0 vs 1.7-1.9) | **Working = short closer phrases; the anomalous seals pattern with the dead majority, but only because they are ordinary.** Their match rate (8 of 60) equals the ordinary 8.9%, so 'nearer dead' means 'typical seal with a shorter text', not a display class. 'Use' is still 90% tablet copies (TAB 157 of 174), so this measures what scribes copied, not what was impressed. Transcription-robust across merge levels; rests on Mohenjo-daro (107 of 174 working) |
| S-DARK-30.4 | **Loop 30, cycle 4: credential-model cross-check.** Prediction A (status display): dead/display seals carry the long nested credential texts (opener + closer-class sign, >= 6 signs), working seals the short frame-only texts (closer, no opener, <= 3 signs). Prediction B (ledger): the reverse. Working = exact TAG/TAB match; working-vs-dead and anomalous-vs-each tested with the label permuted within site x material (1,000x) and within site x material x length; all sites, Mohenjo-daro, Harappa, other sites; seq_raw/strong/all. `tools/dark_loop30.py 4` | Direction A at every level and site: nested credentials 0 of 174-196 working vs 6.5% of dead (p 0.001; MD 0 vs 8.0%, p 0.003; HP 0 vs 4.4%, p 0.24-0.38; other 0 vs 3.5%, p 0.53-0.59); frame-only short 68% vs 12.5-13% (p 0.001 at all four site splits). But the nested effect is 100% length: no working seal text has more than 4 signs (5 at strong/all), so with length held the nested difference equals its null exactly (p 1.0 everywhere). The anomalous-direction seals are not a distinct class on this axis. Holds at all three merge levels; the Mohenjo-daro share is 61% of working seals and the Harappa/other replications are at p 0.005-0.05 for the frame-only result only. Verdict for the loop: display-seal hypothesis NOT supported by any of four tests; the L/R flag is most likely a transcription/photo-orientation artefact concentrated in 2-sign texts (photo check list in loop30_cycle1.txt) |
| S-DARK-28.2 | Derived vs base distribution for every S-DARK-28.1 edge with both signs >= 5 tokens (component types + family proxy). Profiles: position bin (alone/first/second/middle/penult/last), S310 frame label, object type, site, left and right neighbours (top 60 + other + boundary), numeral-before rate, mean relative position. Distance = Jensen-Shannon; null = 1,000 draws of a random sign with frequency within x2 of the base and no graphic link to the derived sign; statistic = percentile of JSD(base, derived) in the null (0 = twin, 0.5 = stranger) and the relative-position shift of the derived sign vs the base against its shift vs strangers. Also co-occurrence of base and derived in one text vs independence and vs random partners, and minimal pairs (texts identical except base <-> derived) vs random partners. Train MD+H, confirm on held-out sites and on IM77 through one-to-one bridge mappings. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | Usable edges 108 / 95 / 92 (strokes 18/17/16, doubling 10, enclosure 4, roof 3/2, family 73/62/60; ligature 0). **Added strokes:** derived keeps the base's position (pct 0.32/0.31/0.31, P < 0.05), frame label (0.35/0.35/0.33, P < 0.05), right neighbours (0.31, P < 0.05) and left neighbours (0.37/0.39/0.36); relative-position shift +0.05 vs stranger +0.05 (no operator shift); numeral-before rate RISES by +0.12/+0.13/+0.11 (W595 -> W597 0.00 -> 1.00; W31 -> W55), i.e. marked forms are counted more, not less; co-occurrence 7 vs 10 expected; minimal pairs 9 vs 1.9-2.1 random (W233 <-> W231 x3, W741 <-> W742 x2, W142 <-> W97 x2). **Doubling:** stranger-like profiles (pct 0.42-0.65) but a consistent LEFTWARD move: shift -0.23 vs -0.09/-0.10 for strangers, last-slot share -0.23, tablet share -0.23; doubled forms of closers and suffixes leave their slot (W700 -> W697/W698 CLOSER/last -> NAME/first, W520 -> W519, W400 -> W401, W820 -> W821); minimal pairs 6-7 vs 1.8-2.0. **Enclosure** (n = 4): stranger-like, never co-occurs with the base, no minimal pairs. **Roof**: W233 -> W235 (fish bar -> fish hat) co-occur 26 vs 8 expected and give 8 minimal pairs = the S296 fish word class. **Family proxy** (same Wells block): label 0.41/0.39/0.39 (P < 0.05), right neighbours 0.44 (P < 0.05 at seq_raw), shift 0.00 vs +0.02, minimal pairs 44/35/35 vs 10.4/7.7/7.8. Train (MD+H) gives the same pattern (strokes 0.32/0.29/0.37/0.30; doubling shift -0.23 vs -0.09); held-out sites: only 6-7 train-significant edges survive the 3-token cut, mean pct 0.23-0.25 (position) and 0.11-0.15 (label), all below 0.5; IM77 (26 edges): strokes position 0.25, left 0.20, right 0.21 (P < 0.05 at seq_raw), family 0.40-0.41, doubling 2 edges only | **No operator layer.** Added strokes do not shift a sign's slot, do not convert an item into a credential, and do not replace a numeral (the marked forms take MORE numerals): a stroke-marked sign is a paradigm-mate of its base, substituting for it in otherwise identical texts at 4-5x the random rate while keeping slot and neighbours, exactly like the fish qualifiers (S296) and like Proto-Elamite variants (S-DARK-28.4). Enclosure has no measurable grammar (4 pairs). Doubling is the one type with a directional effect, and it is the opposite of 'plural in place': the doubled sign leaves the closer/suffix slot and moves to the front, on seals rather than tablets; with 10 pairs this is a lead, not a result. Transcription-robust for strokes (IM77), stable over the three merge levels, weak on held-out sites for lack of tokens. |
| S-DARK-28.3 | Prediction test. (a) If a derived sign is a paradigm-mate of its base, RARE derived signs (1-4 tokens, never used in S-DARK-28.2) should stand in the base's contexts: share of their tokens whose left neighbour, right neighbour and position bin fall inside the base's majority sets (60% mass) vs 300 draws of a frequency-matched random base. (b) Holes: frequent bases (>= 30 tokens) with no attested derivative of a type; rare signs that the component detector derives from such a base at a relaxed threshold (0.80) are candidate fillers and are scored the same way. (c) IM77 signs with no Wells counterpart (unbridged, 2-10 tokens) scored against Mahadevan signs with adjacent numbers (|dM| <= 2, shape families) vs random bases, as the only graphic proxy available for signs without a glyph. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | (a) Rare stroke-marked signs (23/22/20): mean context hit 0.47/0.46/0.45 vs null 0.34/0.33/0.33, **P = 0.003 / 0.007 / 0.017**; left neighbour 0.66 vs 0.46, position 0.54 vs 0.40. Doubling (11): 0.35 vs 0.31, P = 0.25-0.30. Enclosure (3) and ligature (2): at or below null. All 153/144/139 rare edges incl. the family proxy: 0.39 vs 0.33, P <= 0.003. (b) Of 70 frequent bases, 50 lack a strokes derivative, 60 a doubling, 67 an enclosure, 67 a ligature; relaxed-threshold fillers 10 / 2 / 2 (nearly all one sign, W807 <- W806, 2 tokens, in jar-400 and 33-388 contexts), context hits 0.30/0.10/0.70 vs null 0.30/0.11/0.59: no filler takes its base's contexts. (c) 128 unbridged IM77 signs, 162 pairs: 0.39/0.22/0.50 vs 0.37/0.18/0.49; adjacent base beats random in 53% (P = 0.47) | **The paradigm-mate prediction holds out of sample**: stroke-marked signs too rare to enter cycle 2 still sit where their base sits, at 1.4x the random rate (P <= 0.017 at all merge levels). The 'operator' predictions have nothing to fill: the holes (bases without a marked form) are not filled by any rare Wells sign or by any unbridged IM77 sign, which is what a non-productive, lexical set of marked forms predicts and what a productive operator would contradict. Caveat: (c) uses Mahadevan's numbering as a shape proxy, which is weak; (b) found only one real candidate. |
| S-DARK-29.1 | Same-holder test (a): pairs of complete seals (and, separately, tablets/sealings) at Mohenjo-daro and Harappa in five kinship classes - identical text; same middle (>= 2 signs, S310/S331 parser) + different closer unit; same closer unit (closer + frozen title) + different middle; one-sign substitution (minimal pair, same length >= 3); same emblem with no shared sign (production control). Share of pairs in the same area-section, the same house block, the same room (area + block + room-grid), the same recorded period, and mean |depth difference| (ft; depth from inscriptions.csv by CISI), vs 1,000 draws of random same-site pairs matched on object type x text length; zero counts as < 3/n; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (tools/dark_loop29.py 1) | **Seals: no kinship class predicts the room or the house.** Mohenjo-daro (971 seals, 706 with a room): same-middle-different-closer pairs 18-24, same area 2/17-23 (0.09-0.12 vs null 0.17-0.19, P 0.75-0.91), same block 0/13-16 (< 0.19), same room 0/6-9 (< 0.33), depth diff 6.6 vs 6.1 ft (P 0.68); minimal pairs 193-258: same room 0/117-159 (< 0.02), same area 0.16 vs 0.18 (P 0.76-0.83); same closer + different middle (3,000 sampled): same room 0-1/1,449-1,456 (null 0.001), same area 0.17-0.19 vs 0.19. Identical seal texts (59-75 pairs): same area 0.30-0.32 vs 0.20-0.21 (P 0.02-0.03) but same block 0/40-50 (< 0.075), same room 0/32-40 (< 0.09), depth 6.6-7.0 vs 6.6 ft (P 0.5-0.7). Harappa seals (271): identical pairs 13-15, same grid square 2/6-7 (null 0.015, P 0.007-0.016); same-middle-different-closer 2 pairs (0/1 same square); same-closer-different-middle 4/275-304 same square vs 0.004 (P 0.01-0.016, the Granary/Trench I squares). **Tablets/sealings behave differently:** identical tablet texts cluster at Harappa (same square 0.07-0.11 vs 0.022, same area 0.18-0.20 vs 0.12, same period 0.38 vs 0.32, all P 0.001) and at Mohenjo-daro (same block 9/55 vs 0.038, depth 3.1-3.6 vs 4.7 ft, P <= 0.004); Harappa tablet minimal pairs also cluster (same square 11/192 vs 0.02, same period 0.53 vs 0.37, P <= 0.005); but same-middle-different-closer tablets do not (0/6-16 same room, area P 0.23-0.43). Identical at all three merge levels. | **Null for the holder model on seals; the only spatial kinship is copy-sharing on tablets.** Two seals that share a middle and differ in the closer are found no nearer each other than frequency-matched random seals: 0 of 13-16 in the same house block (< 19%), 0 of 6-9 in the same room, no depth or period match. Minimal pairs and shared closers are equally unclustered. The one seal effect, identical texts 1.5x more often in the same area-section at Mohenjo-daro (P 0.02-0.03), stops at the area level (never the same block or room) and is the S-DARK-12.2(c) stock-copy effect, not co-ownership; at Harappa the 2/6 identical seals in one grid square are the Granary/Trench I stock seals. Tablets with identical texts or one-sign substitutions cluster by square, period and depth (S-DARK-12.4 replicated with a length-matched null), consistent with batch deposition of vouchers. Rests on Mohenjo-daro (18-24 holder pairs) + Harappa (2); same-middle pairs with a known room are only 6-9, so the room-level upper bound is weak (< 33%); the block-level bound (< 19%) and the area-level deficit are the usable results. |
| S-DARK-29.3 | Same-holder test (c), production control: cycle-1 kinship classes with the random-pair null additionally matched on material x coarse emblem class (object type x length x material x emblem strata), so proximity that survives is deposition and proximity that vanishes is production; also the share of kin pairs sharing material / emblem under the length-only null (cycle 1) as the production signal; Mohenjo-daro and Harappa, seals and tablets separately, 1,000 draws, seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (tools/dark_loop29.py 3) | **Holder classes stay null under both nulls** (Mohenjo-daro same-middle-different-closer: same area 2/17-23 vs 0.16-0.17, P 0.66-0.84; same block 0/13-16; same room 0/6-9; depth 6.6 vs 5.6 ft, P 0.82-0.86; minimal pairs same room 0/117-159, area P 0.73-0.78; Harappa 2 pairs, 0/1). **Production signal is strong for identical seal texts:** identical Mohenjo-daro seal pairs share the emblem 0.84-0.86 vs 0.65 expected and material 0.91-0.92 vs 0.94; yet their same-area excess survives emblem + material matching (0.30-0.32 vs 0.20, P 0.015-0.020) while block (0/40-50) and room (0/32-40) stay empty. **Tablet clustering splits:** Mohenjo-daro identical tablets (mostly copper, same emblem) lose everything once material + emblem are matched - same block 9/55 vs 0.12-0.13 (P 0.06-0.07 from 0.001), depth 3.1-3.6 vs 3.2-3.7 ft (P 0.32-0.34 from 0.001), same area 0.39 vs 0.39 (P 0.45) - so it is production batches; Harappa identical tablets lose the grid-square excess (0.08-0.09 vs 0.10-0.11, P 0.97-0.99, from 0.001) but keep area (0.18-0.20 vs 0.13, P 0.001), period (0.39-0.40 vs 0.33, P 0.001) and depth (5.2 vs 5.8 ft, P 0.001-0.002); Harappa tablet minimal pairs keep all of it (square 11/192 vs 0.011-0.012, area 0.15 vs 0.09-0.10, period 0.53 vs 0.40, all P 0.001). Same at all three merge levels. | **No holder effect to decompose; the kinship that does cluster is partly production, partly deposition, and all of it is copy-level.** Seals sharing a middle with a different closer are no closer in space under either null, so the credential model's one spatial prediction fails at both big cities (power limited: 18-24 pairs, only 13-16 with a known house block). The spatial kinship that exists is (i) a workshop/batch effect for Mohenjo-daro copper tablets and Harappa square-level tablet clusters, which vanishes when material and emblem are held, and (ii) a deposition effect for Harappa tablets at the area/period level and for identical Mohenjo-daro seal texts at the area level, which survives it. Identical seal texts being 1.3x more emblem-concordant than random seals of the same length points to stock seals cut in series (S-DARK-12), not to one owner holding duplicates in one house (0 of 40-50 same block). Rests on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa. |
| S-DARK-29.4 | Same-holder test (d): cycle-1 kinship classes and cycle-2 room grouping repeated at Kalibangan, Chanhu-daro, Lothal and Dholavira (the only other sites with area/room fields; canonical JSON joined to inscriptions.csv for depth), then the direct test at every site: among seal pairs found in the same room (area + block + room-grid), the share with the same middle (>= 2 signs, also >= 1) and a different closer unit ('one holder, several grades'), against room labels permuted within site x object type (1,000x); seals and tablets separately; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (tools/dark_loop29.py 4) | **Holder pairs do not exist at the small sites and never share a room anywhere.** Same-middle-different-closer seal pairs: Kalibangan 0, Chanhu-daro 0, Lothal 0, Dholavira 0 (>= 2-sign middles; 1-sign middles 1, 1, 3-4, 1 pairs, none in the same area or room). What co-deposited seals do share: Harappa same-square seals have Jaccard 0.13 vs 0.056 (2.3x, P 0.001), the same closer unit 6/45 vs 0.028 (4.7x, P 0.004) and identical text 2/45 (P 0.001; the Granary and Trench I stock seals); Chanhu-daro 3 same-room pairs include one identical pair (C-53 = C-58, 861-2-235-222-740-31-32, loc. 211 sq. 9/D; P 0.004-0.007); Mohenjo-daro same-room seals share nothing beyond chance (Jaccard 1.12x, P 0.13; closer 9/218 vs 0.038, P 0.42; opener 0.53 vs 0.59, P 0.95); Kalibangan, Lothal and Dholavira same-area seals null (Jaccard 0.7-1.1x). Lothal identical sealings (101 pairs, 28/36 same area) are the one-seal impression set and sit at the null of their own stratum. Same at all three merge levels. | **The direct prediction of the credential model fails everywhere it can be tested: no room at any site holds two seals with the same middle and different closers (0 of 277 same-room seal pairs over four sites, < 1.1%).** Nothing replicates at the small sites because the kinship classes themselves are empty there (0 holder pairs, 0-2 minimal pairs), which is itself informative: on 196 complete seals outside the two big cities not one pair repeats a 2-sign middle under a different closer. The room-level sharing that exists is identical stock texts (Harappa squares, one Chanhu-daro room) and, at Harappa, shared closer units (the office/title, 4.7x), i.e. co-deposited seals share the formula, not the holder element. Result rests on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa for power; the small-site bounds (< 27%, < 100%) exclude nothing on their own. Combined with cycles 1-3: textual kinship between seals tracks production (series-cut stock texts, same emblem) and office (closer), never deposition of a person's several grades. The holder-in-the-middle reading loses its one spatial test; the middle still has no observable that behaves like an owner (S321, S-DARK-26). |
| S-DARK-2.1 | Arrow-in-the-dark, pair+set machine (tools/strat_dark_pairs2.py, seed 101). Pair arrows: random text-pair relation (30 families incl. new: complementary, samemid_diffclose, prefix1, suffix1, numeq, numdiff, summod, sameopen_diffmid, sharek, revmatch, sameclose_diffmid, bothopen, bothjar, lendiff, ident) x random object-pair fact (type, emblem, material, shape, boss, area, room, time, period, size h/v, depth, cult, color, completeness, direction, condition, sides); MI statistic; null = object-level permutation of the fact within site (and within site x type as a second null); train MD+Harappa, replicate on held-out sites (p<0.01) and seq_all; Bonferroni over arrows fired. Set arrows: find-groups (site, area, block, room) >= 3 objects, 29 set statistics x size band x type filter x dedup; null = re-deal objects among groups of same sizes within site x type; train MD, replicate Harappa (same direction) + seq_all. Control: whole machine on facts scrambled within site | PAIR 1500 fired (1472 testable), 300 train hits p<0.002 (44 replicated on held-out sites + seq_all, 0 also Bonferroni at 1500 arrows AND within-site-x-type null). SET 500 fired (279 testable), 1 MD hits p<0.004 (0 replicated at Harappa + seq_all) | pending control |

The unidentifiable part (authorities, credentials per holder, name vs qualification) stays so with twice the simulations: it is a limit of the corpus, not of the sample size** |
| S-DARK-26.4 | **Lexicon or attribute code, part 4: the same tests on deduplicated populations** (one copy per distinct site + middle: Indus 2,643 -> 1,774, seals 1,231, tablets 447, frame-purged 1,660; Ur III names 1,065 by string, the conservative dedup of S-DARK-18.2; planted code 2,263; planted lexicon 1,684). | S-DARK-31.2 | **Interpreting the offsets.** (a) Known relations as offset directions: for each documented relation kind, the pairs inside the 150-sign MD+H vocabulary, statistic = mean pairwise cosine of their offset vectors E_a - E_b (PPMI+SVD dim 30, cycle 1 embedding); null = the same number of pairs drawn at random with the same log2-frequency bins, 2,000x. Kinds: numeral steps (short 1->3->4->5, tall 1->2->3->4), short<->tall numeral of equal value (S204), plain fish -> marked fish (W220 -> 233/240/235/231/226), marked<->marked fish, jar -> marked jar (740 -> 741/742/745), seal/tablet and S268 allographs, closer -> its own qualifier (S289/S303, 16 pairs), opener<->opener, closer<->closer, pre-jar titles. (b) The blind cycle-1 offset classes scored against this list (share of member pairs that are known relations vs the share among all vocabulary pairs). (c) New-pair prediction: for each kind that passed (a), o = mean offset; for every other sign c, d = NN(E_c + o) if cos >= 0.6 and d is not already c's nearest neighbour; the predicted (d, c) pairs tested OUTSIDE the fitting corpus on held-out Wells sites (436 complete texts, not MD/H) and on IM77 (3,287 texts, bridge_extended + loop 27 proposals): context-profile cosine of d and c vs 500 frequency-matched pairs, and for closer -> qualifier the count of c directly before d; controls = random vocabulary pairs through the same test, calibration = the known pairs themselves. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | (a) **Two relations are real directions in the space at all three levels: plain fish -> marked fish, offset cos 0.55 / 0.54 / 0.54 vs null median 0.03-0.04, 95% 0.20-0.21 (P = 0.0005; the four marked fish 233/240/235/231 pairwise 0.79-0.82, the 4-stroke fish W226 only 0.25), and closer -> its qualifier, 0.33 / 0.38 / 0.38 vs 0.01-0.02, 95% 0.06-0.07 (P = 0.0005; 154/158-806 0.96, 527-550/555 0.84, 700-32/34 0.82, 520-220/240 0.75, 740-100/176/760 0.72-0.74).** Weaker: short<->tall numeral of equal value 0.26 / 0.36 / 0.34 (P = 0.007 / 0.0015 / 0.002; 3-33 and 17-33 0.71, 16-32 0.50). Not directions: numeral steps (short -0.11 / -0.10 / -0.08, tall -0.27 / -0.24 / -0.25, P > 0.8: the stroke numerals are a cluster, not a line), allographs (S262/S268: -0.00, P = 0.60 in seq_raw where 15 pairs exist; they are near-identical vectors, cos 0.68, so their offsets are noise), jar -> marked jar (0.59 but null median 0.50 because 741/742/745 are all rare and sit in one place: P = 0.09), marked<->marked fish (0.08, P = 0.09-0.22), titles (0.04, P = 0.18). Opener<->opener and closer<->closer have |cos| 0.30-0.47 (P = 0.0005) with directional cos 0.04-0.19: these are tight clusters, not relations. (b) **The blind classes carry the known relations at base rate**: 20 / 29 / 26 of 1,503 / 1,545 / 1,572 member pairs are known relations vs 17.1 / 14.1 / 14.1 expected; 0 classes at any level hold two known pairs of one kind. (c) 114 / 114 / 111 predicted pairs. Held-out sites: 12 / 3 / 9 of 57 / 50 / 53 beat the 95% context null (control random pairs 4 / 4 / 3 of 60 / 48 / 59). IM77: 19 / 16 / 20 of 107 / 102 / 98 (control 5 / 7 / 5 of ~103). But the closer -> qualifier predictions FAIL the adjacency test the relation is defined by: predicted qualifier directly before its closer >= 2x and P < 0.05 in 0 / 0 / 0 of 24 / 28 / 31 held-out and 3 / 1 / 2 of 44 / 50 / 52 IM77 cases (expected 1.2-2.6), while the known pairs pass it 15 / 11 / 12 of 16 / 14 / 14 in IM77. Context hits are same-slot signs (3<-4, 16<-416, M104<-M106, M67<-M70), not new relations. | **The geometry has two genuine, sharp offset directions and nothing else: the fish mark (plain fish -> any of the four marked fish) and head -> modifier (a closer -> the qualifier class it selects). Both are relations the frame already documents (S296, S303); the fish mark is the one case in the sign list where a graphic modification is also a consistent vector offset.** Numeral steps are not encoded as a direction (the stroke numerals form a cluster; nothing in the space orders 3 < 4 < 5), allographs are identities not offsets, and the blind parallelogram search does not recover even the two real directions. The probe-direction predictions generalise to held-out sites and IM77 only as 'same slot class' (3-4x the control hit rate on context similarity), not as pair-specific relations (adjacency at chance). No new relation candidate survives; the offsets encode slot-class membership (move from the closer cluster to the qualifier cluster, from plain to marked fish), which is what a slot code with a few marked classes gives, and not a lexicon with productive analogies. Robust to merge level; held-out and IM77 figures are transcription-robust but rest on 436 held-out texts. |
| S-DARK-31.3 | **Is a derived sign the vector sum of its parts?** Embeddings (PPMI+SVD dim 30, same features as cycle 1) on all complete Wells texts of every site (3,276; seq_raw keeps ligatures and marked forms apart), vocabulary = every sign with >= 4 tokens (287 / 270 / 266 signs). Derivation edges from loop 28 (`loop28_edges_all.json`: strokes, enclosure, roof, doubling, ligature found blind from the glyphs, plus the 'family' proxy = same Wells 20-block and glyph similarity >= 0.6) and the two documented attached-mark families (fish marks, jar marks). (a) Ligatures: cos(E_derived, E_base + E_partner) vs the same with 2,000 frequency-matched random part pairs, and vs each part alone. (b) Operator consistency: mean pairwise cosine of the offsets E_derived - E_base within one derivation type and sub-type (is 'add a stroke', 'enclose', 'double' one direction?) vs 2,000 frequency-matched random base/derived pairs. (c) cos(derived, base) vs random (does the derived sign behave like its base?). (d) The fish-mark direction applied to the 50 other bases with a marked variant: rank of the real derived sign among the nearest neighbours of base + fish-mark vs a random derived sign (500x). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | (a) Only 2 of 15 ligatures have all three members at >= 4 tokens (W101 = W794 + W920, W48 = W422 + W903): cos(sum) 0.11 and 0.07 vs null 0.14 and 0.09 (P = 0.55, 0.53) at every level; **no additivity, and the test is underpowered by the rarity of ligatures (median 2 tokens, S-DARK-28.1)**. (b) **No derivation type is a direction**: strokes n = 26-28 edges offset cos 0.02-0.03 vs null 0.01 (95% 0.03; P = 0.07-0.12), 1-stroke 0.04 (P = 0.035, seq_raw only), 2-stroke 0.09 (P = 0.08), 4-stroke 0.21 (P = 0.047, n = 4); enclosure n = 5: 0.09 / 0.04 / -0.02 (P = 0.10-0.70); doubling n = 12: 0.06 / 0.05 / 0.05 vs 0.04-0.05 (P = 0.35-0.40); roof n = 2-3: 0.19 (P = 0.04-0.16; driven by 233 -> 235, the fish roof); family proxy n = 68-84: 0.04 vs 0.03 (P = 0.02-0.19); ligature n = 2: -0.10 to -0.02. Against this, the two documented mark families are directions: plain fish -> marked fish 0.52 (null 0.06, 95% 0.25, P = 0.0005) and jar -> marked jar 0.69 (null 0.01, 95% 0.20, P = 0.0005; but see (c)). (c) **Derived signs with detached strokes or in the same glyph family are used like their base**: cos(derived, base) 0.27 / 0.25 / 0.26 vs null 0.10 (P = 0.0005) for strokes, 0.24 / 0.19 / 0.21 vs 0.09 (P = 0.0005) for the family proxy, 0.65 vs 0.18 for the marked fish (P = 0.0005); not for doubling (0.14 / 0.12 / 0.12 vs 0.08, P = 0.09-0.19), enclosure (0.04 vs 0.09, P = 0.78) or the marked jars (0.03 vs 0.08, P = 0.70: W741/742/745 share a direction away from W740 because they share a slot, S-DARK-19.2's '741 first in the middle', not because they are jars). (d) Base + fish-mark puts the real derived sign in the top 5 for 5 / 4 / 6 of 50 / 48 / 47 edges vs random-derived median 1 (95% 3): a weak excess carried by the fish family itself (233 -> 235, 235 -> 236). | **Not compositional.** Adding a stroke, an enclosure or a doubling to a sign does not move it by a fixed vector, and the two testable ligatures are not the sum of their parts; the only operator that is a direction is the fish mark (and, trivially, the marked jars, which are a slot class). What the stroke and family derivations do show is weak retention of the base's usage (cos 0.25 vs 0.10): a derived sign is often used in the base's contexts, which fits a graphic variant or a sub-type of the base (S-DARK-28's 'base + mark' reading), not a word built by composition. Combined with cycle 2: the sign list has exactly one productive mark whose meaning is a consistent shift in use (the fish qualifiers), and the rest of the graphic-derivation layer is either allography or idiosyncratic. Caveat: 85% of loop 28's derived signs have < 4 tokens and are not in the vocabulary; the ligature verdict rests on 2 pairs. |
| S-DARK-32.1 | **Typology classifier, cycle 1: library + joint battery + first LOCO classifier.** 20 reference corpora of known type assembled from open sources (`tools/dark_loop32_prep.py`; SOURCES.txt): 6 language-writing (Ur III legends as words / syllables, Ur III owner names, Linear B lines as words / syllables, Latin EDH; RunesDB 613 texts test-only), 5 designed codes (Rietstap blazons, ICD-10-CM, HTS tariff numbers, Unicode character names, OpenSky aircraft registrations), 2 grammar-without-language (lichess ECO openings, Chordonomicon chord sections), 3 accounting (khipu, Proto-Elamite, proto-cuneiform lines, new), plus Indus seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all / IM77 and three controls (slot-shuffled, Markov-2, bigram Indus). 36 structural features from the repository's own code (strat_codelibrary battery, dark_loop19 pair census, dark_loop25 hubs / loyalty, slot exclusivity, numeral behaviour, slot-vs-bigram held-out bits), 8 length-matched subsamples per corpus (2-13 signs, cap 3,000). L2 multinomial logistic regression, leave-one-corpus-out; label-permutation null (40); nearest centroid and 1-NN (`tools/dark_loop32.py`) | LOCO accuracy **7/16 = 0.44 = majority-class chance** (permutation null mean 0.30, 95th pct 0.62, P = 0.20); nearest centroid 0.50, 1-NN 0.38. Misclassified: khipu, proto-cuneiform, Proto-Elamite, heraldry, HTS, Unicode names, chords, Linear B syllables, Ur III words. Indus gets P(A) 0.91-0.92 at all three levels and IM77 but the controls fail (slot-shuffled -> A 0.76, Markov-2 0.50 / 0.50, bigram -> L 0.54), so the Indus probabilities are uncalibrated and not reported. Model-free nearest corpora of Indus: proto-cuneiform 4.2-4.7, Linear B syllables 4.8, Ur III names 5.0-5.2, Proto-Elamite 5.2 (same at raw / strong / all; synthetic Indus lands nearest Ur III syllables, so that nearness is uninformative). Strongest univariate separator Indus vs language: num_run_share (0.07 vs 0.005, z +8) and num_end_bias (-0.26 vs +0.28), but 3 of 6 language corpora carry no numeral annotation; det_loy50_max 0.34 vs 0.66 (S-DARK-25) is the cleanest | **Inconclusive by design check: the joint 3-class typology is not learnable from 16 heterogeneous corpora with 36 features (chance accuracy), so no Indus class can be read off it yet.** Library and battery are sound and reusable (raw / strong / all agree to 0.01). Cycle 2 reduces features by nested selection, drops the annotation-dependent numeral features and asks the binary question 'language writing or not'. Nothing read, nothing 'cracked'. |

| S-DARK-33.2 | **Held-out acceptance and ROC of the induced automata.** ALERGIA (alpha 0.05), MDL-merge, Markov-1/2 and a 20-state HMM fitted on 80% of MD+H (seals; all types with the object type as start symbol); threshold = 5th percentile of the per-sign log-likelihood on the other 20%. Genuine: held-out sites (297 seals / 451 texts), the 324 IM77-only certainly-new texts (S-DARK-27; 164 seals; M->W via the completed bridge, 0 unbridged tokens). Negatives from the same texts: within-text shuffle, within-slot shuffle, random unigram strings. Controls with the same pipeline (cycle 5): a strict designed code (6 ordered optional fields), the loop 25 planted frame, Ur III legends, Linear B lines, Proto-Elamite lines, a Markov-2 clone of the Indus seals, shuffled Indus. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 2`, `5`) | **Genuine held-out sites accepted 0.94-0.97 (seals), IM77-new seals 0.77-0.82; but within-text shuffles of the same texts are accepted 0.62-0.86 and random strings 0.58-0.92. AUC genuine vs shuffle: held-out sites 0.72-0.77 (texts >= 3 signs 0.82-0.85), IM77-new 0.63-0.72; vs slot shuffle 0.53-0.62.** Controls: strict code AUC 0.97-0.99 (rejects 93-96% of shuffles), planted frame 0.91-0.94, Ur III legends 0.90-0.95, Markov-2 clone of Indus 0.82-0.85, Proto-Elamite 0.79-0.82, Linear B 0.71-0.76, shuffled Indus 0.49-0.50. All three levels within 0.03. | **A compact automaton does not behave like a designed code on new texts.** It accepts genuine new material, but it cannot tell a genuine Indus text from its own shuffle much better than chance beyond the frame (AUC 0.72-0.83 vs 0.97-0.99 for a real code and 0.90-0.95 for Ur III legends); the Indus texts separate no better than a Markov-2 clone of themselves. What the automaton learns is the frame (first/last slots); the inside is unpredictable to it. The 'business code' premise fails this test on all levels. |
| S-DARK-33.3 | **Reading the automaton as a form.** ALERGIA (alpha 0.05, root never merged) on all MD+H seals (CLASS alphabet, 52 symbols) and on all 2,946 texts with the object type as start symbol; every state named by its dominant incoming and outgoing symbols and mean position; loops (edges to an earlier position), second paths, and forbidden class bigrams (expected >= 8 under independence, 0 observed in fit) listed; forbidden bigrams and the class order tested as predictions on held-out sites and the 324 IM77-new texts against within-text shuffles (200x). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 3`; `loop33_automaton_*.json/.dot`) | **Seals: 14-15 states; all types: 61-64 states.** The states are the frame: START -> after-OPENER (CONN 319/400) -> MIDDLE HUB (fish-mark self-loop, numeral phrases, lexicon run, leaf) -> PRE-CLOSER (free titles) or PRE-CLOSER-2 (fixed terms w142/w575/w585/TITLE -> jar) -> END HUB (end 0.79, suffix loop). Extras the hand grammar lacks: loops END HUB -> numeral phrase (27x), -> OPEN (13x), -> CONN (11x) and LEXICON -> OPEN (60x) = second units; a separate state for the fixed pre-jar terms; 18 forbidden bigrams (jar->jar, jar->TITLE, OPEN->fish mark, CONN->tree, jar<->arrow, ...). **Prediction held: forbidden bigrams on held-out sites 5 of 969 (shuffle median 39 [30-48]), IM77-new 4 of 534 (20 [12-26]); all-types 6/1,305 and 9/967 vs 34-39 and 32-33. Class order Kendall tau fit vs held-out 0.51-0.59.** Same on all levels. | **The automaton is the frame plus an unordered hub.** Its only obligatory element is the end; the middle is a hub that returns to itself, so any phrase type may follow any other (S-DARK-26 again), with the lexicon signs all at relative position 0.3-0.5 and no order among them. The new facts are the second-unit loops and the forbidden transitions, which generalise to new sites and new texts (1/7 of the shuffle rate). The 'form' is: [opener connective]? phrases* [titles | fixed term] closer suffix* [second unit]? - a formulaic legend, not a fixed-field record. |
| S-DARK-33.4 | **Size of the code.** Seal automata (ALERGIA 14-15 states; Markov-1/2 for comparison) on all MD+H seals, RAW and CLASS alphabets; best-first enumeration of all strings with P >= 1e-3 / 1e-4 / 1e-5; share seen in the fit set; probability mass of the enumerated set; model P(next text already seen) vs Good-Turing singleton share. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 4`) | **At P >= 1e-4 the automaton generates ~600 (RAW) to ~770 (CLASS) texts, 18-28% of them seen; the set holds only 27-42% of the probability mass (58-73% is in strings rarer than 1e-4).** P(next seal text new): automaton 0.90 (RAW) / 0.73-0.77 (CLASS), Good-Turing 0.82 / 0.70 on the same 1,259 seals (S309: 0.95 for 3+-sign texts, whole corpus). Markov-2 overfits (P(new) 0.67, 47% of its 1e-4 strings seen). Same on all levels. | **The generator is open-ended, not a listable code.** Most of the automaton's mass is in a long tail of individually rare strings, so the low seen/possible ratio is a property of the grammar (a hub of freely combining phrases) rather than of under-sampling a fixed message set; it is consistent with S309's 95% new-text rate without needing the unseen signs. A compact automaton describes the frame and the phrase types, but the number of distinct texts it licenses is unbounded for practical purposes. |

The sum-mod-k features never rank above noise (importance 0.02-0.03, same as in the shuffled runs). Levels agree within 0.05 | **No check sign and no class sign among the sign positions.** Every position is predictable from the rest only to the extent that GRAMMAR.md already says (opener-connective pairing, the ten closer units), i.e. 4-14 points above the slot baseline against a planted ceiling of 100. The last sign is the most 'determined' slot (0.51-0.56) and that determination is lexical (which closer unit), not arithmetic (data/derived/dark/loop34_cycle1.txt) |

(b) No function's exact-match rate beats its null (n signs 0.18 vs 0.20; mod 10 0.08 vs 0.10; mod 7 0.14 vs 0.12, P = 0.06).
