(c) Real anneal held-out -0.02 / +0.04 / -0.01 (sites), -0.17 to -0.25 (IM77), inside the permuted-value spread (-0.03 to -0.22; -0.17 to -0.41); planted additive truth 0.79 -> recovered 0.72 | **The numeral is not a checksum of the text.** Its only predictable component is lexical (W705 takes tall-3) and its only correlate is text length, with the wrong sign for a count-of-signs code and chance-level exact matches. | S366 | **Turing test for the credential grammar** (`tools/strat_adequacy.py`; `data/derived/strat_adequacy.txt`/`.json`; counts from the older `merged-corpus-canonical.json` build, S-DARK-23). Best generative model of a text, fitted on the 4,411 Mohenjo-daro + Harappa texts with the S310 parse: P(opener) × P(marker | opener) × P(middle: order-1 chain with continuation-count backoff) × P(closer | opener present) × P(qualifier string | closer) × P(suffix | closer). 100 synthetic corpora of the same size and object mix; battery of 50 statistics the model is not fitted on (length distribution, unique-text share, nesting, cross-site sharing of 3-runs and texts, MI at distance 1–4, repeated-sign and adjacent-double rates, fish order, long-range attraction and avoidance pairs, middle-initial vs final sets, numeral attachments, positional entropies from both ends, Zipf slope per slot, same middle under different closer, sign/bigram/trigram hapax shares, seal vs tablet length, opener–suffix co-occurrence); real value, synthetic mean ± sd, z, outside-range flag. Mechanisms added one at a time and greedily: object-type effect, site effect, middle lexicon with CRP reuse, closer given the middle's last sign, frame package (opener+marker+closer+suffix jointly), order-2 middle chain, open sign inventory (Good-Turing new-sign mass, S309), whole-text reuse (one CRP per site, θ calibrated to the distinct-text share; a single global CRP was tried first and failed: it spread stock texts over both cities, text3_multisite 0.22 vs 0.02, z = −20). Held-out: 930 texts from 73 other sites with the model fitted on MD+H. Controls: a no-slot order-2 chain as generator; the slot grammar fitted to a no-slot Markov corpus; the order-2 chain refitted to its own output (false-alarm floor of the battery); a planted Tamil syllabic corpus (synth.py) | **Baseline M0: 36 of 50 statistics outside the synthetic range, 35 with |z| > 3, Σ|z| = 378.** Missing structure by |z|: repeated whole texts (texts with ≥ 3 copies 107 vs 19, z = 28; 4-sign runs with ≥ 3 copies 171 vs 33, z = 27), **long-range attraction pairs 127 vs 19 (z = 27)**, unique-text share 0.58 vs 0.76 (z = −27), seal − tablet length 1.36 vs 0.0 (z = 19), **long-range avoidance pairs 272 vs 102 (z = 18)**, bigram conditional entropy 2.77 vs 3.15 (z = −18), **same middle under a different closer 0.20 vs 0.79 (z = −18)**, MI at distance 2 2.49 vs 1.97 (z = 18), trigram hapax share (z = −16), hapax signs 0.35 vs 0.19 (z = 12), **repeated-sign rate 0.043 vs 0.099 (z = −10)**. Single mechanisms: site-local text reuse Σ|z| 222 (fixes unique share, same-middle-different-closer, attraction pairs, MI d4); middle lexicon 271 (fixes MI d1–d4 and attraction pairs, but leaves same-middle-different-closer at z = −35: middles are not recombined freely with closers); closer-given-last-middle-sign 305 (fixes mean length, middle length vs closer, end entropies); order-2 middle 302 (start entropies); object type 347 (tablet suffix, jar-final share, opener–suffix exclusion); site 376, package 380, open inventory 370 do nothing alone. Greedy path: textreuse (221) → closerdep (181) → type (151) → open (137) → site (133). **M* = textreuse + closerdep + type + open + site: 23 of 50 outside, 16 with |z| > 3, Σ|z| = 134, which is at the battery's false-alarm floor (true generator refitted to its own sample: 19 of 50 outside, Σ|z| = 141).** Still outside under M*: long-range avoidance 272 vs 170 (z = 8) and attraction 127 vs 61 (z = 6), repeated-sign rate 0.043 vs 0.092 (z = −8), bigram conditional entropy (z = −6), length sd 2.02 vs 2.33 (z = −6), texts with two numerals 0.044 vs 0.062 (z = −4), start-position entropies (z = −3 to −5), hapax shares (z = 3–5), fish order 0.86 vs 0.75 (z = 2.4). Held-out sites: M0 23/50, Σ|z| 148; M* 19/50, Σ|z| 122 (at the floor); the biggest held-out gap is again long-range attraction (26 vs 4 pairs, z = 12). Controls: no-slot order-2 chain 25/50, Σ|z| 247 (**worse than M* but better than the baseline slot grammar**); slot grammar fitted to a no-slot Markov corpus 34/50, Σ|z| 401 (misfit detected); planted Tamil, order-1 chain 20/46, Σ|z| 180 (its gaps are hapax/unique/MI-d2, as for Indus, plus the length-by-type effect copied from the real lengths; the generator's 14-sign middle cap also shows). Merges: seq_raw M0 379 → M* 139; seq_strong 378 → 133; same ranking | **The frame grammar as a generator is incomplete in three specific ways, and all three are predictions of the credential model, not of a name-plus-title legend:** (1) texts are **copied whole within a city** (stock credentials, S344, S347); a lexicon of middles recombined freely with closers is refuted (80% vs 20% of repeated middles would change closer); (2) the **closer depends on the middle's end** (title phrases, S303), which sets text length and the end-of-text entropy; (3) the **object type sets the frame** (S29). With these, plus an open sign inventory (S309), the real corpus is indistinguishable from the synthetic ones at the battery's calibration floor on 33 of 50 statistics. **What remains unexplained is whole-text planning:** signs that attract or avoid each other across slots at a distance (S51, S67), the near-ban on using a sign twice (S88), a tighter length distribution and fewer double counts than any chain gives. No local mechanism tried here reproduces them; the next model needs a text-level constraint (a set of co-selected, non-repeating elements), i.e. a credential composed from a checklist rather than written left to right. Honest caveat: a plain order-2 chain beats the baseline slot grammar (247 vs 378), so the frame by itself is a poorer generator than local context; its value is in mechanisms (1)–(3), which a chain cannot express. Rests on MD + H (76% of texts); held-out sites agree; not merge-dependent. |
| S-DARK-2.3 | Arrow-in-the-dark, pair+set machine (tools/strat_dark_pairs2.py, seed 303). Pair arrows: random text-pair relation (51 families incl. new: openunit, bothnum, closepair, midlen, midjacc, samelast2, sharenumsign, rotation, frameclass, singlediff) x random object-pair fact (type, emblem, material, shape, boss, area, room, time, period, size h/v, depth, cult, color, completeness, direction, condition, sides); MI statistic; null = object-level permutation of the fact within site (and within site x type as a second null); train MD+Harappa, replicate on held-out sites (p<0.01) and seq_all; Bonferroni over arrows fired. Set arrows: find-groups (site, area, block, room) >= 3 objects, 29 set statistics x size band x type filter x dedup; null = re-deal objects among groups of same sizes within site x type; train MD, replicate Harappa (same direction) + seq_all. Control: whole machine on facts scrambled within site | PAIR 1500 fired (1458 testable), 260 train hits p<0.002 (44 replicated on held-out sites + seq_all, 6 also Bonferroni at 1500 arrows AND within-site-x-type null); scrambled-fact control: 28 train hits, 0 replicated, 0 full. SET 500 fired (298 testable), 1 MD hits p<0.004 (0 replicated at Harappa + seq_all); control: 0 hits, 0 replicated | **Null.** 44 distinct replicated arrows, 6 pass the z-Bonferroni; five are the length/duplication x type/completeness/size confounds, the sixth (frameclass x emblem, z 5.5) is a one-object fact in pair clothing: emblem-less objects (tablets, sealings, pots) have a different opener/closer mix from emblem seals, and among seals the differences are modest (full frame on unicorn 0.18, gaur 0.13, script-only 0.10 at MD; S65/S46 family). Nine arrows die under the within-site-x-type null (material/shape = seal). Control: 28 train hits, 0 replicated. Set stage: 1 hit vs 1.2 expected |
| S-DARK-33.1 | **Induce the whole code as a finite-state machine, part 1: learnability and size.** PFAs learned from MD+H texts (seals; all object types with the type as start symbol) over a 52-symbol CLASS alphabet (frame slots, each paradigm closer, numerals, fish marks, trees, titles, 40 lexicon signs, OTHER) and a RAW alphabet (120 signs + OTHER): ALERGIA (alpha 0.5-0.001), MDL-greedy merging, MAP-EM HMM (K 5-40), unigram / Markov-1 / Markov-2; 80/20 split; two-part MDL. Controls with the same learners: within-text shuffle, within-slot shuffle, a Markov-2 clone of the corpus, Ur III legends, Linear B lines, Proto-Elamite lines (3,000 texts each). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop33.py 1`) | **Seals, CLASS: ALERGIA settles at 8-16 states / 272-426 transitions, dev 2.56-2.59 nats/sign vs unigram 3.02 and Markov-1 2.46; the MDL optimum is a 5-10-state HMM (22-23 kbit vs Markov-1 28.7); HMM-40 is the best predictor (2.33-2.35).** Shuffled texts: 2-4 states, no gain (dev = unigram); slot-shuffled: 5 states, gain 0.30 of the Indus 0.45; Markov-2 clone: gain 0.47-0.54 (learned as well as the real corpus). All types pooled: best automaton 105 states, only equal to Markov-1. RAW gains: Indus seals 0.42 (ALERGIA) / 0.76 (HMM); Ur III legends 1.21-1.29; Linear B 0.63-0.67; Proto-Elamite 0.55-0.77. Same on all levels. | **A small automaton exists and is not an artefact, but it is the frame, not a code.** Two thirds of what it learns is in the first and last positions; a second-order Markov clone is learned just as well; the predictive gain is in the Linear B / Proto-Elamite range and half of Ur III legends. The texts are less predictable than a formulaic legend system, which is the opposite of what a designed business code would give. |

Target of 40 texts not met (14 readable, 7 with >= 3 signs at grade B). |

| S-DARK-29.2 | Same-holder test (b), reverse direction: seals (and separately tablets/sealings) found in the same room (area + block + room-grid; Harappa: trench grid square) or the same area-section - is their pairwise textual similarity (Jaccard of sign sets; same middle >= 2 signs; same closer unit; same opener state; identical text; same middle + different closer = 'holder'; minimal pair) higher than when room/area labels are permuted among same-site objects of the same object type (and type x length bin), 1,000x; groups of >= 2; area-level pairs capped at 6,000 per draw; also seal x tablet pairs in one room vs all same-site seal x tablet pairs; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (tools/dark_loop29.py 2) | **Mohenjo-daro seals: co-deposited seals share nothing.** 108 rooms, 218 same-room pairs: Jaccard 0.066 vs 0.059 (P 0.13-0.26), same closer 9/218 vs 0.038 (P 0.42), same opener state 0.53 vs 0.59 (P 0.95), identical 0/218 (< 1.4%), same middle 0/218, holder 0/218, minimal pair 0/218; by area (6,000 pairs) Jaccard 0.95x (P 0.9), closer 1.00x, identical 5/6000 vs 0.0001 (6.8x, P 0.002-0.005; the stock-seal copies of cycle 1), holder 0-1/6000. **Harappa seals in one grid square share the formula, not the holder element:** 45 pairs, Jaccard 0.128 vs 0.055 (2.3x, P 0.001), same closer unit 6/45 vs 0.028 (4.5-4.9x, P 0.001-0.008), identical 2/45 (P 0.001), same middle 0/45 (< 6.7%), holder 0/45, minimal pair 0/45; by area all null except identical 3/2681 (P 0.02). **Tablets are the opposite:** Harappa same-square tablets Jaccard 0.41 vs 0.12 (3.4x), same middle 235/851 vs 0.007 (37x), identical 299/851 vs 0.043 (8x), minimal pairs 11/851 vs 0.0025 (5x), all P 0.001 (type x length null: 2.1x, 3.2x, 2.9x, 4.8x); same-area 1.25x / 2.8x / 1.7x (P <= 0.003); holder pairs 1/851 (P 0.02-0.04, one pair). Mohenjo-daro tablets: 12 same-room pairs with 1 identical (P 0.01-0.03); same-area 5,373 pairs identical 1.43x (P 0.01), closer 1.32x (P 0.02), holder 14/5373 vs 0.0035 (0.75x, P 0.67). Seal x tablet in one room: Mohenjo-daro 0/35 identical (< 8.6%), Harappa 3/245 (1.2% vs 0.5% for all same-site seal x tablet pairs), same middle 0 at both. Same at all three merge levels. | **Co-deposition does not pick out same-holder seals; it picks out stock copies (tablets, strongly) and, at Harappa, shared closer units.** A room with two or more seals at Mohenjo-daro is textually random (0 of 218 pairs share a middle, an exact text or differ by one sign); at Harappa the 45 same-square seal pairs share the closer/title 4.5x above chance and two are duplicates, with no shared middle (< 6.7%). Tablets co-deposit as batches of the same text (Harappa 8x identical, 37x same middle), replicating S-DARK-12.4 under a room-label permutation, and the one tablet 'holder' pair is within noise. Sealings and tablets were not found with the seals that made them (0/35 and 3/245 identical; no shared middle). The credential model predicted the reverse pattern (shared middles across different closers in one house); the office/closer is the only slot that co-deposited seals share, which fits an institution or a formula held by a place, not a person's set of grades. Rests on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa. |
| S-DARK-2.4 | Arrow-in-the-dark, pair+set machine (tools/strat_dark_pairs2.py, seed 404). Pair arrows: random text-pair relation (52 families incl. new: combo (random and/or/xor of two boolean relations)) x random object-pair fact (type, emblem, material, shape, boss, area, room, time, period, size h/v, depth, cult, color, completeness, direction, condition, sides); MI statistic; null = object-level permutation of the fact within site (and within site x type as a second null); train MD+Harappa, replicate on held-out sites (p<0.01) and seq_all; Bonferroni over arrows fired. Set arrows: find-groups (site, area, block, room) >= 3 objects, 29 set statistics x size band x type filter x dedup; null = re-deal objects among groups of same sizes within site x type; train MD, replicate Harappa (same direction) + seq_all. Control: whole machine on facts scrambled within site | PAIR 1500 fired (1435 testable), 265 train hits p<0.002 (64 replicated on held-out sites + seq_all, 10 also Bonferroni at 1500 arrows AND within-site-x-type null); scrambled-fact control: 28 train hits, 2 replicated, 1 full. SET 500 fired (290 testable), 3 MD hits p<0.004 (0 replicated at Harappa + seq_all); control: 0 hits, 0 replicated | **Null.** 64 distinct replicated arrows, 10 pass the z-Bonferroni (closepair x type z 11.9: tablets carry a fixed small closer set; closepair x shape; lensum/anypos x size; frameclass x emblem/completeness; jacc/lcs2 x type; bothopen x v; lensum x completeness): the same type, breakage and size confounds as cycles 1-3. The new combo family (and/or/xor of two relations) added nothing beyond a tablet-duplicate arrow below Bonferroni. Material/shape arrows die or weaken under the within-site-x-type null. Set stage: 3 MD hits vs 1.2 expected, none replicate at Harappa. Four cycles, 6,000 pair + 2,000 set arrows: no two-text relation predicts any object-pair fact beyond type, breakage, size and emblem-vs-none; see loop2_final.txt |
| S-DARK-35.1 | **Closer paradigm ranked by face area, ordinality test.** Complete seal texts at Mohenjo-daro + Harappa with horizontal and vertical mm in inscriptions.csv (canonical JSON aligned row by row to the CSV; 1,193 seals: MD 931, Harappa 262). Mixed model log(h x v) ~ closer class (S289 paradigm with >= 8 home seals: jar 740, arrow 520, 151, 156, 527, 154, 617, 226; plus 'none', opener-final and rare as own levels) + text-length bin + site + material class + emblem class, random intercept for site x area-section (51 groups; REML-profiled, Woodbury form). Closer effects vs jar with 95% CI. Ordinality: Spearman rho of the closer effects between independent splits (MD vs Harappa; unicorn vs other emblem; square vs non-square; random halves; steatite vs other material has only the jar in common), against closer labels permuted among seals within site x length bin (1,000x, both splits refit each time). Omnibus: variance of the closer effects vs the same null. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | Length and emblem carry the size (len 2 -> 10+: +0.06 to +1.08 log units; no-emblem seals -0.60); the closers add almost nothing. Effects vs jar (seq_raw): W226/M60 +0.22 [-0.07, +0.52], W520/M211 +0.03 [-0.08, +0.13], W617 +0.03 [-0.21, +0.26], W156/M15 -0.02, W151/M12 -0.08 [-0.26, +0.09], W527/M254 -0.10 [-0.30, +0.10], W154 -0.14 [-0.37, +0.09]; every CI covers 0. Omnibus variance 0.011 vs null median 0.007, 95% 0.020 (P 0.26). **Rank agreement between splits is at or below chance:** MD vs Harappa rho -0.07 (P 0.56; e.g. W527 is lowest at MD -0.18, highest at Harappa +0.27), unicorn vs other -0.11 (P 0.58), square vs non-square -0.30 (P 0.69), random halves -0.14 (P 0.62). Raw medians: jar 645 mm2 (n 492), arrow 671 (108), W226 759 (11), W151 595 (34), W527 524 (26), W156 477 (29), W617 459 (18), W154 389 (20). Same at seq_strong / seq_all (see loop35_c1_seq_strong.txt, loop35_c1_seq_all.txt) | **Null for ordinality.** Once text length, emblem and find-area are held, no closer costs more face than another, and whatever order the point estimates suggest does not reproduce across cities, emblems, shapes or random halves. The only closer that stands apart in raw size, W154 (small), is the bar-seal closer 806-154 (15 of 21 home seals have no emblem, 14 of 21 are cuboid-convex or rectangular): the format rule of S157, not a grade. The nominal 'top' closer W226/M60 rests on 11 Mohenjo-daro seals and 0 at Harappa. The closers are not a size-graded hierarchy |
| S-DARK-37.1 | **Multi-sided objects, arrow A (one text across the sides). 827 objects with >= 2 inscribed faces in inscriptions.csv (id n.k = recorded face), 766 after collapsing identical moulded / impressed face-sets (S-DARK-13): Harappa tablets 621, Mohenjo-daro 104 (12 with >= 3 faces: 6 TAB:B prisms, 4 SEAL:S, 2 tags), seals 37, sealings 28. (A1) is the opener on recorded face 1 and the closer on the last face? null = face labels permuted within object 1,000x. (A2) how many faces per object are closer-final / opener-initial? null = faces reassigned among objects of the same site x type (W700 count faces not counted as closed). (A3) concatenate the faces in recorded order and parse (S310 parser): share with an internal closer or non-initial opener, vs random same-site-same-type faces concatenated (model C) and single-face texts of the same length (model A). (A4) slot-rank continuity at each boundary vs face order permuted. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop37_c1.py`, `data/derived/dark/loop37_c1_<level>.txt`)** | (A1) opener on face 1: 39/79 = 0.49 vs 0.49 null (P = 0.48; Harappa tablets 0.52, seals 2/6, MD prisms 1/3); closer on the last face 169/323 = 0.52 vs 0.49 (P = 0.17; complete faces 0.56, P = 0.038; MD two-faced 25/36 = 0.69, P = 0.017). (A2) objects with >= 2 closed faces: 39 vs 107 expected (P < 0.001), but entirely Harappa tablets (13 vs 79; without count faces 11 vs 29); Mohenjo-daro prisms 4 vs 4.1 (P = 0.68), MD two-faced 9 vs 9.7, seals 4 vs 4.4, sealings 11 vs 10.8, other sites 11 vs 11.2; >= 2 opener faces 6 vs 5.7 everywhere at chance. Of 582 Harappa one-closed pairs the open face is a count face in 247, a single sign in 44, a text ending in W700 / W824 / W690 / W868 / W861 in most of the rest. (A3) frame violations in the recorded-order concatenation 0.58 vs 0.60 for random faces (P_lo = 0.11) vs 0.19 for real single texts of the same length; MD prisms 0.83 vs 0.81 vs 0.37; Harappa 0.60 vs 0.60 vs 0.17. (A4) 0.43 vs 0.41 (P = 0.07). Three levels within 0.01 | **Arrow A dead.** The sides are not one text: a multi-sided object concatenated in recorded order is indistinguishable from random faces glued together and far from a single long text, and openers fall on any face at chance; the only inter-side constraint is Harappa's two-field voucher (one closed text + one count or short label, S93/S94), which Mohenjo-daro prisms, seals and sealings do not show (their faces are independent, model C). Not MD-prism-specific: the null holds on 621 Harappa tablets, 12 MD prisms, 37 seals and 28 sealings alike |
| S-DARK-36.3 | **Out-of-corpus test set, part 3: blind scoring against the frozen predictions (loop36_predictions.txt, 16 predictions).** STRICT set = A/B-grade signs (6 texts, 18 tokens; 4 texts >= 3 signs), LENIENT = all read signs (13 texts, 51 tokens). Nulls: within-text shuffle 500x, home bigram model length-matched 500x (S-DARK-21.1), home resampling at matched lengths 5,000x; home base rates for comparison; zero counts as < 3/n (`tools/dark_loop36_score.py`) | Testable with n > 0: P4 jar final 1/1, comb final 1/1 (consistent); P8 chain order 3:0 strict, 5:1 lenient (P = 0.15/0.17); P14 unseen signs 0/18 (< 0.17) coded, but 4 of 36 tier A/B sign forms (11%) had no Wells counterpart; new texts 9/9 (S309: 95%). **FAILS: P6 repetition, 2 of 4 strict texts repeat a sign (Met 1-503-861-1, Cleveland 617-90-617) = 7x the bigram expectation (P = 0.026) and P = 0.002 vs home texts of the same lengths (home 2.7-4.8%, corpus museum 'Unknown' texts 12.5%); P10 reuse, 0 of 12 texts carry a 3-sign run attested at home vs 5.8 expected (P = 0.002; held-out sites 40%, S349).** Untestable (no tokens): P2, P3, P5, P7, P9, P11, P12, P13, P15, P16 | **The first out-of-corpus set is too small and too unprovenanced to test the grammar: 0 passes, 2 fails, 3 consistent-underpowered, 9 untestable.** The two failures sit in the museum pieces and are what either a 25%-per-sign misreading or non-genuine objects would produce; the repeated-sign failure is the one a misreading does not easily explain (the repeated signs are the clearest on their seals). This is not evidence against GRAMMAR.md, which rests on excavated material; it is a warning that unprovenanced museum seals read from photographs are a different population (the corpus's own 24 'Unknown' texts already repeat signs at 12.5% vs 4.8% at home). Needed: CISI 3.1 concordance for the Met/Cleveland pieces, BM photos of Gadd 1932 nos. 3 and 15, and the Manjul & Manjul 2021 Binjor plate. |
| S-DARK-35.2 | **Cost table of the closers across independent cost measures.** Same home seals and mixed model as 35.1, outcome in turn: log face area (n 1,193), log thickness (746 seals with thickness mm), material rank (clay/terracotta 0 < faience/paste 1 < steatite 2 < hard stone/metal/shell/ivory 3; 1,136; material covariate dropped), unicorn vs other emblem (1,267; emblem covariate dropped), boss present (842 with a boss record), used (text found on a TAG/TAB anywhere, S-DARK-16.3; 1,277). Closer effects vs jar; Kendall W of the eight closers' rankings across the six measures and across the three physical ones (area, thickness, material), against closer labels permuted within site x length bin (1,000x, every measure refit). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | seq_raw table (effect vs jar; area, thickness, material, unicorn, boss, used): W226/M60 +0.22, +0.07, +0.01, +0.18, +0.01, -0.06; W617 +0.03, +0.00, +0.03, -0.13, +0.04, +0.03; W520/M211 +0.03, +0.06, +0.01, +0.03, 0.00, +0.02; W156/M15 -0.02, +0.03, +0.03, +0.02, -0.04, **+0.18 [+0.10, +0.27]**; W527/M254 -0.10, -0.02, +0.02, -0.03, +0.02, -0.05; W151/M12 -0.08, -0.01, -0.01, +0.13, 0.00, **-0.18 [-0.26, -0.10]**; W154 -0.14, -0.05, -0.08, **-0.39**, +0.06, -0.11. Only three cells have a CI clear of 0: W156 used more (the Harappa tablet text 3-156, S-DARK-16.5), W151 used less (S292), W154 less often with a unicorn (bar seals). Kendall W over six measures 0.32 (null median 0.16, 95% 0.34, P 0.063); over the three physical measures 0.73 (null 0.40, 95% 0.67, **P 0.030**) - but area and thickness rank the closers almost identically (rho +0.93) because they are two dimensions of one object, and material rank is carried by one terracotta W154 seal; the pairwise rank correlations of area with material, unicorn, boss and used are +0.05, -0.02, -0.21, +0.38. Material by closer (home): every paradigm closer is 94-100% steatite; faience/clay seals carry the jar (10 of 471) or no closer (8 of 403), never W156/527/617/226 (0 of 28-10, upper bounds < 0.11-0.30). Levels: see loop35_c2_seq_strong.txt / seq_all.txt | **Null to marginal; no common order.** The six cost measures do not agree on a ranking beyond what two measurements of the same seal body guarantee (area and thickness), and the one marginal W rests on them plus one odd seal. Nothing beats a 0.05 null after counting the two W tests. Usage separates W156 (copied onto tablets) and W151 (not), which is the known tablet/seal genre split, not cost. Material is nearly constant (steatite) in the closer-bearing population, so a material ladder cannot be read off this corpus; the cheap-material seals are jar or closer-less texts. Verdict: the closers do not form a hierarchy visible in cost |
| S-DARK-38.1 | **Loop 38, cycle 1: the space budget, glyph widths vs face width.** Each Wells sign rendered from the lipi font (U+E000 + W number, as S164) and its ink bounding box measured; width expressed in units of a reference sign height (median bbox height of the 100 commonest signs; `data/derived/dark/loop38_glyph_widths.json`). Proportion check against the CISI photo of M-6 (data/images/cisi-M-6a.png). A text's 'ink width' = sum of its sign widths. Complete single-line seals with H and V (`horizontal(mm)`, `vertical(mm)` in inscriptions.csv), no unread sign, L >= 2, all signs in the font: 1,367 (home MD+HP 1,124; other sites 243). Nested regression of log H on log n (sign count) vs log n + log ink, 10-fold CV R2 (10 repeats); fit on home, scored on the other sites; control = sign widths shuffled among signs 1,000x and the whole regression refitted. Three merge levels (`tools/dark_loop38.py 1`) | Font widths (w_rel): median 0.52, IQR 0.42-0.65; jar W740 0.67, plain fish W220 0.42, arrow W520 0.51, tall-2 W2 0.23, openers 0.61-0.74, man W90 0.45, widest common W125 0.85. Photo M-6: the font draws the four signs 15-40% narrower than the cut glyphs (820: 0.64 vs 0.87; 880: 0.76 vs 0.83; 65: 0.29 vs 0.48; 244: 0.99 vs 1.26) but keeps their rank; text band = top 24% of the face, sign height 0.21 of V, 4 signs spanning 85% of H. rho(n, ink) = 0.92, so the two predictors are nearly the same quantity. CV R2 of log H: n only 0.194, ink only 0.193-0.195, both 0.201-0.202; gain +0.008 (shuffle null median -0.001, 95th +0.004, max +0.021; P = 0.013-0.020 at the three levels). Held-out sites: R2 0.075 (n) vs 0.074-0.077 (n + ink), gain -0.001 to +0.002, P = 0.16-0.51. Emblem seals: if signs stood 1/3 of V tall, the font ink of 32% of texts would overrun H; at 1/4, 11%; at 1/5, 3%; the implied line-height fraction H/(ink x V) falls steeply with n (rho -0.91) | **Null beyond sign count, at all three levels.** Knowing which signs a text contains (and hence how wide they are) adds under one per cent of variance to what the bare sign count already gives, the gain is significant only at home and vanishes on the held-out sites. Faces were not sized to the ink of the text; rho(H, n) = 0.44 is all there is (S-DARK-5.1's length effect). Font-proxy caveat: the lipi glyphs are systematically narrower than cut signs (by about a quarter on M-6) but preserve the rank order of widths, which is all a width-shuffle test uses; a photo-measured width table for the 60 commonest signs would be the next step if any width effect were ever found |
| S-DARK-38.2 | **Loop 38, cycle 2: density. Does the scribe squeeze (signs per mm rise with length) or was the object sized to the text (density flat)?** Elasticity b = OLS slope of log H on log n (and on log ink), bootstrap 95% CI, per object class: seals (home, MD, Harappa, other sites, emblem, script-only), moulded tablets TAB:B, incised TAB:I, copper TAB:C, sealings TAG; same filters as 38.1 (single line, complete, H known, L >= 2). b = 1 means the face grows with the text, b = 0 a fixed face into which the text is squeezed. Also rho(n, H/V) on square/rectangular seals (do long texts get wider-shaped faces). Three merge levels (`tools/dark_loop38.py 2`) | Seals, home (1,173): b = 0.27 [0.24, 0.31] for n and 0.28 for ink; Mohenjo-daro 0.24, Harappa 0.37, other sites 0.22 [0.15, 0.29]; emblem seals 0.22, script-only 0.45 [0.38, 0.52]. Signs per mm rise with length everywhere: rho(n, signs/mm) 0.71-0.83, ink/mm 0.55-0.74; by length bin the median home-seal face goes 22.0 / 24.1 / 25.4 / 26.7 / 29.0 / 35.6 mm for 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 6-7 / 8+ signs while ink per mm rises 2.3x (0.057 -> 0.134); incised tablets go 13.7 -> 17.8 mm from 2 to 5 signs with ink/mm 0.086 -> 0.146. Tablets: TAB:B moulded b = 0.43 [0.38, 0.48], TAB:I incised 0.27 [0.22, 0.31] (ink 0.17), TAB:C 0.27; TAG sealings 0.08 [-0.19, 0.29] (n = 44). Shape: rho(n, H/V) = -0.003 on 1,227 square/rectangular seals; b(log V ~ log n) = 0.22 = b(log H ~ log n) = 0.26; only 11% of faces are wider than tall by 10% or more. Identical at seq_raw / strong / all | **Squeezed, not sized to the text.** A seal 3 x as long in signs is only 1.3 x wider; the extra text is absorbed by smaller signs (M-6's 0.21 V sign height for 4 signs is the upper end), not by a bigger or wider face. The elasticity is the same for made-to-order seals and for moulded tablets (0.27-0.43), so 'ordered to fit' does not describe either medium; long texts simply get a somewhat larger blank. Script-only seals respond more (0.45) because they lack the emblem that fixes the face. A fixed sign height would predict b = 1; nothing comes near it. Descriptive, transcription-independent (sizes are object facts) |
| S-DARK-38.3 | **Loop 38, cycle 3: are closer-less texts truncations forced by the face?** 1,426 complete single-line seals with H, no unread sign, L >= 2; closer = last sign in the S289 set (jar, arrow, W156/527/617/226/390/405/154/158/15/254/12), variant B adds a final opener (S286): 500-508 closer-less vs 918-947 closer-bearing. Space-driven omission predicts closer-less texts (i) denser (font ink / H), (ii) on smaller faces (H, log H x V), (iii) ending in a normally non-final sign; label permuted within site x length (1,000x). (iii) is tautological between the groups (a closer-less text cannot end in a closer), so it is tested inside the closer-less set: rho(crowding, leave-one-out final rate of the last sign), crowding permuted within site x length; plus the cosine of the last-sign distribution against the pre-closer sign of closer-bearing texts. Direct test of 'ran out of room': the 62 wrapped (two-segment '/') seals vs 1,476 single-line, closer rate and H matched on site x length; segment order is unknown (S-DARK-8.2), so a closer at the end of either segment counts. Wide signs on narrow faces: 69-72 signs with >= 20 texts, rho(font width, mean residual log H of the texts carrying the sign | n, site), widths shuffled 1,000x. Three levels (`tools/dark_loop38.py 3`) | (i) Closer-less texts are not denser: ink/H 0.093 vs 0.103, diff -0.009 against a matched null of -0.007 (P = 0.09-0.18); not on smaller faces: H 27.1 vs 27.2 mm, diff -0.1 to -0.5 mm vs null -0.7 (P = 0.08-0.75); log area P = 0.12-0.70; closer-less share on the most crowded quarter of faces 0.30-0.31 vs 0.35-0.37 elsewhere (P = 0.57-0.88). (iii) Inside the closer-less set the crowded faces end in MORE final-like signs: rho = +0.23 to +0.26 vs null 95% [+0.02, +0.19], one-sided P = 1.00 for the truncation direction; last sign final rate 0.54-0.57 on the crowded quarter vs 0.45-0.47 elsewhere. The closer-less endings (W90 x50, W400 x49, W151 x45, W407, W368, W820) look nothing like the pre-closer signs of closer-bearing texts (W760, W33, W100, W220, W3, W32; cosine 0.14, vs 0.37-0.38 for pre-closer against ordinary middle signs). Wrapped seals: in the stored order only 16% end in a closer (P = 0.001), but counting either segment end the rate is 0.645 vs 0.636-0.641 for single-line seals of the same site and length (P = 0.63-0.67); 40 of 62 have a closer at a segment end; wrapped faces are 30.1 mm vs 27.1 (matched diff +3.0 mm inside the null [+2.2, +5.7]). Wide signs: rho = +0.03 to +0.04, null [-0.24, +0.24], P = 0.36-0.39; fish 0.42-0.45 wide sit on faces +0.01 to +0.05 log-mm above expectation, the jar (0.67) -0.01, the man W90 (0.45) -0.02 to -0.03. Identical at seq_raw / strong / all | **Missing closers are not truncations: a category difference, at all three levels.** None of the three space predictions holds, and the one that moves goes the wrong way (crowded closer-less texts end in slot-final signs, W90 / W400 / W151, which S289/S-DARK-35 already treat as alternative endings). When a scribe did run out of room he wrapped (62 seals, about 4% of complete seals) and kept the closer at the ordinary rate, which is the direct observation the truncation story needs and does not get. No sign, wide or narrow, is avoided on narrow faces. Transcription-robust (sizes are object facts; the closer label follows the Wells text) |
| S-DARK-38.4 | **Loop 38, cycle 4: the outside prediction. Squeezing predicts narrow allographs at the ends of crowded texts and a last sign whose width shrinks with the ink already spent; planning predicts neither.** (a) The 27 allograph pairs of sign_allographs_levels.json with both glyphs in the font; the narrower member (font width) is 'narrow'; 785 tokens on single-line seals with H over the 10 pairs whose members differ by >= 0.05 (817/861, 405/390, 242/240, 706/705, 158/154, ...). Narrow share at the text end vs elsewhere, and crowding (ink of the other signs / H) for narrow vs wide tokens; variant labels shuffled within pair 1,000x. seq_raw only by construction (the merged levels collapse the pairs). (b) 1,190 seals with L >= 3 and H: partial Spearman of first-sign width vs last-sign width given n, H and middle ink; and rho(ink before the last sign / H, last-sign width); null = widths shuffled among signs 1,000x (keeps the frame, destroys width meaning). Three levels (`tools/dark_loop38.py 4`) | (a) Narrow variants are not commoner at the end: 0.63 vs 0.60, diff +0.03 (null [0.00, +0.11], P = 0.32); the only per-pair end effects are the known slot facts (W817 final 22% vs 46% for W861; W158 final 8% vs W154 50%). Crowding is LOWER for narrow variants, 0.085 vs 0.090 (P = 0.002), the reverse of squeezing, carried by 242/240 and 706/705, i.e. transcription variants on roomy faces; on final tokens alone diff -0.014, P = 0.25. (b) partial rho(first width, last width) = -0.03 (null [-0.07, +0.06], P = 0.41-0.43); rho(prior ink / H, last-sign width) = +0.00 (null [-0.16, +0.13], P = 0.84-0.94). Last-sign width is a class fact only: jar 0.67, 'other' closers 0.62-0.64, no closer 0.56, arrow 0.51. Same at seq_raw / strong / all | **Planning wins both arrows, with the font caveat.** The width of what has already been cut does not predict the width or the variant of the final sign, and narrow allographs do not gather on crowded faces or at text ends; the one 'significant' crowding result points the wrong way and rests on two probable-level variant pairs. Together with 38.2 the picture is: the whole text was laid out at one sign height chosen for the face (smaller for longer texts), not written at a fixed size and compressed at the end. Caveat: the font gives rank widths only; allograph 'narrowness' is a drawing fact of the lipi font, not of the seals, and only 10 pairs have enough tokens |
| S-DARK-37.2 | **Multi-sided objects, arrow B (sides as paraphrases). For the 914 face pairs of the 766 collapsed multi-sided objects: any shared sign, Jaccard, shared middle sign (S310 parser), identical text, same middle, grade swap (same middle, different closer), abbreviation (shorter face a subsequence of the longer, >= 2 signs), same closer with different middle, normalised edit distance; null = face 2 replaced by a face of another object of the same site x type x length (500x); subsets without count faces, Mohenjo-daro, seals, sealings, Harappa tablets. Plus (2b) do whole face-SETS recur on other objects more than if face texts were assigned independently within site x type (1,000x), on hand-incised Harappa TAB:I, moulded TAB:B and Mohenjo-daro separately. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop37_c2.py`, `tools/dark_loop37_c2b.py`; `data/derived/dark/loop37_c2_<level>.txt`, `loop37_c2b.txt`)** | Grade swaps 1 of 914 vs 0.5 expected (the one is a count variant, 32-226-400 / 32-700); same middle 28 vs 19.6 but 27 of them are identical texts repeated on two sides (26 vs 7.9 without count faces; Mohenjo-daro 16 vs 1.0); abbreviation pairs 12 vs 5.6 (P = 0.008; without count faces 11 vs 1.6; seals 5 of 74 pairs vs 0.2: 817-2-5-390 / 817-2-5, 861-741 / 861-741-240-630-740, 575-407-746 / 575-407, 798-17-575-740-679 / 575-740-679, 575-31 / 575-31-2-3-390); any shared sign 0.150 vs 0.197 and edit distance 0.932 vs 0.908, i.e. faces are LESS alike than random same-length faces; same closer with different middle 21 vs 78. The shared-middle excess (0.123 vs 0.076) is two stock series (hand-incised '3-156 | 3-861 | 34-700' x20; moulds '413-575-335-711-33-700 | 240-705-33-550-525-400'). (2b) whole face-sets recur on 125 of 342 hand-incised Harappa tablets vs 56 expected (P < 0.001; 35 sets), 79 of 326 moulded vs 35, 27 of 104 Mohenjo-daro objects vs 0.4 (231-17-585-95-520-32-407 | 777 x4; 323-690 | 550-60-690-740 x3; 5-706 | 233-585-407 x3). All three levels agree | **B dead as paraphrase: no synonym or grade-swap pairs; the sides never restate one message with another closer.** Two side-effects survive: (i) 12 abbreviation pairs, concentrated on two-sided seals (a legend and its shortened form, opener kept), a small lexical lead worth a photo check; (ii) the face-set is a stock unit even when hand-incised, so the sides are not independent ledger entries (against pure C) but fixed fields of one object type, the S93 voucher being the commonest. Harappa + Mohenjo-daro only; MD prisms 60 pairs, no abbreviation, 7 identical |
| S-DARK-31.1 | **Does the sign space contain analogical offsets (a - b = d - c) beyond what the frame predicts?** Embeddings PPMI + SVD (dim 30) over L1/R1/L2/R2 neighbours, position class, S310 slot label and object class; Mohenjo-daro + Harappa complete texts >= 2 signs (2,840 texts, 11,407 tokens), vocabulary = 150 commonest signs. Analogies mined with the 4-corner rule (all four 3CosAdd predictions hold, a/b and c/d not near-synonyms cos < 0.5, offset must beat plain similarity, offsets agree cos >= 0.5) and the weaker 2-corner rule; offset class = greedy star of linked pairs with >= 3 mutually disjoint pairs and mean offset cos >= 0.5; 'sharp' = mean cos >= 0.7, 'big' = >= 5 disjoint pairs. Nulls, each re-embedded and re-mined: (i) tokens shuffled within S310 slot label (frame kept, pairing destroyed), 100x at seq_raw, 50x at seq_strong/seq_all; (ii) slot-grammar synthetic corpus (slot skeleton kept; opener/marker/suffix/count from slot unigrams, title conditional on closer, middle an order-1 chain), 100x / 50x / 50x; (iii) shuffle within position class, 50x. Calibration on the same pipeline with null (iii), 50x: Ur III seal legends (syllables, 4,400 texts), Linear B lines as words+ideograms and as syllabic signs, Proto-Elamite lines, and the Indus with position-only labels. P floors at 1/(n+1) = 0.010 (100x) or 0.020 (50x). Robustness (loop31_c1_robust.txt): absolute class counts depend on embedding dimension (864 at dim 20, 130 at dim 50, 459 at dim 30) so only real-vs-null ratios are read. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | **Real, rule 4: 459 / 465 / 480 offset classes holding 1,503 / 1,545 / 1,572 pairs; sharp 91 / 72 / 80; big 22 / 26 / 32; sharp AND big 0 / 0 / 0.** (i) Within-slot shuffle, rule 4: classes null median 376 / 374 / 370 (95% 407 / 414 / 399), ratio 1.22 / 1.24 / 1.30, P = 0.010 / 0.020 / 0.020; sharp null 38 / 36 / 34 (95% 50 / 46 / 47), ratio 2.4 / 2.0 / 2.4, P = 0.010 / 0.020 / 0.020; big null 14 / 14 / 13 (95% 19 / 19 / 22), P = 0.020 / 0.020 / 0.020; sharp+big null median 0 (95% 1), real 0. Links themselves are NOT in excess (4,733 vs 4,572, P = 0.17; seq_strong P = 0.04). Rule 2: classes ratio 1.79 / 1.66 / 1.66 (P <= 0.02), sharp 2.6 / 1.9 / 1.8x (P <= 0.02), sharp+big real 0 vs null median 3 (deficit). (ii) **Slot-grammar synthetic corpus reproduces most of the excess**: rule 4 classes null median 424 / 432 / 422 (95% 470 / 462 / 469), ratio 1.08 / 1.08 / 1.14, P = 0.149 / 0.059 / 0.039; sharp null 53 / 54 / 54 (95% 74 / 77 / 67), ratio 1.7 / 1.3 / 1.5, P = 0.020 / 0.137 / 0.020; big null 16 / 16 / 16 (95% 24 / 26 / 23), P = 0.129 / 0.098 / 0.020. Rule 2: classes ratio 1.25 / 1.15 / 1.14 (P <= 0.02), sharp 2.5 / 1.6 / 1.6x (P = 0.01 / 0.08 / 0.02). (iii) Position shuffle, rule 4: classes ratio 1.12 / 1.12 / 1.18 (P = 0.039 / 0.020 / 0.020), sharp 4.5 / 3.2 / 3.7x (P = 0.020), big 1.1 / 1.1 / 1.5x (P = 0.29 / 0.24 / 0.04). Calibration, rule 4 classes ratio and sharp ratio vs position shuffle: Ur III syllables 1.51x and 10.5x (sharp+big 0); Linear B syllabic signs 1.83x and 4.3x (0); Linear B words+ideograms 0.51x classes (fewer, because 2,675 types and the 150-sign vocabulary is mostly ideograms and numerals) but 4.8x sharp and 2 sharp+big classes (the only corpus with sharp+big classes under rule 4; 25 under rule 2); Proto-Elamite 1.20x and 2.0x (0); Indus position-only 1.10x and 4.5x (0). Rule 2 sharp ratios: Ur III 41x, Linear B words 36x, Indus 15-32x, Proto-Elamite 5.8x, Linear B syllables 4.9x (null medians of 2-12 make these ratios unstable). | **Weakly positive and almost fully explained by the frame.** The Indus space has more and sharper offset classes than a within-slot shuffle (about 1.2x classes, 2x sharp, real above every one of 50-100 replicates), so the pairing of signs across slots carries some structure; but a slot grammar with an order-1 middle chain gets within 8-14% of the real class count (P = 0.04-0.15) and within 1.3-1.7x of the sharp count, so the excess is mostly what frame slots plus adjacent-sign dependencies produce, not a lexicon of analogies. No class is both sharp and large at any level or rule (null expects 0-3), and the class members (e.g. class 1: W440:W347, W585:W33, W388:W171, W636:W167 ...) are cross-slot pairs that share a direction only because they move from one slot cluster to another; cycle 2 (S-DARK-31.2) confirmed the blind classes carry known relations at base rate. On the calibration ladder the Indus sits with Proto-Elamite (1.1-1.3x classes, 2-4.5x sharp) and below Ur III syllables (1.5x, 10x) and Linear B syllables (1.8x); the only corpus whose geometry yields sharp+big classes is Linear B at the word level, i.e. a known language read in words. Robust to merge level. Caveats: P floors at 0.01-0.02; absolute counts and even ratios move with dimension (ratio 1.1 at dim 20, 2.3 at dim 50 for one shuffle), so the verdict rests on direction of effect across three nulls and three levels, not on a single number; MD+H only. |
| S-DARK-35.3 | **Qualifier statistics vs the cost order.** For each of the eight paradigm closers (all complete seal texts, all sites, n 15-631): mean title-phrase length (signs to the left that belong to the closer's 60%-mass qualifier set, S303/S310 rule), number of distinct left partners, type/token ratio, mean rarity (-log frequency) of the left partner, share of texts that are the bare closer unit, mean text length, share of the top partner. Spearman rho of each statistic against the 35.2 mean cost rank and against the 35.1 area effect, exact two-sided P over all 8! = 40,320 closer orders. Kendall W of the six cost measures together with the seven qualifier statistics (oriented so + = 'higher grade'), null = exact permutation of the closer labels on the qualifier block (cost-block agreement held fixed, so only cost-vs-qualifier agreement is tested). seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | No qualifier statistic tracks cost. seq_raw: rho with mean cost rank: title_len -0.19 (P 0.67), qual_types -0.24 (0.58), TTR -0.10 (0.84), rarity -0.19 (0.67), bare +0.26 (0.54), text_len -0.10 (0.84), top_share -0.12 (0.79); with the area effect: -0.12 to -0.38, all P >= 0.36. The qualifier statistics disagree even among themselves (W 0.21 over seven rankings); W of cost + qualifier 0.114 vs exact null median 0.128, 95% 0.209 (P 0.62); mean cost rank + qualifiers 0.163 vs null 0.176 (P 0.64). seq_strong: W 0.112 (P 0.64), seq_all 0.121 (P 0.60); every single rho P >= 0.50. The table itself is informative: the 'top' cost closer W226/M60 has the shortest title phrase (0.67) and a common partner set; W740 jar has 122-130 partner types but a mean title length 0.83-0.85 like the rest; W156/M15 is bare in 41% of its texts (the 3-156 Harappa unit), W154 in 0-3% (always after 806/803) | **Null.** The S303 qualifier sets are real (each closer has its own partners) but they carry no order that lines up with seal cost, and they do not line up with each other either: closer units differ in kind (bar-seal unit 806-154, tablet unit 3-156, pincers-box 550-527), not in grade. Any ranking of the closers by their qualifiers is at chance against the cost ranking at all three merge levels |
| S-DARK-35.4 | **Out-of-sample test of the closer order.** Held-out sites (everything but Mohenjo-daro and Harappa; complete seal texts with h and v: 267 seals, Lothal 57, Dholavira 56, Chanhu-daro 42, Kalibangan 38, Banawali 14, Nausharo 13, nine smaller sites), same mixed model (closer + length bin + site + material + emblem, random site x area-section), closers with >= 3 held-out seals (jar, arrow, W151, W156, W527, W154; W617 1, W226 2 so untestable). Spearman of the held-out area effects with the home area effects and with the home mean cost rank vs labels permuted within site x length (1,000x); thickness, unicorn, boss and used refit on the held-out set; per-site medians. IM77 (data/im77/im77_corpus_lines.csv) has no size field, stated. Prediction from the hierarchy reading: the top-ranked closer should almost never sit on clay/terracotta or faience/paste seals; counted with upper bound < 3/n. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | Held-out area effects vs jar: W154 +0.13 [-0.38, +0.63], arrow 0.00, W527 -0.08, W151 -0.14, **W156 -0.46 [-0.88, -0.05]** (the one CI clear of 0; 6 seals, 3 of them Dholavira/Lothal small square seals). Spearman with home area effects -0.14 (null median -0.14, 95% +0.66, P 0.53); with home mean cost rank -0.31 (P 0.69). The order flips between held-out sites: Dholavira W156 > jar > W151 > arrow > W154; Lothal W154 > W527 > jar > W156 > W151 > arrow; Kalibangan arrow > W151 > jar; Chanhu-daro jar > arrow > W151. Held-out unicorn/boss/used effects: rho with home cost rank +0.43, -0.49, +0.29 (seq_raw); +0.26, -0.49, +0.14 (strong/all); only W151 used-less survives (-0.18 [-0.34, -0.03], the S292 seal-only closer). IM77 carries no dimensions, so size cannot be replicated on an independently read object list. Prediction: W226/M60 (nominal top) 0/12 on clay/terracotta and 0/12 on faience (< 0.25 each: no power); the nominal bottom W154 1/19-23 clay; jar 15/542 cheap, 'none' 16/403; all complete seals 36/1,360 cheap (2.6%); the 36 cheap seals carry jar 15, none 16, W527 2, W154/W520/W151 1 each; 15 are Mohenjo-daro, 7 Lothal, 6 Harappa, 6 Kalibangan. Cheap material is 2.8% of jar seals and 2.6% of all seals: the cheap seals carry closers in proportion to frequency | **Null, and the prediction is untestable at the sample size.** No closer order from the home cities reappears at the held-out sites; the only held-out effect, W156 small, is the opposite sign to its home effect (-0.02) and sits on six seals. The cheapest material carries the jar and closer-less texts at the base rate, and the top-ranked closer is too rare (12 seals) to say anything (0/12 is an upper bound of 0.25). Steatite is 96-100% of every closer class at every site, so material is not a dimension along which the closers vary at all |
| S-DARK-39.1 | **Form generator, part 1: fit and Turing test.** FORM = ordered fields built from the fit set: signs clustered into mutually exclusive menus (co-occurrence <= 0.33 of independence, mean relative position within 0.12), merged to 25 fields, then 4 rounds of coordinate descent on violated before/after and co-occurrence counts plus insertion re-ordering of the fields; every text parsed into units (a new unit starts where the field index falls back); unit = subset of fields, one sign each, in field order. Generator: field subset ~ pseudo-likelihood Ising model (25 x 25 couplings, L2 1.0; covariates intercept, seal/tablet, site, second-unit flag); element per field from the site x type table shrunk to pooled (lambda 5); Good-Turing new-sign mass per field (S309); 1-3 units per text (per type); site-local whole-text CRP reuse with theta calibrated to the distinct-text share (S366). 100 synthetic corpora of the real size and site x type mix, the identical 50-statistic battery of S366. Controls: independent fields (no Ising couplings); fields written in a random order (refitted form); ablations without second units and without whole-text reuse; false-alarm floor = form refitted to its own output. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | **Form: 25 fields; 85% of all sign-pair orders agree with the field order; 2.6-2.9% of texts repeat a field inside a unit; 67-68% of fit texts are single-unit valid instances, 24-25% need 2 units, 7% need 3.** FORM: outside 25/50 (seq_all), 27/50 (seq_raw, seq_strong), |z|>3 25-26, Sigma|z| 256-265 (S366 chain M*: 23/50, 16, 134; battery floor 19/50, 141; own floor 2-4/50, 44-60). Controls worse, as required: independent fields 29-33/50, 282-294; shuffled field order 35-37/50, 420-430 (seal_jar_final z 77, H_end1 z -32); no second units 31-32/50, 343-349 (repeat_rate 0, len_sd 1.26 vs 2.02, attraction pairs 14 vs 127); no reuse 34-35/50, 408-417. Key statistics under FORM: long-range attraction 127-141 vs 87-93 (z 2.0-2.6, inside the range at all levels; M* 61, z 5.6), avoidance 263-272 vs 181-187 (z 5.7-7.0, still outside; M* 170), repeat rate 0.043 vs 0.101-0.109 (z -8, worse than M* 0.092), len_sd 2.02 vs 2.14-2.15 (z -2.7 to -3.0; M* 2.33 z -5.9), two-numeral share 0.044 vs 0.060-0.063 (z -3.3 to -3.7; M* -4.4), H_start1 inside (z -1.4 to -1.7), H_start3 inside (z 0.0-0.8), H_start2 z -2.8 to -3.5. Largest new gaps: H_cond_bigram 2.77 vs 3.50 (z -28), mi_d1 3.18 vs 2.36 (z 23), run3_multisite 0.076 vs 0.027 (z 21), nesting 0.163 vs 0.062 (z 17), same_middle_diff_closer 0.20 vs 0.05 (z 15), closer_sign_nonfinal 0.08 vs 0.20 (z -14). Field couplings (Ising w): F01(opener 861/820/140)-F03(connective 2/60) +2.2, F17-F18 +1.8, F12-F13 +1.8; repulsions F14-F21 -2.6, F14-F16 -2.2, F06-F08 -1.9, F08 (numerals 32/3) against F13/F16/F12/F10/F09 -1.4 to -1.8. Same picture at all three levels (outside 25-27, Sigma|z| within 9). | **Partly supported, not better than the chain.** A filled form with field co-selection reproduces the long-range attraction (inside the range at every level, the chain's worst residual) and brings the tight length spread and the start entropies close, and both controls fail as a form hypothesis predicts (independent fields +25-30 Sigma|z|; random field order +165). But choosing the elements of the fields independently throws away the adjacent-sign dependence (conditional bigram entropy 0.7 bits too high, MI d1 0.8 bits too low) and makes repeats twice too common, so the form as fitted here is worse than S366's chain on the battery as a whole (Sigma|z| 256-265 vs 134). The attraction result says the long-range pairs are field co-selections; the bigram result says the elements are not chosen field by field independently. Cycle 2 adds element dependence across fields. Held-out numbers of this cycle discarded (covariate bug). |
| S-DARK-40.1 | **Loop 40, cycle 1: are long texts concatenations of attested complete short texts? Decomposition census.** `tools/dark_loop40.py 1 LEVEL` (logs `loop40_cycle1_{seq_raw,seq_strong,seq_all}.txt`, cuts `loop40_c1_cuts_*.json`). 3,415 complete direction-recorded texts (merged-corpus-canonical, older build, S-DARK-23), 1,168 of >= 5 signs. Unit inventory = every complete text attested on another object (2,083-2,144 distinct). A long text 'decomposes' if it splits into 2-3 contiguous units all attested, each unit >= 2 signs (minlen 2) or >= 1 (minlen 1). Matching exact, S262 variants, and M-space via bridge_extended + S-DARK-27 proposals. Nulls: within-slot shuffle of the NAME signs (200x), within-text shuffle (200x), 10 synthetic corpora each from the S366 best model, the S366 M0 slot grammar and an order-2 chain (same inventory of REAL units). Random-concatenation control: 5,000 joins of two random attested 2-4-sign texts, drawn by object count; where does the join fall? All three merge levels | **3.7% (43/1,168 seq_raw), 5.3% (62, seq_strong), 5.4% (63, seq_all) of long texts decompose at minlen 2; 7.4% / 10.4% / 12.1% at minlen 1. S262 variants add 0, M-space adds 1 (64).** Above the slot-shuffle null (median 16 / 23 / 23, ratio 2.7-2.8, P = 0.005) and far above the text shuffle (median 1). But: (a) the share FALLS with length: 5 signs 7.1-9.6%, 6 signs 2.1-3.9%, 7 signs 0.4-1.8%, 8+ signs 1.1% (2/186), the reverse of a concatenation process, which has more cut points in longer texts; (b) the order-2 chain generates the same or more decompositions (MD+H minlen 2: chain 0.051 [0.039-0.061] seq_raw, 0.074 [0.065-0.087] seq_all vs real 0.039 / 0.055; the S366 slot models generate 0.011-0.017); (c) the random-concatenation control puts the join at a frame boundary (closer/suffix before, or opener after) in 70-71% of joins (68% of short texts end in a closer or suffix), whereas the real cuts fall at a frame boundary in only 8/43 = 19% (seq_raw) and 8/61-62 = 13% (strong/all); the joined text itself is attested 4/5,000 times; (d) 24 of 35 (raw), 41 of 52 (strong), 42 of 53 (all) non-boundary cuts are after W2, i.e. the 'first unit' is the opener phrase 817/861-2, which happens to stand alone as a complete 2-sign text on 7 objects, followed by one ordinary complete text (861-2 + 415-100-740, 861-2 + 590-390-740 ...). Units restricted to <= 4-sign texts: 40 / 55 / 58. By site: MD 22-34 of 671, Harappa 17-21 of 320 (Harappa holds 6 of the 8 closer-boundary cuts: two-faced tablets with a 33-700 / 34-700 count face, S-DARK-12.2); held-out sites with MD+H units 3/165 (1.8%, raw) to 8/165 (4.8%, strong/all). Seals 2.9-5.0%, tablets 5.8-6.5% | **Not concatenation. Long texts are not stacks of complete short texts: the 'decomposable' 4-5% are almost all opener-phrase + one text, the cut is at a closer boundary 4-5x less often than real joining would produce, the share drops with length, and an order-2 sign chain already generates as many. The excess over the slot shuffle is the phrase reuse of S-DARK-21.2 (P2: opener phrases, closer units), not a text-level operation. Transcription-robust across merge levels; not Mohenjo-daro alone (Harappa and held-out sites give the same shares, and Harappa carries the few closer-boundary cases) |
| S-DARK-40.2 | **Loop 40, cycle 2: where is the cut, and is there a total order of units?** `tools/dark_loop40.py 2 LEVEL` (logs `loop40_cycle2_*.txt`, fixed edges `loop40_c2_order_*.json`). For every 2-way decomposition of cycle 1 (exact matching, minlen 2 and 1): kind of boundary at the cut (closer/suffix before, opener after, none) vs a random cut position in the same texts (200x). Units classed by their ending as in S289 (OPENER-unit, JAR, JAR+suf, ARROW, C700, C527, C617, C154 ..., COUNT = numeral + counted good, PLAIN); precedence matrix A-before-B : B-before-A over consecutive units, one-sided binomial, BH at 0.05 over pairs with n >= 5 (the S-DARK-19 order statistics), cycle check and longest chain of fixed edges; MD+H fixed order applied to held-out-site decompositions; identity-level order constancy of unit pairs seen >= 2 times; most-used units compared with the S-DARK-21.2 P2 reused runs. All three levels | **Cut at a frame boundary 0.19 (raw) / 0.13 (strong, all) at minlen 2, 0.21-0.27 at minlen 1, vs random cut in the same texts 0.07-0.09 [0.02-0.17]: above a random position but far below the 0.70 of true joining (cycle 1 control).** Sign before a non-boundary cut: W2 (24/35, 41/52, 42/53), then W60, W690, W368; so the cut is after the opener phrase, never inside the closing slot. Unit classes: OPENER-unit only in position 0 (29 / 48 / 49 of 43 / 62 / 63 texts); JAR in position 1 (26 / 37 / 37). Texts with >= 2 closer-bearing units ('true stacks'): 4 of 1,168 at minlen 2 (strong, all), 10 at minlen 1; opener phrase + one text 29 / 48 / 49. Fixed class pairs at minlen 2: OPENER-unit -> JAR 21:0 / 33:0 / 33:0 (P < 1e-6); OPENER-unit -> ARROW 5:0 (strong, all); PLAIN -> JAR free (4:4, 5:4). At minlen 1 (single-sign units allowed): 3 / 5 / 7 fixed edges of 4 / 6 / 7 tested (OPENER -> JAR 28-58:0, OPENER -> PLAIN 11-39:1, PLAIN -> C700 11:1, PLAIN -> ARROW 9-12:0, PLAIN -> JAR 25:18 '?' to 44:20 fixed only at seq_all); no cycle; longest chain 3 classes = opener < middle < closer, i.e. the S289/S310 frame. Held-out sites: 3-23 conforming, 0 violating; held-out fixed edges 2/2 also fixed on MD+H (minlen 1). Identity level: unit pairs seen >= 2 times always in the same order 3/3, 7/7, 7/7 (minlen 2), 0.86-0.89 (minlen 1). Most-used units: 817/861-2 (18 / 37 / 38 uses, 7 copies), 820-2 (6 uses, 1 copy), 415-100-740 (4-6), 90-400 (4), 590-390-740 (3-4), 240-904-740 (3), 33-700 (3, 117 copies), 34-700 (2, 103 copies): the same opener phrases and closer units that S-DARK-21.2 found reused (P2) in the IM77-only texts | **The 'order of units' is the frame order (opener before everything, closer unit last) and nothing more: with the opener phrase removed, PLAIN vs JAR is free (4:4, 5:4), and only 4-10 texts in 1,168 contain two closed units at all. No evidence of a unit grammar above the sign/slot grammar; the precedence reduces to S289 slots. Not Mohenjo-daro alone (held-out 0 violations) |

The parts of a long text were not separate seals kept in the same room or house. Rests on Harappa for the one positive and on Mohenjo-daro for the zero; Mohenjo-daro block provenance covers only 15-29 hosts, so the zero is an upper bound of about 20%, not a proof |
| S-DARK-40.4 | **Loop 40, cycle 4: replication on the 324 IM77-only texts (S-DARK-21 / loop27 sets, M numbers).** `tools/dark_loop40.py 4 LEVEL` (logs `loop40_cycle4_*.txt`). What recurs inside them is the P2 phrase inventory of S-DARK-21.2 (opener phrases, arrow and jar closer units, the 33/34-700 count unit), joined to signs that were never a text of their own. Upper bounds only: the inventory is the Wells corpus (S309: ~95% of new texts are new), so a true stack whose parts were never found would be invisible; the cycle 1 controls (length trend, boundary rate, chain null) carry the negative, this cycle confirms it does not rest on Wells's own transcription or on Mohenjo-daro alone |
| S-DARK-42.1 | **Grammar as an authenticity scorer, part 1: rule set and calibration.** Eight rules, every parameter fitted on the FIT set only = Mohenjo-daro + Harappa texts with an excavation register number (4,377 texts, 2,416 with >= 3 signs; data/raw/inscriptions.csv, 5,680 rows, texts read first-read-first): R1 non-adjacent repeat of a sign not among the 9 signs that repeat in >= 3 fit texts (S88, S-DARK-15); R2 both members of a mutual-exclusion pair (S360 cut: both >= 25 texts, E >= 4, O <= 0.1E; 113-124 pairs); R3 W2 twice, W60 twice, W2 text-initial, W2 with a marked jar (S-DARK-15.2); R4 adjacent bigram expected >= 8 times and never written (S-DARK-33.3; 31-36 bigrams); R5 sign pair written in the direction never used (>= 10 co-occurrences, 0 reversals; S-DARK-19; 209-226 pairs); R6 complete text ending in a never-final sign or starting with a never-initial sign (>= 20 tokens; S289 closer tendency); R7 frozen pair (S229 list + pairs adjacent in >= 90% of >= 10 co-occurring texts; 74-75) present but not adjacent in order; R8 fish qualifiers out of the S296 order. Score = rules broken. Calibration: 5-fold CV on the fit set; held-out excavated sites with register number (591 texts, 313 with >= 3 signs); power on within-text shuffles, on an order-2 KN chain fitted to the fit set (the S366 'skilled forger'), an order-1 chain and a unigram sign-copier, all length-matched. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | **False alarm (>= 1 rule, texts >= 3 signs): CV 0.129-0.131, held-out sites 0.214-0.217; by length CV 2: 0.03, 3: 0.07, 4: 0.09, 5: 0.13, 6+: 0.11; held-out 3: 0.20, 4: 0.18, 5: 0.19-0.23, 6+: 0.09. At >= 2 rules: CV 0.001-0.03 by length, held-out 0.004-0.04.** Held-out excess over CV is R3 (W2 placement, 19 of 67 flags; Lothal 22/124, Kalibangan 17/55, Chanhu-daro 13/57 flagged) and R7 frozen pairs, i.e. regional conventions, not misreadings. Power (>= 1 rule, >= 3 signs): within-text shuffles 0.71 (CV) / 0.68 (held-out); order-2 chain 0.37-0.40; order-1 chain 0.44; unigram copier 0.64-0.67. At >= 2 rules: shuffles 0.22-0.56 by length vs forger 0.01-0.12. Rules that catch the order-2 forger are R2 exclusion, R6 edge, R7 frozen and R1 repeat (a chain forgets what it has written); R4 forbidden bigrams almost never (5 of 2,955), because the chain learns them. Same on all three levels (rule counts move by < 10%) | **The scorer is calibrated but weak against a skilled forger.** A text that breaks one rule is 2.5-3x more likely to be a chain-generated fake than a genuine excavated text (0.39 vs 0.13), and 5x for shuffles; a text that breaks two rules is 4-10x more likely. But 60% of chain forgeries pass, so the scorer can rank and flag, not certify. The held-out false alarm of 0.21 means any object from outside MD+H must be judged against the 0.21 rate, not 0.13; the W2-placement rule in particular is a Mohenjo-daro/Harappa convention. Any single flag on a short text (<= 5 signs) is within the false-alarm band; only >= 2 rules or a repeat (R1 false alarm 0.016) carries weight |

No-context texts at MD+H themselves: 0.043 vs 0.064 (n = 69, P = 0.72). The 24 'Unknown' museum texts break a rule 3 times (1-740 bigram on a round seal 467-550-1-740-740; frozen 927-740 split on Un-100; W2 with marked jar 741 on 741-298-2-136), each a rule with false alarm 0.003-0.045, and 0 repeats (the 12.5% repeat rate of S-DARK-36 is adjacent doubles 33-33 / 740-740 and the repeatable sign 32, which the rule exempts). The one unprecedented score (Ns-66, a no-context pot graffito, 4 rules) is more plausibly two texts run together or a pot register effect (S-DARK-23) than a forgery. | S-DARK-39.3 | **Reading the form; new texts as form instances.** From the PARTIAL form of cycle 2 (25 fields, MD+H fit): field probabilities, obligatory fields (P >= 0.9), forbidden field pairs (O <= 0.2E, E >= 5), attracting pairs (O >= 3E), Ising couplings |w| >= 1, pair orders licensed by the fit set (an order of two fields counts as licensed if written >= 2 times and >= 20% of that pair's co-writings), distinct field subsets and how many cover 90% of texts (all texts; seals). Three levels | **Specification: 25 fields, none obligatory (most frequent F22 = closer menu {740, 520, 97, ...} P = 0.43; F21 title/tree menu {700, 390, 407, 100, 760} 0.29; F03 connective {2, 60} 0.19; F01 opener {861, 820, 140} 0.18; 21 fields at P 0.06-0.16). Texts use 1 field 16%, 2 28%, 3 17%, 4 15%, 5 11%, >= 6 12%; a field is used twice in 6.6-7.1% of texts (5.1-5.8% two menu signs, 2.6-2.8% the same sign). Forbidden field pairs: 0 (seq_raw 0, seq_strong 0, seq_all 0); attracting pairs 0-4 (opener-connective 861/820 + 2/60; 255/904/850 + 840/142/435 4.7x; fish 240 + 705 / 220 at seq_strong); Ising |w| >= 1: 12 couplings, all between the frame menus (opener + connective +2.1, 705-menu + 33-menu +1.8, 255-menu + 840-menu +2.0; closer menus repel each other -1.1 to -1.2). Order: 85% of co-written field pairs follow the linear field order; 410-424 of 600 ordered pairs licensed, 97-112 of 300 unordered pairs free in both directions (the numeral menu F08 {32, 3} is order-free with 12 fields), 174-190 fixed one way, 0-1 never co-written. Distinct field subsets 1,836-1,907 over 4,411 texts; **1,395-1,466 subsets are needed to cover 90% of texts (seals: 1,118-1,172 of 1,776, 1,102-1,179 singletons)**; the commonest subset (two fields, numeral/count + title) covers 3.6%. Validity as a form instance: fit set 0.74 vs shuffle 0.26-0.27 / permutation 0.27-0.29; held-out sites 0.56-0.60 vs 0.27-0.29 / 0.29-0.31; IM77-new 0.46-0.48 vs 0.19-0.20 / 0.22-0.23, seals 0.35-0.38 vs 0.12-0.14 / 0.18-0.20; with IM77's adjacent doubles merged 0.58-0.60 vs 0.26 / 0.25-0.26, seals 0.52-0.54 vs 0.22-0.25 / 0.23-0.24. Failures of IM77-new: repeated field 102-108 (of which 79 texts repeat the sign in the M transcription itself, 66 as adjacent doubles; IM77 overall has repeats in 10% of texts vs 3% in Wells), unlicensed pair order 65-66, forbidden co-selection 0. **Controls: a Markov-2 clone of the fit set is just as valid (0.75-0.77 vs 0.27) and the model's own output less so (0.70-0.72); order-model AUC genuine vs shuffle: fit 0.81-0.82, held-out 0.70, IM77-new 0.71, Markov-2 clone 0.80.** Same at all three levels (within 0.04) | **The form can be written down but it is not a checklist.** Twenty-five menus, none compulsory, no forbidden combination at all, one weak attraction, and a field order that is linear for 85% of pairs with the numerals free: that is GRAMMAR.md's frame (opener-connective, closer menus that exclude each other, suffix) plus an open middle, not a code with required fields. It needs ~1,400 distinct field subsets to cover 90% of the texts, so the message set is not a short list of forms (cf. S-DARK-33.4). The validity test does separate genuine new texts from their shuffles (IM77-new 0.58 vs 0.26 after merging doubles; held-out 0.58 vs 0.28), and the failures outside the fit set are mostly a transcription convention (IM77 doubles) and the free order of numerals, so the form generalises; but a Markov-2 clone passes the same test as well as the real texts and the order AUC is 0.70-0.71 on new material (S-DARK-33.2 gave 0.72-0.83 for automata), so being a valid form instance is a property any chain-like corpus with this frame has, not evidence for field-wise composition. The 'two signs of one menu in one text' cases (5-6% in MD+H, 11-15% at held-out sites) are the texts the form cannot represent; they double at the small sites, which is where the next test of the frame should look. Transcription-robust (Wells + IM77), merge-robust. |

| S-DARK-44.1 | **Heads as Proto-Elamite commodity classes, part 1: does the head (closer-slot sign, S289 paradigm + W595/W700) predict the numeral SERIES (short vs tall, S204), the numeral VALUE and the counted ITEM in the same text?** Mohenjo-daro + Harappa complete texts whose last sign (after the W400/W90 suffix) is one of 12 heads and which carry a counted numeral (short W3-7/12-20/25-29, tall W32-39; W1, W2, tall-1 W31 excluded as markers, S234; W55/56 excluded as named numbers, S273): 757 texts, 806 numeral tokens. Three numeral sets: ALL; ADJ = numeral directly before the head (499 tokens: the head is itself the counted/qualified sign); FAR = count elsewhere in the text (307); GOODS = FAR counts of trees W390/405/407, bracket W900, plain fish W220 (88). Statistics: MI (bits) and Cramer's V of head x series, head x value (3-7, 8+), head x item (items < 5 tokens pooled); null = heads permuted among texts within site x object type x length class, 1,000x; cells reported by permutation z. Controls: 20 planted corpora with heads re-assigned independently of the numerals (must give uniform P); Proto-Elamite (6,166 entries, final sign x numeral system capacity/count/other, heads shuffled within tablet x entry length) through the same code must recover the known class -> number-system link. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop44.py 1`) | **ALL numerals: head x series MI 0.293 bits (null 0.092, 95% 0.105, P < 0.001), V 0.60 (null 0.34); head x value MI 0.31 (null 0.20, P < 0.001); head x item MI 0.71 (null 0.53, P < 0.001). But the whole effect is the numeral DIRECTLY BEFORE THE HEAD: ADJ MI 0.378 (null 0.041), V 0.86; cells: tall n + W700 339/343 (voucher), tall 3 + arrow W520 39/39, short 3 + carrier W156 35/38 (38/38 short), tall 2 + W226 7/7, tall 2 + jar 45/62. FAR counts (same texts, count not touching the head): head x series MI 0.032 = null 0.032 (95% 0.053; P = 0.44 / 0.36 / 0.36 by level), V 0.19 = null; head x value P = 0.34 / 0.23 / 0.23; seals only P = 0.52 and 0.44; heads other than jar and W700 P = 0.80 and 0.96. GOODS: series P = 0.87, value P = 0.45, item P = 0.011 (one cell: jar x bracket W900 20 vs 13.9, z 3.0; arrow x W900 0 vs 3.4). FAR head x item is significant (MI 0.45 vs 0.30, P < 0.001) but every driving cell is a known closer qualifier, not a commodity: W617 x pincers W550 5 vs 0.5 (z 7), box W527 x W550 2 vs 0.1, W617 x W597 8 vs 3.3, W154 x W877 2 vs 0.3 (the 550-527, 32-877 phrases of S289/S303). Planted control: P values median 0.68 / 0.34 / 0.66, share < 0.05 = 0.00 / 0.05 / 0.00. Proto-Elamite positive control: MI 0.249 vs within-tablet null 0.142 (95% 0.148; global null 0.028), V 0.44 vs 0.33, P < 0.001; cells M297 x capacity 184 vs 50 (z +22), M263 x count 179 vs 143, M376 x fractions 59 vs 7, M288 mixed (0.34) - the FINDINGS.md classes come back. Identical at seq_strong and seq_all (W158 merges into W154) | **Not Proto-Elamite classes.** The PE code sees the PE link at once; run on the Indus it finds the head x numeral link only where the numeral is the head's own fixed qualifier (33-520, 3-156, 32-226, tall n + 700, 32-740: the frozen pairs of S197/S216/S303, already in GRAMMAR.md). A count standing elsewhere in the same text has the same series and the same value distribution whatever the head (MI at the null, three levels, seals alone, non-jar heads alone), and the counted good (tree, bracket, fish) does not choose the head beyond one jar x bracket cell at P = 0.01. The item effect that survives is the closer-qualifier structure (pincers with the box and W617), i.e. frame, not commodity. So far the heads behave like Ur III titles with fixed epithets, not like capacity/count determinatives. Rests on MD + Harappa (cycle 2 replicates) |
| S-DARK-44.2 | **Heads as Proto-Elamite classes, part 2: replication.** Same tests and null as 44.1 on (a) Wells texts from sites other than Mohenjo-daro and Harappa (59 texts with a head and a count, 66 tokens: Dholavira 11, Kalibangan 11, Lothal 9, Chanhu-daro 6, others 22; 47 seals); (b) all IM77 texts via the bridge (654 texts, 699 tokens; tall M87-96, short M102-120, markers M86/97-100 and M121 excluded; heads M342/211/12/15/254/60/328/252 + proposed M245/M66); (c) the 210 IM77-only texts never transcribed by Wells (S-DARK-21; 24 texts with head and count). (b) IM77: ALL series MI 0.263 vs null 0.211 (P < 0.001; cells M211 x tall 50 vs 39, M60 x short 0 vs 3.9, M328 tall 251/253); FAR series MI 0.029 = null 0.030 (P = 0.49), V 0.187 = null; FAR item P < 0.001 but again the qualifier cells (M254 box x pincers M216 4 vs 0.3, M245 x M216 4 vs 0.8, M328 x M97); GOODS series P = 0.52, value P = 0.03, item P = 0.02 (jar x bracket M287 18 vs 12.8, the same single cell as Wells). One IM77-only effect: FAR head x value MI 0.168 vs null 0.119 (P = 0.005), still P = 0.004 with fixed-term items removed; cells are short 6 avoiding the jar (4 vs 10.5) and going with the arrow (6 vs 2.2) and M245 (3 vs 0.5), i.e. 3-6 tokens, and Wells has no such effect (P = 0.21-0.34 with and without fixed terms). (c) IM77-only texts: ALL series P = 0.30, FAR series P = 0.78, value P = 0.34 (13 tokens). Series x head with the fixed-term items removed: MD+H P = 0.94, IM77 P = 0.93. Same at all three levels (identical numbers; the merges touch no head but W154/158) | **Holds up: the head predicts only the numeral it is written with (its own frozen qualifier); it predicts nothing about a count elsewhere in the text, at held-out sites, in the IM77 transcription or on the texts Wells never saw.** The single IM77 value effect (short 6 avoids the jar) is transcription-specific and rests on cells of 3-6 tokens; treat as noise unless it returns in new material. Transcription-robust (Wells + IM77 are 70% the same objects) and replicated on the small held-out sets (59 and 24 texts), so the negative is also an upper bound: a head-dependent series shift of the PE size (V 0.44) would have been seen at n = 40 |
| S-DARK-44.3 | **Heads as Proto-Elamite classes, part 3: the measure-class table and the reverse prediction.** (a) Head -> series table on MD + Harappa (class TALL / SHORT if >= 80% of its numeral tokens are one series), separately for all numerals and for counts away from the head (FAR). (b) Reverse prediction: predict the head from the numeral's series, counted item, series + item, series + value (most frequent head per feature in training); 5-fold within MD + H, then trained on MD + H and scored on the held-out Wells sites (66 / 40 tokens) and, series only, on IM77 (699 / 301 tokens) and the IM77-only texts (27 / 13); baselines = training modal head and the test set's own modal head. (b) ALL numerals: series + item predicts the head at 0.72 in 5-fold (modal 0.43) because the adjacent numeral names the head; on the held-out sites 0.42 (series + value 0.48) against the held-out own modal (jar) 0.55: **no gain out of sample**; IM77 series -> head 0.57 vs modal 0.44 (same frozen pairs), IM77-only 0.19 vs 0.41. Identical at the three levels | **No measure-class table exists beyond GRAMMAR.md's frozen pairs.** In Proto-Elamite the class sign fixes the number system of every quantity entered under it; in the Indus the head fixes only the numeral that is part of its own name, and the independent counts in the same text (trees, fish, bracket; short 3-9 or tall 2-3) are distributed the same under every head. The reverse test confirms it: once the frozen pairs are set aside, series + item predict the head no better than always saying 'jar', in-sample and out. | S-DARK-46.1 | **Hands (a): do carvers co-select allograph forms?** For every pair of merged signs with >= 2 variant forms of >= 20 tokens (S268 classes at strong / all / ext levels; ext adds the doubtful 151/156 because S-DARK-24 shows both readers draw it as two signs) that share >= 20 texts, the 2x2 table of (non-head form of A, non-head form of B) in the texts that carry both; G = sum N x MI; nulls = variant labels permuted among texts within site x type (city dialect, N1), site x type x period (N2), site x type x area-section (N3), 1,000x each; per-pair log-odds vs own null. Within-text: 17 of 20 double-token texts repeat the same form (15.3 expected). Planted hands: +/-0.4 detected 30/30 (z +11), +/-0.3 25/30 (z +5.4), +/-0.2 9/30 (z +1.0) | **No hands beyond the city dialect at the strength the data can see.** A carver who favoured one form of the tree would, if hands existed with a 70/30 vs 30/70 split, have been caught 25 times in 30; the corpus gives z +0.2 to +0.9. Variant co-selection is a city habit (S-DARK-14.2), not a personal one, at least for the seven testable sign pairs. Caveats: only 230-270 texts carry two variant signs (power stops at about a 60/40 vs 40/60 hand); five of the seven testable distinctions are Wells-only (Mahadevan draws one sign), so the robust test rests on one pair; the 405+806 package on four Mohenjo-daro seals is a lead for cycle 3 (imports or a travelling carver), not a hand |
| S-DARK-47.1 | **Re-read the loop 36 museum seals from the best open-access images.** Cleveland 1973.161, 1964.104, 1973.160 from the CC0 full TIFFs (2.5-4.1k px; seal ~1,000 px wide, 20x the loop 36 working size), Met 49.40.1 from the 2,003 px DP23101 (seal and modern impression side by side; the 3,783 px 143690 is the reverse with the boss), Met 49.40.2-4 from the only images (338-389 px), Sindh 'MM-2831' from the 1,170 px banner (seal 250 px). Confidence A/B/C per sign; contested repeat pairs judged identical / shape-similar (S-DARK-24 pair named) / damaged. Control: the loop 36 readings of the same photographs | **Three of the four flagged readings change; the repeats split two ways.** (1) Cleveland 1973.160 (zebu): signs 1 and 3 are each a single 3 x 8 grid of raised squares, identical in form and size, separated by the person W90; loop 36 coded each as the doubled grid W617 (= Wells' two grids side by side). Reading 615-90-615-793-400 (B-A-B-B-B); sign 4 is the bracketed leaf W793, not W838 + brackets; sign 5 a 7-tooth comb. The repeat is REAL: identical glyphs, not damage, not a confusion pair. (2) Met 49.40.1: 1-503-861-1 (B-A-A-C); the two strokes are the same height; the end stroke's top is in a chip on the seal face with a faint groove up-left (= a branch W64/65 or a crack). Identical or damaged; cannot be decided from a 2,003 px photograph. (3) Sindh seal: 371-435-520-64-435 becomes ?-902-515-435-62 (all C): the two 'W435' are three different hooks (an inner-loop hook, an angular '4'/Z, a lambda), i.e. the repeat was a misreading of shape-similar hooks. (4) Cleveland 1964.104: 645-700-33-790-? becomes 690-706-33-832-? (C-C-B-C): the 'two-dot cup' W700 is a U with ONE hanging stroke (W706/703), the 'oval' is a pointed oval with an inner dash (W832); the loop 42 exclusion 700 x 790 rested on two shape-pair misreadings (S-DARK-24: U-family and oval-family neighbours). Cleveland 1973.161: ?-435-923-415-690-740 becomes 1-902-927-415-685-740 (C-C-B-B-B-B): double S-curve W927 (not the chevron W923), six-armed asterisk W685 (not the ticked X W690), hook W902 (not W435); the 'Y curl' of loop 36 is the horn tip beside a stroke. Met 49.40.3/.4 unchanged in content (49.40.4 order corrected to 690-441-1-741). Mirror re-reading agreed with the direct reading on 31/31 sign families (one order change on 49.40.4). Sign-level change rate vs loop 36: 12 of 31 tier-B signs re-identified (39%), all within shape families, consistent with the ~75% per-sign accuracy claimed in S-DARK-36 | **The repetition failure of S-DARK-42.3 is half misreading, half real.** Sindh and Cleveland 1964.104 were shape-pair misreadings and no longer repeat or exclude; Cleveland 1973.160 is a genuine identical-glyph repeat (grid .. person .. grid) and Met 49.40.1 a possible one (stroke .. stroke) hanging on a chipped sign. The loop 36 photographs were too small for the hook family (W435/902/61) and for the U/oval families; readings from < 400 px images should be graded C throughout. Per-sign accuracy from small photographs is ~60-75%, as S-DARK-36 said |
| S-DARK-47.2 | **Re-score loop47_newfinds_v2.csv with the loop 42 rules (R1-R8, fitted on the 4,377 MD+H register texts) at seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all, strict (A/B signs) and lenient (all read signs), unread positions splitting adjacency rules as in S-DARK-42.3; then the copy hypothesis: sign-level edit distance (either orientation, '?' = wildcard) from each text to its nearest of the 5,530 corpus texts, against the leave-one-out nearest-neighbour distribution of 300 excavated MD+H seals of the same length. Sindh, Met 49.40.3, 49.40.4, 49.40.2: score 0 on every level. Binjor 90-32-93 flags R1 only on seq_all where 93 merges into 90 (merge-dependent). Copy test: nearest corpus text at d = 2 (1964.104, 49.40.1, 49.40.3), d = 3 (1973.161, 1973.160, 49.40.4, Sindh); excavated seals of the same length have median NN d = 1-3 and P(d_exc <= d) = 0.64-1.00, i.e. every museum text is at least as far from the published corpus as a typical excavated seal, none within edit-1 of any Marshall/Mackay/Vats text (the material a 1949-73 copyist could have seen).
