The 'copy of a published seal' hypothesis fails for every object (no text within edit distance 1 of the corpus, NN distances at the excavated median or beyond). Same on all three levels except the Binjor merge artefact |
| S-DARK-47.3 | **Provenance and verdict.** Museum records read through the Cleveland Open Access API (provenance, citations, exhibitions fields) and the Met Collection API (creditLine, objectDate, tags), the Smithsonian NMAA dealer biography of William H. Wolff (asia.si.edu, 2024), and harappa.com's 2026 Met visit (Playwright Chromium under xvfb; curl is blocked). CISI 3.1 (collections outside India and Pakistan) itself is not open access and could not be checked. Verdict categories: misread / genuine exception / probable modern piece / undecidable, from S-DARK-47.1 (glyph identity), 47.2 (rule score, copy test) and the record | **Cleveland 1973.160 and 1973.161: bought 1973 from William H. Wolff Inc., New York (J. H. Wade Fund), no earlier owner; Wolff (1906-1991) bought on biannual trips to Pakistan and India through local middlemen 'who secured artworks from archaeological sites' (NMAA biography) and the Smithsonian lists no authenticity dispute. Published: CMA Bulletin 61 (1974) nos. 193-194 p. 79, Handbook 1978 p. 286 and 1991 p. 3, A Cleveland Bestiary 1981 p. 31 (160), Masterworks of Asian Art 1998 pp. 106-107. Cleveland 1964.104: from the Museum of Fine Arts Boston by exchange 1964 (so in Boston before 1964; MFA source not given), shown in 'Year in Review 1964'; no citations. Met 49.40.1-4: Dodge Fund purchase 1949, no dealer or prior owner in the record, no references; 49.40.2 is the only one on view (gallery 234). Sindh 'MM-2831': Mohenjo-daro museum display piece, no accession number given, almost certainly an excavated object already in CISI/Wells under another reading (S-DARK-36). Verdicts. (1) Sindh seal: MISREAD; the two 'W435' are different hooks; score 0; nothing to decide. (2) Cleveland 1964.104: MISREAD (700/790 were shape-pair errors); the residual lenient flag (33 x 690, both C) is at the false-alarm rate; genuine as far as the script can say. (3) Cleveland 1973.161: passes strict; the lenient frozen-pair split is a C-grade sign and the same split occurs on a corpus museum seal; genuine as far as the script can say. (4) Met 49.40.1: UNDECIDABLE between genuine exception and misreading of a damaged sign: if the two strokes are W31 the text is unexceptional (W31 repeats in 5 corpus texts and stands at both ends of two excavated Mohenjo-daro texts); if W1, it is a 1-in-3,463 pattern; the end stroke's top is chipped and may carry a branch. The 1949 purchase predates the main forgery market and the text copies nothing published; the modern impression is the museum's. (5) Cleveland 1973.160: GENUINE EXCEPTION or PROBABLE MODERN PIECE, not separable by the script alone: the identical double grid is real (not damage, not a confusion pair), unattested in Wells (0 of 3,463) but attested once in IM77 (Mohenjo-daro 1004, M245 twice), and the rest of the text (615 with 400, person W90, bracketed leaf W793, comb final) is well-formed; the copy test rules out a copy of any published seal (NN d = 3, P = 0.99), which is the usual mode of 1970s forgeries; the seal was published in 1974 and 1998 by the museum and never challenged. Weight of evidence: genuine exception (one rule, false alarm 1%, with an IM77 precedent) over modern piece. (6) Met 49.40.2-4, Cleveland 1964.104, Sindh: pass. What a human must do: ask the Cleveland Museum of Art (Indian and Southeast Asian Art dept.) for a silicone cast or RTI/photogrammetry of 1973.160 and 1973.161 and for the Wolff invoice/correspondence (the museum archive holds dealer files), and ask the Met (Ancient West Asian Art) for RTI of 49.40.1's left end sign and the 1949 Dodge Fund purchase file (vendor); check CISI 3.1 for Cleveland and Met entries, which would give Parpola's reading of the same signs | **None of the seven museum seals is a demonstrable modern piece.** Two of the four loop 42 failures were misreadings from small photographs, one (Met) hangs on the W1/W31 stroke convention and a chipped sign, and one (Cleveland 1973.160) is a genuine identical-glyph repeat whose best explanation is a rare licensed repetition with a Mohenjo-daro precedent rather than forgery. The loop 42 group signal (P = 0.002-0.003) was a reading artefact plus one real outlier; the corrected group sits at the false-alarm rate. Corrected table: loop47_newfinds_v2.csv (two new columns: loop36_reading, mirror_reread_agreement) |
| S-DARK-44.4 | **Heads as Proto-Elamite classes, part 4: the quantity seals and an out-of-sample prediction.** (a) Description of the MD + Harappa seals that carry a count not touching the head (the 'quantity seals'): heads, opener rate, counted items x series, values, item -> head. (b) Frozen prediction from the class reading: a head that takes one series at home (>= 80% of >= 10 tokens) never takes the other series at a held-out site; tested with the numeral touching the head (ADJ, where the home link is 39/39 arrow, 38/38 carrier, 339/343 W700, 51/62 jar), with all numerals, and with FAR counts, on the Wells held-out sites and on the 210 IM77-only texts; violations reported with the texts, upper bound < 3/n when none. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop44.py 4`) | (a) 190 quantity seals: head = jar 148 (78%), arrow 17, W154 7, W617 7, W151 5, box 4; opener present on 51 (27%). Counted items: plain fish W220 51 (tall 2 x33, short x18), bracket W900 19 (all short), W585 12 (short 7, a title), W840 7, W140 6, W590 6 (tall), W877 6 (tall 2); values short 3 x44, 7 x20, 5 x9, 4-6 x12; tall 2 x78, 3 x40, 4-5 x3. Item -> head: fish -> jar 37 / arrow 11 / W151 2; bracket -> jar 18 / W154 1; W585, W840, W140, W760, W590, W575 -> jar only; pincers W550 -> W617 3 / box 2. So the 'quantity seal' is [opener] + count + good (fish / bracket / tree) + [title] + jar, and the good does not pick the head except through the arrow phrase (fish + arrow, S299) and the pincer-box phrase. (b) **The prediction fails at the rate S-DARK-21.1 found for the minimum-lot rule.** ADJ: Wells held-out 3 violations of 18 (7 + jar at Allahdino Ad-4 66-741-235-495-17-740 and short 6 + jar at Chanhu-daro C-3 317-920-2-16-740 - both the '7/6 + jar' term also seen at home 11/62 - and **two-tier short 3 + arrow at Miri Qalat MQ-12 (13-520) against 39/39 tall at home**); IM77-only 3 of 11 (tall 2 + M15 x2, where M15 merges W154/156/158 and W154 takes tall at home; 7 + jar 2695). ALL numerals: 3 of 17 and 4 of 12. Also W226: tall 2 x7 of 7 at home, short 3 / 4 x3 of 3 at the held-out sites (below the n = 10 training cut). FAR: no one-series head exists to test (0 of 0). Same at the three levels | **The heads do not behave like Proto-Elamite commodity classes.** Their only numeral link is the fixed numeral inside the title term, and even that leaks at 10-30% on held-out material (arrow with a short 3 at Miri Qalat, W226 with short 3/4 three times). The quantity seals read as a credential plus a consignment line - count + good in the middle, title + jar at the end - in which the head is the issuing office, not the unit of measure; the counted good never selects the head beyond the two closer phrases already in GRAMMAR.md. Only 84 of the 200 dossier entries carry a description: 49 pictorial, 35 geometric (12 of them stroke numerals). **AUC(PICT_SHAPE, pictorial > geometric) = 0.84; 0.76 on non-numeral signs (49 vs 23).** Components: orientation spread 0.84, skeleton segments 0.78, asymmetry 0.76, holes 0.73, far-from-strokes 0.67, perimetric complexity 0.53. Disagreements are the simple pictures (tree W390 -1.35, pitchfork W415, arrow W520, plain fish W220 all below the median) and hatched geometric figures (W503, W460, W565 above). PICT_SHAPE vs log frequency (465 non-numeral signs >= 2 tokens): Spearman -0.15 (S164 abbreviation effect, so frequency must be matched in every slot test) | **The two measures agree moderately (AUC 0.84) and the shape index is partly a complexity index**: a plain tree or fish scores as 'abstract', a hatched triangle as 'pictorial'. The label proxy covers only 84 signs. Both are usable, neither is a clean pictoriality meter; results in parts 2-4 are read against perimetric complexity alone and against the curvature-only index to separate 'more picture' from 'more complex'. |
| S-DARK-50.2 | **Pictoriality by slot, part 2: slot role vs shape, frequency controlled.** Roles per token from the S310/S331 frame parser (as dark_loop26): OPENER, MARKER, CLOSER (= head), TITLE (closer-selected adjacent qualifier, S303), NAME_ADJ (other sign directly before the closer block), NAME_FAR (non-adjacent middle), COUNT, SUFFIX; each sign with >= 5 tokens takes its modal role (>= 50%, else MIXED). Statistic = mean PICT_SHAPE / PICT_CURV / dossier label / perimetric complexity per role; null = roles permuted among signs inside log2-frequency bins, 1,000x; also OLS with log frequency and a token-weighted version. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all (`tools/dark_loop50_c2.py LEVEL`; loop50_cycle2_*.txt, loop50_roles_*.json) | 3,155 complete direction-recorded texts; 239 / 221 / 218 signs with >= 5 tokens (221 / 205 / 202 non-numeral): NAME_FAR 140 / 129 / 128, NAME_ADJ 28, CLOSER 11 / 10 / 10, TITLE 9, OPENER 5, MARKER 1, SUFFIX 2. **Heads: PICT_SHAPE +0.32 vs null -0.15 [-0.42, +0.16], P = 0.004 / 0.016 / 0.022; head - far middle +0.47, P = 0.002 / 0.011 / 0.015.** But perimetric complexity alone gives the same picture (heads 18.6 vs 13.5, P = 0.006 / 0.004 / 0.007; head - far middle +5.2, P = 0.003 / 0.003 / 0.001), and the curvature-only index does not (heads P = 0.18 / 0.48 / 0.53; head - far middle +0.25, P = 0.18 / 0.48 / 0.52). Dossier label: heads 6/6 pictorial vs far middle 0.69, P = 0.28 (72 labelled signs). Openers, markers, suffixes: at or below the far-middle value on every measure (frame slots minus far middle -0.23, P = 0.77). NAME_ADJ slightly more complex than NAME_FAR (perim +3.4, P = 0.02; PICT_CURV P = 0.50). OLS with log frequency: CLOSER +0.53 (t 3.0) on PICT_SHAPE, +0.25 (t 1.0) on PICT_CURV, +6.2 (t 3.1) on complexity. Token-weighted means: head -0.24 = far middle -0.23. The head effect rests on W617 (index +2.6, 30 enclosed regions): without it, head vs far middle P = 0.14 (part 3) | **No pictorial / abstract split by slot.** The one significant contrast, heads above the middle, is a complexity contrast (perimetric complexity reproduces it, curvature and the hand labels do not) carried largely by one hatched sign, and the openers, markers and suffixes that a cross-language code should make most recognisable are the least picture-like signs in the system. The middle is not more abstract than the frame once frequency is matched. Same at all three merge levels. |
| S-DARK-50.3 | **Pictoriality by slot, part 3: calibration on scripts with known sign classes**, same 5-part shape index (holes, orientation spread, asymmetry, perimetric complexity, skeleton segments; z within each script) and curvature-only index. Linear B (Noto Sans Linear B, 205 glyphs): ideograms (commodity / animal / object signs, the slot analogue of Indus heads) vs syllabograms; DAMOS frequencies for 121 signs (syllable values via Unicode names, ideogram abbreviations via a fixed table), class label permuted inside log2-frequency bins 1,000x. Egyptian (Noto Sans Egyptian Hieroglyphs, 1,072 glyphs): Gardiner classes A-L (people, body, animals) vs Z (strokes) as positive control; the 24 uniliteral phonograms vs both; no frequencies. Cuneiform (Noto Sans Cuneiform, 1,034 glyphs): signs vs numeric signs. Indus heads / openers / adjacent vs far middle on the identical 5-part scale, with and without W617. Proto-Elamite: no glyphs in the repository, not measurable (`tools/dark_loop50_c3.py`, `dark_loop50_shape.py`; loop50_cycle3.txt, loop50_calib_signs.json) | **Positive control works: Egyptian living things vs strokes AUC 0.97 (curvature-only 0.94).** Linear B ideograms > syllabograms: AUC 0.74 (curvature-only 0.61); frequency-matched difference +0.42, null [-0.08, +0.34], P = 0.003 (curvature-only P = 0.075, complexity alone P = 0.072). Egyptian uniliteral phonograms are LESS pictorial than the semantic classes (AUC 0.14) but more than strokes (0.75): N37 -2.0, Q3 -2.0, D21 -1.4 ... G17 +0.7. Cuneiform, an abstract script, still separates signs from numerals at AUC 0.82: the index reads simple-vs-complex as well as picture-vs-stroke. Indus on the same scale: heads vs far middle AUC 0.66 (curvature-only 0.60), matched P = 0.004 (curvature P = 0.22, complexity P = 0.002); **without W617 AUC 0.63, P = 0.14, complexity P = 0.07**; openers vs far middle AUC 0.43 (P = 0.67); adjacent vs far middle 0.62 (P = 0.04, complexity P = 0.002, curvature P = 0.50). Linear B Spearman(index, log freq) -0.35, Indus -0.15 | **Linear B shows the predicted ideogram / syllabogram split (AUC 0.74, matched P = 0.003) and Egyptian shows that even phonetic signs in a pictographic script stay pictures; the Indus head / middle contrast is weaker (0.66, 0.63 without one sign) and is the complexity component, not the curvature component that carries the Linear B and Egyptian splits.** Indus heads do not look like Linear B ideograms set against syllabograms; the Indus middle is no more stroke-like than its heads once W617 and frequency are allowed for. Proto-Elamite could not be calibrated (no glyph source). |
| S-DARK-50.4 | **Pictoriality by slot, part 4: IM77 replication and the S-DARK-27 prediction.** (a) 2,605 IM77 texts (lines joined, sign-0 texts dropped) parsed with the cycle-2 frame parser after mapping every Wells class set to Mahadevan numbers through bridge_extended (OPEN M150/267/293/391, MARK M99/100/123, SUF M1/176, CL M12/15/60/211/254/328/342, plus M66/M245 from the proposals in the second run); each M sign's shape index = that of its bridged Wells glyph(s); same frequency-matched permutation, 1,000x; run with bridge_extended only (129 M signs with >= 5 tokens and a glyph) and with the S-DARK-27 proposals added (171). (b) Prediction: the 129 S-DARK-27 newly bridged rare Wells signs, never used in a glyph test: slot of the M partner in IM77 (head = final before suffix in >= 50% of >= 3 tokens; middle otherwise) x Wells glyph class (index above / below the non-numeral median); the arrow predicts middle -> abstract, head -> pictorial (`tools/dark_loop50_c4.py`; loop50_cycle4.txt) | (a) IM77 heads vs far middle: PICT_SHAPE +0.07 (null [-0.41, +0.28], P = 0.45) with bridge_extended, +0.03 (P = 0.52) with proposals; curvature-only +0.02 / +0.03 (P = 0.85 / 0.47); complexity +1.6 / +1.2 (P = 0.22 / 0.53). Heads: M211 arrow -0.7, M328 -0.6, M342 jar -0.3, M254 box -0.0, M60 -0.0, M12 +0.6, M15 +0.9; W617 (M245) is a middle sign in IM77 (proposal), so the Wells head effect of part 2 does not transfer. Openers -0.08 / +0.02 (P = 0.36 / 0.27), suffix -0.64 / -0.45; only COUNT separates (curvature-only P = 0.03, complexity P = 0.02, i.e. stroke numerals). (b) 129 proposals, all with IM77 tokens: **middle-slot proposals 115, of which abstract 51 (0.44; binomial vs 0.5 P = 0.26), head-slot 14, of which pictorial 8 (0.57)**; share pictorial 0.56 in the middle vs 0.57 in head position. Wells-attested rare middle signs (5-10 tokens) for reference: abstract 48/74 = 0.65. Most abstract middle proposals W392, W420, W600, W442; most pictorial W617, W345, W346, W821 | **Replication fails for the only Wells effect: in the Mahadevan transcription heads are not more picture-like or more complex than the middle (P = 0.45-0.85), and the predicted split in the 129 newly bridged rare signs is absent (0.44 abstract in the middle vs the 0.5 chance line; 8/14 heads pictorial).** The 'code for non-speakers' arrow (recognisable pictures in the shared slots, arbitrary shapes in the identifier slot) is not supported anywhere in the sign repertoire: frame signs (openers, connectives, suffixes, most heads) are among the plainest shapes, and the middle holds the same mixture of pictures and geometric figures as the heads. What the repertoire does show (S164, this loop) is a frequency-driven simplification that treats all slots alike, as in Linear B (Spearman -0.35) and cuneiform. Transcription-robust null (Wells and IM77 are largely the same objects, S312). |
| S-DARK-51.1 | **Origin tracing of the foreign-found texts (loop 51, cycle 1).** Catalogue: 34 objects with signs from 22 find-spots outside the Indus culture area (Mesopotamia: Ur 5, Kish 2, Girsu, Nippur, Tell Umma, Tello; Iran: Susa 2, Luristan, Tepe Yahya; Gulf/Oman: Karzakan 3, Ra's al-Junayz 3, Failaka 2, Salut 2, Dilmun, Janabiyah, Qala'at al-Bahrain, Saar, Hajar, Kalba; Central Asia: Altyn Depe, Gonur, Shortughai), all present in `merged-corpus-canonical.json` (older build, S-DARK-23); 31 distinct texts of >= 2 signs (17 round Gulf-type seals, 7 square seals, 1 cylinder, 4 pots, 1 sealing, 1 tablet). Miri Qalat and Shortughai flagged as borderland (Shortughai kept, Miri Qalat excluded). Home populations: Mohenjo-daro 1,883, Harappa 2,528, Lothal 202, Kalibangan 199, Dholavira 203, Chanhu-daro 72, other-Indus pooled 218 texts. Scorer (a): per-site boundary bigram model (START/END tokens, so opener and closer menus, frozen pairs and site-local stock texts enter together) with Dirichlet backoff to the pooled corpus (k = 10) -> log likelihood ratio per site, assignment = argmax; (b) nearest-neighbour normalised edit distance per site, ties broken at random; (c) exact copies at each site (S366 reuse). Calibration: leave-one-out assignment of all 4,396 home texts of >= 2 signs (object removed; and all copies of the text removed). Null for the foreign set: 1,000 draws of 31 length-matched home texts from (i) the corpus mix and (ii) a uniform mix of the six named sites, read off their LOO assignments. Levels seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all; sign space W and M (bridge_extended + loop27 + S-DARK-27 proposals); home population with and without stock-text copies collapsed | **Power: LOO accuracy 0.42 (object removed) vs 0.22 chance, 0.27 vs 0.18 with all copies removed (stock texts carry a third of the site signal); recall MD 0.33, Harappa 0.54, Lothal 0.37, Chanhu-daro 0.40, Kalibangan 0.23, Dholavira 0.16; precision is high only for MD (0.73) and Harappa (0.86), 0.04-0.13 for the small sites. NN accuracy 0.23-0.26 vs 0.14 (size-biased toward MD). Foreign assignments (seq_raw, W): Chanhu-daro 6, Harappa 6, Kalibangan 6, Dholavira 5, other-Indus 5, Mohenjo-daro 2, Lothal 1; top-site share 0.19 vs corpus-mix null 0.29 [0.19-0.42] (P(>=) = 0.998), top-2 0.39 vs 0.49 (P = 0.98): the foreign texts are MORE dispersed over home sites than home texts are. The one consistent per-site effect is a Mohenjo-daro deficit: 2/31 vs 5.8 expected [2-10], P_lo 0.044-0.052 at all three levels, 1/31 vs 4.9 (P_lo 0.025-0.032) with stock texts collapsed. Kalibangan excess 6 vs 3.0 (P_hi 0.07, W) rises to 8 vs 2.8 (P_hi 0.004) in M space but falls to 5 vs 4.9 (P 0.58) once stock-text copies are collapsed (W): it is the eight K-69..75/K-156 copies of the twins text that make START-W91 look Kalibangan-specific; in M space with dedup 8 vs 4.7 (P 0.08), driven by the proposed bridge entries. LR margin (best - second) foreign 1.02 vs length-matched home 1.45 [0.97-2.05], P_lo 0.05: foreign texts are assigned less confidently. LR and NN agree on 7-8 of 31. Exact copies at home: 0 of 31 in W space; 3 in M space, all 2-sign texts (Kish 240-643 = MD 240-636, Ur 90-405 = MD 90-390, Ra's al-Junayz 4-405 = Banawali 4-390), where 47% of home 2-sign texts also have a cross-site twin. By shape: round seals abroad (17) go Harappa 5, Kalibangan 3, Dholavira 3, Chanhu-daro 3, other 3, MD 0; square seals abroad (7) one each to seven labels | **The foreign-found texts do not trace to a home city. No site receives more of them than chance at every level and in both sign spaces; the only robust signal is negative (Mohenjo-daro, the largest producer, gets 1-2 of 31 against 5-6 expected), and the Kalibangan pull is the S149 twins text itself. A port concentration (Lothal/Dholavira) is absent: Lothal 1-2 vs 3.5-4.9 expected. The method has real but modest power (0.42 LOO accuracy, mostly from MD/Harappa stock texts), so a true single-city origin would have shown as a top-site share above 0.29; it is 0.19. Consistent with S276 (foreign texts misfit the home grammar, not a particular site) and S-DARK-6 (no geography in the signs). Caveat: 31 texts, 76% of the home population is MD+H, and M-space results depend on S-DARK-27 proposals. |
| S-DARK-51.2 | **S149 tested directly with the enlarged bridge (loop 51, cycle 2).** K = Kalibangan K-69..75/K-156 (91-56-2-417-890-892, 8 moulded tablets of one mould, S272) vs J = Bahrain Gulf seal no. 10 = Janabiyah (71-31-831-55-121-99-55 in Wells; S276), under four equivalences: exact Wells (seq_raw and seq_all), M collapse (bridge_extended + loop27 + S-DARK-27 proposals), M + composite split from xlits (W56 = 55 55, W91 = 90 90, W93 = 1 90), sign-set overlap. Statistics: edit distance, longest common substring, shared sign types (multiset), shared bigrams, same first sign. Chance: 20,000 cross-site home pairs of lengths 6 x 7 and 48,000 of lengths 5-8 x 5-8 under the same statistics. Also: every run of K (91-56, 56-2, 2-417, 417-890, 890-892) and the person + grid pattern searched over all distinct texts (home, foreign, Unknown); person(90/91/93/71) + grid(55/56) co-occurrence foreign vs home (Fisher); for all 31 foreign texts the nearest home text under each mode and the cross-site twin rate of home texts of the same length (60 per length) | **K and J are not twins in any transcription-level sense.** Exact and M: edit 7 of 7, LCS 0, 0 shared signs, 0 shared bigrams, different first sign (W71 is the s-variant of W70 in xlits, not the twins W91; Wells does not read twins on J at all). Only the composite split gives anything: both texts then contain two 12-grids (K's W56 = 55 55; J's two W55), LCS 1, 2 shared types, which 21-23% of unrelated cross-site home pairs of these lengths also reach; 'same first sign + >= 2 shared types' holds for 2-3% of random pairs and K-J fail it. **No text anywhere repeats any run of K**: 91-56, 56-2, 2-417, 417-890, 890-892 each occur only on the eight Kalibangan copies (K's five rare signs are unbridged: W91, W56, W417, W890 have no M number; W892 -> M307 proposed). The W56 link is 10 texts = K x8 + H-14 (56-415-60-240-772-740) + L-115 (31-56-705-575), neither near J. What does survive is a pattern: person + 12/24-grid in one text occurs in 3 of 31 foreign texts (Qala'at al-Bahrain 190-60-55-90-160, Saar 55-220-91-1-93-31, Janabiyah) vs 4 of 3,535 distinct home texts (K itself, H-1710, L-124 bale sealing, M-1369 round seal), Fisher P = 2 x 10^-5; the exact adjacency 55-90 occurs on Qala'at and an Unknown round seal, 90-55/91-55/55-55 nowhere. Nearest-home-text verdicts for the 31 foreign texts (MC mode): 3 exact twins, all 2-sign texts (Kish 240-643 = MD 240-636; Ur 90-405 = MD 90-390; Ra's al-Junayz 4-405 = Banawali 4-390 x3), 8 near (d = 1; 7 of them 2-3-sign), 20 chance; home 2-sign texts have a cross-site exact twin 47% of the time and 3-sign texts 17%, so 3/9 foreign 2-sign twins is the chance rate. Same on seq_all | **S149 does not survive as a text identity: it was never a shared text in our data, only Parpola's reading of a drawing plus a shared rare grid, and the Wells transcription of the Bahrain seal shares nothing with the Kalibangan tablets. It survives, downgraded, as what S276 already made it: the Kalibangan tablets are the one inland text written in the Gulf 'person + grid' formula (P = 2 x 10^-5 for the formula's foreign enrichment, but K is 1 of 4 home instances). No cross-Gulf identical text exists in the corpus beyond chance-rate 2-sign coincidences. Would revive: a photo of Bahrain no. 10 showing a 24-stroke block (S272) or any new Gulf text with 91-56 / 417-890. Transcription-robust (Wells only has J; IM77 has no Bahrain seals), not replicated. |
| S-DARK-51.3 | **Is there a reduced export register (loop 51, cycle 3)?** Sets of distinct texts >= 2 signs: FA = round/cylinder seals found abroad (18), FS = square seals abroad (7), FO = pots/sealing/tablet abroad (6); controls HR = home round/cylinder seals (10), HS = home square seals (1,889), HO = home pots/sealings/tablets (1,233), UR = Unknown-provenance round seals (7). Per-text: opener-initial, closer-final, any frame, person sign (90/91/93/71), person first, numeral, share of tokens in the home top-50 signs, share of rare tokens (< 5 home texts), share of adjacent pairs attested at home (outside the text), type-token ratio; per set: distinct signs, Simpson concentration, own top-10 share. Null: 1,000 set draws of home texts matched one-to-one on object shape class and length (FA vs HR with fallback to HS at missing lengths; FA vs HS; FS vs HS; FO vs HO; HR vs HS as the shape control; UR vs HS; all foreign vs all home). Direct FA vs FS contrast by label permutation (1,000x). Three merge levels | **No reduced inventory.** FA uses 45 distinct signs in 79 tokens vs 37.3-38.7 [32-43] expected from shape-matched home round seals (P_hi 0.001, i.e. MORE varied) and 50.3 [43-57] from home square seals (P_lo 0.08); all foreign 65-67 vs 68.6-71.1 [60-80] (P 0.23-0.31); Simpson and own-top-10 concentration at chance throughout; top-50 share 0.58-0.63 vs 0.67-0.69 (P_lo 0.04-0.08) and rare-sign share 0.15-0.16 vs 0.08 (P_hi 0.006-0.013): the foreign texts draw on the rarer end of the home inventory, the opposite of a pidgin. **The frame loss is a shape effect, the person sign is not.** FA vs home round seals: opener 0.00 vs 0.08, closer 0.17 vs 0.14, frame 0.17 vs 0.18 (all n.s.); home round seals themselves lack the frame (closer 0.00 vs 0.55 of length-matched square seals, P = 0.001; S46 replicated); attested-pair share FA 0.36 vs 0.43 (P 0.17, seq_raw) / 0.52 (P 0.01, seq_strong/all; thin stratum). FA vs home round: person 0.72 vs 0.12 [0.00-0.22] (P 0.001) and person-first 0.39 vs 0.001 (P 0.001) are the only robust differences at every level. FS (square seals abroad) vs HS: nothing at P < 0.07 (frame 0.29 vs 0.61, P 0.07; attested pairs 0.71 vs 0.68; person 0/7). FO vs HO: rare share 0.23 vs 0.08 (P 0.046), else chance. UR (Unknown round) behaves like FA: person 0.57, attested pairs 0.29 vs 0.70, rare 0.33 vs 0.08 (P 0.001). FA vs FS directly: person 0.72 vs 0.00 (P 0.004), attested pairs 0.36 vs 0.71 (P 0.06), frame 0.17 vs 0.29 (n.s.). Inventory of all foreign texts: 127 tokens, 67 signs, 2 unseen at home (W117 Hajar, W340 Ur); repeated signs are W90 x7, W1 x7, W220 x7, W740 x7, W2 x6, W390 x5; FA alone 45 signs with W90 x7, W1 x7, W55 x5, W91 x4, W31 x4 (S47's person + number vocabulary) | **There is a foreign register, but it is not a reduced code: it is the round-seal (Gulf) formula, person(s) + strokes + grid, written with a full and if anything rarer sign inventory, and it belongs to the seal format, not to the find-place (S46, S276 replicated on a shape x length null; Unknown-provenance round seals carry it too). Square Indus seals carried abroad keep the home grammar within what 7 texts can show (frame 2/7, rule-of-three bound on 'no frame abroad' not met). Pidgin and abbreviation hypotheses fail: distinct-sign count, concentration and type-token ratio are at or above home levels. Caveat: 18 round texts, 10 home round controls; the person-sign enrichment is the whole register signal. |
| S-DARK-51.4 | **Held-out check on IM77's West Asian finds (loop 51, cycle 4).** IM77 texts 9801-9905 (17 with signs, lines of one text concatenated, 0 dropped) linked to the 34 Wells foreign texts through the bridge (W -> M via bridge_extended + loop27 + S-DARK-27 proposals; unbridged W signs cannot match; accept a unique best within cost 1 + 0.15 L, the loop 24 rule). Origin assignment in M space with the IM77 home populations (Mohenjo-daro 1,540, Harappa 985, Lothal 165, Kalibangan 99, Chanhu-daro 66, other sites 34) by the same per-site boundary bigram LR model; LOO calibration on 2,740 IM77 home texts; null = 1,000 length-matched draws (corpus mix, uniform mix). Agreement of IM77-based with Wells-based (cycle 1) assignments on the linked texts, chance by pairing permutation. Register features (IM77 frame: openers M267/M391/M293, closers M342/M211/M15/M12/M254/M162/M169, person M1 = W90, M3 = W93) vs IM77 home seals matched on length | **Linked 4 of 17 strictly** (9821 = Kish 240-643 read with a jar 67-244-342; 9822 = Kish 416-840-60-3-220-590-390-740; 9833 = Ur 90-1 fragment; 9846 = Dilmun 415-803-1-717-354); **13 not strictly linked**, of which at least 9811 (Umma sealing 29-336-99-104-162, cost 2 through the 390 -> M161/M162 split) and 9852 (Girsu 51-103-345-307-342, unbridged W744) are the same objects read differently, so the genuinely new IM77 WA material is about 8-11 texts (9801, 9832, 9842, 9845, 9851, 9901-9905), not 13. LOO accuracy in IM77 0.39 vs 0.21 chance (MD recall 0.33, Harappa 0.48, Lothal 0.42, Kalibangan 0.31). Assignments of all 17: other-Indus 8, Kalibangan 3, Harappa 2, Chanhu-daro 2, MD 1, Lothal 1; 'other sites' (34 texts) 8 vs 3.1 [0-7] (P_hi 0.009; uniform null P 0.015), MD 1 vs 3.7 (P_lo 0.08); the not-strictly-linked 13: other-Indus 6 vs 2.4 (P 0.02), MD 1 vs 2.8. The 'other sites' pull is the smallest population absorbing texts whose LR is negative at every named city (9801, 9811, 9822, 9842, 9845, 9846, 9902, 9904 are all negative at MD, Harappa, Lothal and Kalibangan): unlike-everything texts, not an origin. Agreement on the 4 doubly assigned texts: 2 of 4 (9821 Harappa, 9846 other-Indus), permuted expectation 1.0 (P 0.35); the two disagreements (9822 Dholavira vs other-Indus, 9833 Harappa vs Kalibangan) are small-margin calls. Register in IM77 (16 WA texts >= 2 signs): opener-initial 0.00 vs 0.27 [0.06-0.50] (P 0.009), closer-final 0.38 vs 0.65 (P 0.03), attested pairs 0.42 vs 0.76 (P 0.001), rare share 0.13 vs 0.04 (P 0.001), distinct signs 56 vs 47.8 [40-55] (P_hi 0.015), person 0.25 vs 0.05 (P 0.004); not-strictly-linked WA texts alone: opener 0/12 (bound < 25%), person 3/12, attested pairs 0.41 | **Replicates the negative on transcription-independent material: the IM77 West Asian texts, 8-11 of them not in Wells, likewise trace to no home city (MD 1 of 17), misfit every home population at once (which the model expresses as a pull to the smallest pool), and show the same register (no opener, fewer closers, unattested pairs, rarer and more varied signs) with no inventory reduction (56 distinct signs vs 48 expected). The Wells/IM77 assignment agreement (2 of 4) is at chance, as expected when neither assignment carries signal. Upper bounds: opener-initial foreign texts < 3/12 (IM77-only) and < 3/31 (Wells); MD-assigned foreign texts 1-2 of 31 (Wells) and 1 of 17 (IM77). Caveats: IM77's 'other sites' pool is 34 texts; person sign in M space rests on M1/M3 only (W91 twins is unbridged). |
| S-DARK-53.1 | **Capacity utilisation of the identifier slot, part 1: does the Indus middle fill its code space like a designed ID code (dense, even, Heaps exponent ~1, low Gini) or like a name stock (sparse, Zipfian, heavy reuse)?** Middle = S-DARK-26 definition (NAME + COUNT residue of the S310/S331 parser); duplicate texts collapsed to one per site x object type x text (5,369 rows -> 2,168 objects; 1,971 middles with >= 1 element, 1,590 with >= 2; 617 elements). Per corpus: combinatorial capacity (k^L summed over observed lengths; product of per-position observed alphabets; entropy capacity 2^sum H_i), realised distinct middles, Chao1 and Good-Turing P(next middle new), log10 utilisation = log10(Chao1) - log10(capacity), Gini and Zipf slope of middle frequencies, per-position evenness H_i / log2 K_i (positions with >= 20 texts), Heaps exponent (10 random orders, 5%-100% of n), adjacent-element MI excess over a shuffle; CIs from 100 0.8-subsamples centred on the full-n value; seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all; no-dedup and one-per-seal-row variants for comparison with S-DARK-18/26 (`tools/dark_loop53.py 1`) | **seq_raw: 1,971 middles, 1,591 distinct (uniqueness 0.81), Chao1 12,800 [9,100-12,200 at 0.8n], P(next middle new) 0.72. Capacity: k^L 10^41.9, per-position product 10^12.0, entropy capacity 10^11.6; utilisation 10^-37.7 / 10^-7.9 / 10^-7.5.** Middle-frequency Gini 0.18, Zipf slope -0.29, top-10 middles 6% of texts; per-position evenness 0.92 (0.66 against the whole inventory); Heaps 0.94; element Gini 0.74, element Zipf slope -1.28; adjacent MI excess 0.76 bits. Middles >= 2 elements: uniqueness 0.90, Gini 0.10, Heaps 0.97, Chao1 16,400. Without the dedup: uniqueness 0.61, Gini 0.36, Heaps 0.86 (the Harappa tablet series); seals one per object row: 0.84 / 0.16 / 0.95. Whole texts dedup: uniqueness 0.94, Heaps 0.98, Chao1 25,000. seq_strong / seq_all within 0.01 on every statistic (Chao1 13,000-13,400) | **At the level of identifier frequencies the slot is nearly flat (Gini 0.10-0.18, Heaps 0.94-0.97, no heavy-reuse head), which is the code-like end; at the level of code space it is extremely sparse (10^-7.5 of its entropy capacity, 10^-8 of the per-position product), so it does NOT fill a designed space densely.** The sparseness alone cannot discriminate (hierarchical designed codes are sparse too), so cycle 2 places these numbers on the reference ladder at matched n. Chao1 12,800-16,400 distinct middles agrees with S-DARK-26.3 (>= 8,800) and sits at the low end of the 10^4.5-10^5 credential stock of S-DARK-17.5 (Chao1 is a lower bound when f1/f2 is large: f1 = 1,426, f2 = 91). | S-DARK-48.1 | **Centralization index, part 1: how far apart are Mohenjo-daro and Harappa, measured against random halves of one city?** Wells complete direction-recorded texts >= 2 signs (3,155; merged-corpus-canonical, older build, S-DARK-23), collapsed to one per distinct text per site x object type (2,168; seals MD 916, H 254, Dholavira 62, Lothal 49, Chanhu-daro 42; all types MD 1,087, H 709, Dholavira 72, Lothal 65, Kalibangan 58, Chanhu-daro 47). For a pair of groups, n texts drawn from each (60 draws) and 17 divergence components computed: Jensen-Shannon divergence (bits) of unigrams, bigrams, text-initial signs, text-final signs, non-edge signs ('middle inventory'), lengths, the 30 pooled-commonest signs ('frame vocabulary') and all other signs; 1 - Spearman of sign frequencies (>= 5 pooled tokens); 1 - Jaccard of frozen-pair sets (adjacent >= 3x, >= 90% of co-occurrences); and, from the S310/S331 parser (dark_loop37), JSD of the opener menu, of the closer menu, mean |difference| of the opener/marker/closer/suffix/title rates, JSD of NAME tokens, of COUNT numerals, of TITLE tokens; whole-text reuse = share of distinct texts attested in the other group (uncollapsed objects). **Null = the same site split at random into two disjoint halves of n texts (300x), for each site large enough**; each between-site component reported as excess over within (bias-corrected) and as a multiple x = between / within (reuse: within / between, so > 1 = local). Designs: n = 200 (MD vs H), n = 30 (all 5-6 sites pairwise), and area-sections with >= 60 collapsed texts inside MD and H at n = 30 (seals: DKG(S) 345, DK- 82, DKG(N) 82, DKC 65; all types adds HRB 60 and Harappa F- 84, F Trench I 61), same-city section pairs vs cross-city section pairs. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all | **Seals, MD vs H at n = 200: every component within x0.96-1.20 of the within-city value except the two menus, and none significant.** Unigram x1.07 (0.151 vs 0.142 bits, P 0.15; strong/all x1.03), bigram x1.01, Spearman x1.14 (rho 0.69 vs 0.73), initial x1.04, final x1.06, middle inventory x1.01, length x1.00, frame-30 x1.20 / x0.96, rest x1.04, frozen pairs x1.01, NAME x1.04, numerals x0.99, TITLE x1.02; opener menu x1.50 (0.014 vs 0.009 bits, P 0.23; strong/all x1.60), closer menu x1.42 (0.030 vs 0.021, P 0.17; x1.64), slot rates x1.12 (x1.02). **Whole-text reuse on seals x0.75-0.79: a seal text is shared between MD and H MORE often than between two random halves of one city** (2.7% vs 2.0%). All types at n = 200: unigram x1.26 (P 0.003), final sign x1.44 (P 0.002), length x3.3 (P 0.002), frame-30 x2.4, opener x2.2, closer x2.15, rates x2.4, reuse x5.6 (P 0.002), but middle inventory x1.16, bigram x1.08, NAME x1.13, numerals x1.07 - the object-type mix (Harappa tablets with their count faces and suffix) and the tablets' site-local stock texts (S-DARK-12.3), as S-DARK-6.4 found. n = 30, all site pairs on seals (9 pairs, 3 levels): median unigram x1.05 (range 1.00-1.17), final x1.09 (0.96-1.40), NAME x1.03, closer menu x1.10 (0.87-1.45), rates x0.98 (0.78-1.65); the only pairs above x1.3 are Chanhu-daro vs MD/H on final sign, frame-30 and rates (Chanhu-daro's 42 seals, cf. S11 W31). **Area-sections (all types, n = 30): same-city section pairs x1.05 unigram / x1.05 final / x1.14 closer / x1.10 rates / x1.03 NAME (11 pairs); cross-city section pairs x1.14 / x1.28 / x1.27 / x1.54 / x1.07 (10 pairs)**: a DK-G(S) seal-and-tablet section differs from a Harappa F-mound section about as much as from another Mohenjo-daro section on the sign inventory and the names, and by about a quarter more on the frame rates (type mix). Reuse: same-city sections x1.9, cross-city x6.4 (stock texts stay in their city, S366). Same at all three levels (ratios within 0.05). | **On seals the two big cities are statistically one population: the sign frequencies, bigrams, middle inventory, NAME tokens, numerals and frozen pairs of Harappa seals sit inside the sampling noise of Mohenjo-daro's own halves, and seal texts recur across the two cities more than within one.** The only between-city excess on seals is a 40-60% enlargement of the opener and closer menu divergences, which is 0.005-0.009 bits in absolute terms and not significant at n = 200. Every strong city effect appears only when object types are pooled, and S-DARK-12.3 / S366 locate it in the tablets' local stock texts and in the Harappa voucher genre, not in the script. Calibration against systems of known administration follows in cycle 2. Rests on MD + Harappa; merge-robust. |
| S-DARK-48.3 | **Centralization index, part 3: transcription check and the frozen prediction on the small sites.** (a) IM77 (Mahadevan 1977, M numbers, 3,162 texts >= 2 signs, 2,373 collapsed; seals MD 1,105, H 320, Lothal 87, Chanhu-daro 56, Kalibangan 52) run through the identical code with the M-space frame parser (MOPEN/MMARK/MCL/MSUF of S-DARK-45), n = 200 (MD vs H) and n = 25 (all 5 sites pairwise), seals and all types. (b) Wells held-out sites: each of Lothal, Dholavira, Kalibangan, Chanhu-daro vs Mohenjo-daro at n = 25, and all held-out sites pooled (seals 272, all types 354 collapsed) vs MD and vs H at n = 90 with MD vs H at the same n; **prediction registered before the run: if the system is central, the held-out sites' opener-menu, closer-menu and slot-rate divergence from Mohenjo-daro sits inside the MD-H band (ratio <= 1.5 x the MD-H ratio and between-median below the within-MD 97.5th percentile)**; 8 components x 4 sites x 2 object sets = 64 band tests per level. seq_raw / seq_strong / seq_all for the Wells part | **(a) IM77 seals MD vs H, n = 200: unigram x1.05, bigram x0.98, Spearman x1.08, initial x1.05, final x1.03, middle inventory x1.02, frame-30 x1.18, rest x1.04, frozen x0.99, NAME x1.02, numerals x1.05, opener menu x1.16, closer menu x1.34, rates x1.11, reuse x0.91** (Wells: 1.07 / 1.01 / 1.14 / 1.04 / 1.06 / 1.01 / 1.20 / 1.04 / 1.01 / 1.04 / 0.99 / 1.50 / 1.42 / 1.12 / 0.75); all types x1.20 unigram, x1.24 final, x3.15 length, x2.3 opener, x1.75 closer, x2.4 rates, x3.5 reuse (Wells 1.26 / 1.44 / 3.31 / 2.19 / 2.15 / 2.44 / 5.58). IM77 n = 25, 10 seal pairs: unigram x1.00-1.12, NAME x1.01-1.12, closer menu x0.96-1.87 (Kalibangan vs Lothal 1.87 and vs Harappa 1.45 the only values > 1.4), rates x0.94-1.88. **(b) Band test, seals: 32 of 32 IN at seq_raw, 31 of 32 at seq_strong / seq_all (Dholavira opener menu x2.0, P 0.2); all types 32 / 32 at every level.** Pooled held-out seals vs MD (n = 90): unigram x1.07 (P 0.29), final x1.07, opener x1.09 (0.024 vs 0.022 bits), closer x1.22 (P 0.31), rates x1.50 (0.072 vs 0.048, P 0.13), NAME x1.06, numerals x0.96, reuse x1.34 (P 0.38); vs Harappa: x1.10 / 1.15 / 0.97 / 1.39 / 1.53 / 1.08 / 1.02 / 1.20; MD vs H at n = 90: x1.03 / 1.06 / 1.12 / 1.13 / 1.00 / 0.99 / 1.00 / 1.16. All types pooled vs MD: unigram x1.08, closer x1.21, rates x1.62 (P 0.12), NAME x1.07, **reuse x3.9 (P 0.010)**: the only significant held-out effect, and it is whole-text reuse on the non-seal objects. Strong / all levels within 0.1 of raw on every component. | **Transcription-robust and predicted: the small sites use the same frame menus and the same sign and name inventories as Mohenjo-daro, inside the Mohenjo-daro-Harappa band on 95-100% of tests.** The one component that drifts (slot rates x1.5, not significant, both transcriptions) is how often a text carries an opener or closer at all, which is a seal-vs-sealing and bar-seal genre matter (S29, S157); the one significant local effect is that non-seal texts are copied whole only within their own site (S-DARK-12.3, S366). Not a replication (IM77 reads 70% of the same objects, S312), but the held-out Wells sites are new material relative to the MD + H fit. Caveat: n = 25-90 per side; at these sizes a 10% unigram divergence excess is near the detection limit, so 'inside the band' bounds, it does not prove, uniformity. |

| S367 | **Annealed meaning search: fit a sign-to-attribute reading against outside facts** (`tools/strat_anneal.py`, `tools/strat_anneal_levels.py`; `data/derived/strat_anneal.txt/.json`, `strat_anneal_levels.txt/.json`; counts from the older `merged-corpus-canonical.json` build, S-DARK-23). Each of the 80 commonest signs gets one of 16 labels (COMMODITY-1..6, COUNT, OFFICE-1..4, PLACE, QUALIFIER, PERSON-MARK, CONNECTIVE, UNKNOWN; the rest OTHER); a text's reading = label multiset + first/last label + label bigrams + length bucket; one tempered naive-Bayes classifier per outside fact (object type, seal emblem, find-region, seal size class, material, coarse period) fitted on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa (region: all IM77 sites) and scored as bits/text gained over the prior on 458 texts from 71 non-IM77 sites (S349 split). Simulated annealing over the sign→label map. Objective A anneals on the held-out score itself (50 restarts × 3,000 steps); objective B anneals on Mohenjo-daro↔Harappa cross-prediction and scores held-out once (honest out-of-sample; 8 × 1,000 after a restart under heavy CPU contention). Controls: C1 facts permuted across texts (within train, within held-out), same search; C2 sign order shuffled within texts; raw sign-identity classifier (ceiling: a reading is a coarsening) and length-only classifier (floor); 50 random readings; label-aligned stability over the top-10 solutions vs 10 aligned random assignments; drop-one-block test (gain lost when each label block is merged into UNKNOWN); merge levels seq_raw / seq_strong by transferring the best readings and by a reduced anneal (4 × 800) with its own permuted control | **Baselines on held-out: raw signs 0.323 bits/text, length-only 0.242, random reading median 0.136 (max 0.265).** A (annealed on the test set): 0.904 vs permuted facts 0.054 (gap +0.85), order-shuffled 0.769, so most of A's score is search overfitting of 458 texts plus the length/type/material frame; its label blocks lose 0.04–0.09 bits each and 13 of 15 blocks 'predict' region, i.e. site, not meaning. **B (never saw held-out): 0.262 (top-10 median 0.231, range 0.17–0.29) vs its permuted-fact control 0.229 (top-10 max 0.315); gap +0.03, inside the spread.** B is below the raw-sign classifier (0.323) and barely above length-only (0.242); per fact only material (+0.24) and size (+0.09) gain, region and period lose. Stability: 1 of 80 signs keeps its label in > 80% of top-10 for A (W390) and for B (W400); mean agreement 0.46 / 0.43 vs 0.34 for aligned random assignments; A and B partitions agree on 0.888 of sign pairs vs 0.884 random. Merge levels: seq_raw transferred A 0.778 / B 0.235 (raw 0.351, length 0.232), seq_strong 0.883 / 0.255 (raw 0.321, length 0.240); reduced anneals reach 0.70–0.73 vs permuted 0.00–0.01 but their partitions agree with the seq solution at chance (0.899 vs 0.885; 0.889 vs 0.885). Verdict by the tool's own criterion (beat every permuted-fact solution and the length-only floor out of sample): **not identifiable** | **Null.** No annealed sign-to-attribute assignment beats the permuted-fact control out of sample; what the search finds is unstable across restarts, across the two objectives and across merge levels, and the only facts a reading predicts (material, size, type) are the frame and format rules already known (S29, S157, S330). The 16 label names carry no meaning in an outside-fact-only scorer (labels are exchangeable clusters), so even a positive result would have identified genre blocks (S270), not words. Lesson: annealing directly on the held-out score (objective A) inflates the gap to +0.85 purely by searching 80 free parameters against 458 texts; the permuted-fact control for that design must itself be a full search, and the honest figure is the cross-city design (B). Rests on MD + H for training; held-out sites are the test; not merge-dependent (same null at seq_raw and seq_strong) |
| S-DARK-45.1 | **Loop 45, cycle 1: who owns the middle inventory? Is the set of middle elements partitioned by head (closer), by site or by object type, or shared?** `tools/dark_loop45.py 1 LEVEL 1000` (logs `loop45_c1_*.txt/.json`). Complete direction-recorded texts >= 2 signs, collapsed to one per distinct text per site x type (S-DARK-13; 3,155 -> 2,167). Head = S310/S331 closer unit (11 + 'none'); middle split into the ADJACENT qualifier (sign before the closer, S303) and the NON-ADJACENT rest. Per element: distribution over heads, 7 site groups, 5 object types; MI(element, label) as % of H(label); 'loyal' = >= 80% of >= 5 tokens under one label; loyal to a NON-dominant label; share seen under >= 2 labels. Nulls 1,000x: labels permuted among texts within length x type, within length x type x site/head, and the S366 chain null (heads permuted among texts with the same adjacent sign). Three merge levels | **Texts with a closer (1,345):** NON-ADJACENT elements x head MI = 31.9% of H(head), excess over the permutation null +8.7% (seq_strong +8.7, seq_all +8.8; P = 0.001), **but under the S366 chain null (adjacent sign held) the excess is +1.6% (+2.0 / +2.3) and loyalty is at chance (0.365 vs 0.355, P = 0.43–0.77)**; 90% of well-attested elements occur under >= 2 heads (null 94%); exactly 1 element in 96 is loyal to a non-jar head (W468 -> 154, 4/5; null 0.02). ADJACENT qualifier x head (positive control): MI 66.9% of H(head), excess +36% (P = 0.001), loyal 0.68 vs 0.21, 5 non-jar-loyal. The 35 'head-loyal' non-adjacent elements are all jar-loyal (W590, W255, W806, W840, W17, W503, W176, W435 ...): members of the frozen pre-jar titles standing one slot left (S229), and the jar is the 67% default. SITE: excess +1.5% of H(site) (P = 0.007–0.027), loyal 0.156 vs 0.105 (P = 0.05–0.06); Mohenjo-daro vs Harappa +2.1%, loyal 0.287 vs 0.239 (P = 0.046); 97% of elements seen at >= 2 sites (null 98%). TYPE: excess +3.5% (P = 0.001) but loyal 0.60 vs 0.56 (P = 0.11), all to 'seal'; incl. unclosed texts +5.8% (the tablet voucher words). Same at all three levels; no result depends on a merge | **Shared, not partitioned.** The non-adjacent middle vocabulary is one pan-Indus inventory: it carries about 9% of the head information, of which all but 1.6–2.3% is already in the sign next to the closer (S303/S366), and it carries 1–2% of site information and 3% of type information, far below the 36% that the adjacent qualifier carries about its head. No office has its own identifier register and no city has its own: an element seen at Mohenjo-daro under the jar is as likely to turn up at Harappa under the arrow as chance allows. Cycle 2 asks whether weak block structure exists anyway; cycle 3 replicates on held-out sites and IM77 and tests the loyal elements on the 324 new texts |
| S-DARK-45.2 | **Loop 45, cycle 2: is there block structure in element x head or element x site?** `tools/dark_loop45.py 2 LEVEL` (logs `loop45_c2_*.txt/.json`; 40 permutations at seq_raw, 20 at seq_strong/seq_all because the machine was shared, P floors 0.024 / 0.048). Count matrices of non-adjacent middle elements (>= 5 tokens) x heads / sites / types (>= 20 texts), spectral co-clustering k = 2..8 (k by the largest gain of diagonal concentration over independence), element purity, null = labels permuted within length x type and re-clustered at the same k; adjusted MI between the head-partition and the site-partition of the same elements. Positive control: adjacent qualifier x head (S303). Outside controls through the same code: Ur III legends (owner-name syllables x profession title; whole name x title) and Proto-Elamite (non-final signs x final numeral class) | **Indus non-adjacent middle x head (93 x 8):** k = 5, gain +0.088 vs null +0.041 (2.1x, P = 0.02–0.05) but **element purity 0.551 vs null 0.559 (P = 0.51; strong/all P = 0.38)**: the blocks are {jar, 151, 156} (51% of tokens), {arrow, 527} = the fish words, 705/706 and 350-798-415 (the S299 arrow phrase), {700} = the Harappa tablet words, {617, 154} with 3 elements; i.e. the frame's own phrases, nothing finer. Adjacent qualifier x head: gain +0.336 vs +0.053 (6.3x), purity 0.77 vs 0.36 (P = 0.024): the S303 partition is recovered. Element x site: gain 1.5–1.7x null (P = 0.05), purity 0.42 vs 0.45 (P = 0.6–0.7); closed texts split {Mohenjo-daro + small sites} vs {Harappa, Dholavira} (the bar-seal/tablet axis), seals alone P = 0.05–0.29. Element x type: 1.4x, purity P = 0.10–0.24 (the tablet voucher words). Head-blocks vs site-blocks: adjusted MI 0.001–0.017. **Controls:** Ur III name syllables x profession gain 3.7x null with purity 0.55 vs 0.37 (P = 0.024): Akkadian-syllable names under arad2/aga3-us2/dam-gar3, Sumerian under dub-sar/gudu4/lunga, a real partition of the name stock by office; whole names 1.6x, purity P = 0.024; Proto-Elamite 8.7x (commodity signs choose the numeral system), purity at null. Same picture at all three levels | **No block structure beyond the frame.** The Indus middle does not divide into office registers (purity at chance; the only blocks are the known arrow and jar phrases) nor into city registers (purity at chance; the one split is Harappa/Dholavira bar-seals vs the rest), and the two weak partitions ignore each other. Ur III shows what a partitioned identifier stock looks like (ethnic name types by office, purity far above null); Proto-Elamite shows what commodity-class structure looks like (8.7x); the Indus sits at 2x with no purity gain, below both. Next: cycle 3 replication and the new-text prediction |
| S-DARK-45.3 | **Loop 45, cycle 3: replication of the ownership test on held-out Wells sites and on IM77, and the frozen prediction on the 324 IM77-only new texts.** `tools/dark_loop45.py 3 LEVEL 1000` (logs `loop45_c3_*.txt`). Cycle-1 statistics on (a) the 371 collapsed non-MD/H texts, (b) IM77 in M space with its own frame (2,373 texts; closers M342/211/12/15/254/60/245/328/66), with the S366 chain null; (c) loyal sets (>= 5 tokens, >= 80% one head / one site) fitted on Mohenjo-daro + Harappa (Wells) or on the 1,217 non-new IM77 closed texts, mapped W -> M where bridge_extended + loop-27 proposals are one-to-one, then scored on the new texts: share of tokens whose text carries the fitted head/site vs the base rate and vs heads permuted within length x type (1,000x); 0-of-n reported as < 3/n; also without the inflated C617 heads (M245 = W615 + W617). Three merge levels | **Held-out sites (203 closed texts, 22 elements):** non-adjacent x head excess +5.4% of H(head) under the plain null, **+0.5% (+0.4 / +0.3) under the chain null (P = 0.08–0.22)**, loyalty 0.318 vs 0.306 (P = 0.61), 0 of 22 elements loyal to a non-jar head (< 14%); x site excess +2.0% (P = 0.09), loyalty at chance (P = 0.9). **IM77 (1,372 closed):** excess +8.6% plain, **+2.2% under the chain null (P = 0.001)**, loyalty 0.388 vs 0.373 (P = 0.33), 1 non-jar-loyal element; transcription-robust, not replicated. **Prediction on the new texts (155 closed):** Wells-fitted loyal elements keep their head in 33 of 65 tokens (0.51) vs base rate 0.54, null 32.8 +- 3.8, **P = 0.54** (0.56 / 0.47); without C617 heads 33/50 = 0.66 vs 0.61 (P = 0.30); IM77-fitted set 48/81 = 0.59 vs 0.55 (P = 0.16); site-loyal elements at their own site 50/77 = 0.65 vs 0.57, null 53.4 (P = 0.88). On held-out Wells sites the MD/H jar-loyal set does score 52/62 = 0.84 vs 0.64 (P = 0.005–0.014), but 17 of the 52 hits are W590 of the frozen 590-390-jar title and all are the default closer; on all IM77 closed texts 425/541 = 0.79 (P = 0.001) only because 70% are the same objects (S312) | **The prediction FAILS, in the direction the shared-inventory reading expects.** An element 'loyal' to a head or a city at Mohenjo-daro + Harappa is no likelier than chance to carry that head or sit in that city on a text the corpus had never seen; the only reproducible head-information outside the adjacent qualifier is 0.3–2.3% of H(head) and comes from frozen pre-jar titles. The middle inventory is one pan-Indus pool used under every closer at every site. Caveats: 155 closed new texts give power only for a > 15-point effect; 77% of the new texts are still MD + Harappa; M245 inflates the C617 head |
| S-DARK-56.1 | **'Not names' retested against ONE-PERSON-PER-ENTRY name lists.** Comparators deduplicated to one copy per distinct name so uniqueness is 1 by construction and the test rests on element statistics: Ur III seal-owner names (CDLI @seal line 1, 12,493 impressions -> 1,348 distinct, sign tokens; also greedy-chunk 'elements'), Old Babylonian + Early OB seal names (3,177 -> 1,388), Old Assyrian (84 -> 69, small), Linear B personnel/flock/landholder series (DAMOS KN As/B/Ap/V/Da-Dv, PY An/Cn/Jn/Ea-Es/Ad/Ae..., 2,896 -> 1,649 distinct syllabogram tuples; KN and PY separately), Latin EDH 'dis manibus' names (1,197 -> 1,170, words; also letters). Indus = S-DARK-26 middles (NAME+COUNT residue), one per site x type x text then one per distinct middle, >= 2 elements: seq_raw 1,431 / seq_strong 1,403 / seq_all 1,402 / IM77 1,505. References drawn to n=1,431 x40 (full pool if smaller); Indus CIs by 0.8-subsampling. Statistics: element TTR, element reuse share, element Gini / Zipf slope / Heaps exponent; positional morphology = mean over elements with >= 5 tokens of \|P(initial) - P(final)\| minus a within-corpus element shuffle (lengths kept), and share of such elements outside the shuffle 95% band; bigram reuse, reversed-pair share, adjacent MI, pair predictability (S-DARK-26), substring share, all minus the same shuffle null (`tools/dark_loop56_prep.py`, `tools/dark_loop56.py 1`; corpora data/derived/dark/loop56_corpora/, log loop56_cycle1_log.txt) | **Element reuse: Indus is indistinguishable from the deduplicated Ur III name list.** TTR 0.107 / 0.105 / 0.103 (raw/strong/all) vs Ur III 0.108, OB 0.077, LinB 0.014, Latin words 0.574; reuse share 0.960 vs 0.958 / 0.970 / 1.000 / 0.534; Gini 0.733 vs 0.718 / 0.727 / 0.455 / 0.395; Zipf -1.26 vs -1.26 / -1.42 / -1.06 / -0.52; Heaps 0.50 vs 0.47 / 0.40 / 0.07 / 0.86. **Positional morphology: Indus is BELOW every name list.** pos_excess 0.168 [0.150,0.183] / 0.158 / 0.155 / IM77 0.147 vs Ur III 0.256 [0.250,0.262], Ur III elements 0.249, OB 0.200 [0.196,0.206], OA 0.354, LinB 0.236 [0.223,0.251] (KN 0.238, PY 0.204), Latin 0.293 (letters 0.113); pos_bound 0.37-0.41 vs 0.48-0.67 for every name list (letters 0.83). Bigram reuse excess 0.25-0.26 vs Ur III 0.31, OB 0.35, LinB 0.07-0.13, Latin 0.07; reversed pairs -0.32 to -0.35 (recurring pairs almost never reverse) vs names -0.09 to -0.39 (all negative: not diagnostic); MI excess 0.38-0.43 (IM77 0.83) vs Ur III 0.74, OB 1.12, LinB 0.51, Latin 0.17; pair predictability 0.06-0.07 (IM77 0.13) vs 0.10 / 0.16 / 0.10 / -0.02; substring share 0.05 (IM77 0.09) vs 0.09 / 0.04 / 0.04 / 0.004. All three merge levels agree within CI; IM77 differs only on MI / predictability (bridge collapses signs). | **The Ur III comparator was indeed the wrong one for FREQUENCY statistics: once names are deduplicated to one per person, their element-reuse profile (TTR, Gini, Zipf, Heaps) matches the Indus middles almost exactly, so S321 / S-DARK-18's uniqueness contrast measured homonymy in a seal-user population, not the internal make-up of the strings.** What still separates the Indus middles from every real name list is positional morphology: real names (Sumerian ur-/lu2-/-DN, Akkadian DN-verb, Linear B -jo/-ta/-wo endings, Latin praenomen-nomen-cognomen) have slot-bound elements (prefix/suffix/theophoric position), and the Indus middle shows 60-70% of that (0.16 vs 0.20-0.29), with fewer position-bound elements (0.38 vs 0.48-0.67). Whether 0.16 is 'name-like but weaker' or 'code-like' needs the non-name poles (cycle 2). Caveat: COUNT numerals inside the middle may dilute Indus position statistics; NAME-only field in cycle 2. |
| S-DARK-49.4 | **Calendar / cyclic slot hunt, part 4: replication.** (A) Field profiles of 49.1 on the 404 Wells texts from held-out sites (Lothal 86, Dholavira 72, Kalibangan 65, Chanhu-daro 48, Nausharo 19 ...; qualifiers learnt on MD+H only) and on IM77 (2,801 texts; 369 from the small sites) with the class sets bridged W -> M (W34 -> M95 a flagged proposal). (B) Time-stamp test of 49.2 on IM77's own excavation levels (ft below datum: Mohenjodaro 1,191 dated texts, 112 signs; Harappa 676, 75 signs), peak share in 4-ft bands vs levels shuffled within object_type and within object_type x locus (1,000x), BH; cross-transcription check (IM77 mean level percentile vs Wells mean depth per bridged sign) and IM77 cross-city agreement. (C) Cross-side neighbour pairing of 49.3 on IM77 `side` data (445 texts with >= 2 inscribed sides, 329 of them Harappa tablets and sealings) for numerals, tall numerals, closers and fish, vs sides reassigned within site x object_type (500x). Three merge levels (`tools/dark_loop49_c4.py`) | **(A) Held-out sites: every field name-band**, NAME K=246-259 D/K 0.29 slope -0.90 to -0.94; COUNT K=72-75 D/K 0.24; TITLE K=31-34 D/K 0.52; CLOSER K=10 D/K 0.21-0.22 slope -1.78; openers K=4-5 D/K 0.57-0.80; the only field above D/K 0.9 is 350-798-415 (K=3, 29 tokens). | S-DARK-56.3 | **Held-out predictions of a 'names' reading + the parser-stripping control + closed-slot test.** (a) WHOLE Indus texts (dedup, n=2,035 / 1,994) vs WHOLE Ur III legends (4,954 distinct, sign tokens) and WHOLE Linear B personnel-series lines (783 distinct), same statistics, to remove the asymmetry that the Indus middle was stripped of its frame while names were not. (b) Positional-morphology transfer: initial/final preference of elements with >= 5 tokens fitted on split A, scored on held-out split B as share of those elements' tokens at the A-preferred edge vs within-name shuffle of B (200x), plus share of elements frequent in both with the same preferred edge; Indus MD+H -> other sites and MD -> Harappa (seq_raw, seq_all, IM77); positive controls Linear B Knossos -> Pylos, Ur III Umma -> Girsu / Puzris-Dagan (loop48 site field); ICD-10 and Proto-Elamite random splits. (d) Closed-slot test: coverage of the 10 commonest initial / final / interior elements and normalised slot entropy, Indus vs name lists vs PE / HTS / ICD vs frequency-matched random (`tools/dark_loop56.py 3`, `dark_loop56_c3c.py`, `dark_loop56_suffix.py`; loop56_cycle3*.json, _log.txt, loop56_suffix_log.txt) | **(a)** Indus whole texts pos_excess 0.189 / pos_bound 0.425 / MI 1.02 / predictability 0.17 (seq_all 0.188 / 0.439 / 1.05 / 0.18); Ur III whole legends 0.107 / 0.468 / 2.17 / 0.32; Linear B whole lines 0.175 / 0.613 / 1.03 / 0.18. Stripping the frame lowers Indus pos statistics only 0.02-0.05, so the cycle-2 gap is not a parser artefact; whole Indus texts match Linear B personnel LINES (name + ideogram + number) on MI and slot excess, not Ur III legends (which are 2x more predictable). **(b)** Indus positional preferences transfer: MD+H -> held-out 0.333 vs shuffle 0.258 +- 0.013 (z 5.6; excess ratio 1.29; 42 shared elements, 83% same edge), MD -> H 0.405 vs 0.306 (z 7.9; 1.32; 78%); seq_all 1.33 / 1.34, IM77 1.28 / 1.45 (67-74% same edge). Controls: Linear B KN -> PY 0.399 vs 0.260 (1.53; 79%), Ur III Umma -> Girsu 0.600 vs 0.322 (1.86; 88%), Umma -> Puzris-Dagan 1.78 (93%), ICD 1.72 (100%), Proto-Elamite split 1.46 (86%). Indus transfers, but with the smallest excess of every system tested, below Proto-Elamite. **(c)** Only 30 sealings survive the complete/direction filters (Lothal 9, Mohenjo-daro 7); Lothal: 1 of 9 verbatim on a Lothal seal (permuted 0.006, 97.5% 0.111), 1 of 9 on another site's seal; element coverage by Lothal seals 0.857 vs permuted 0.668 (97.5% 0.786): marginal, n=9, inconclusive. **(d) Decisive: the Indus middle has NO closed initial or final set.** Top-10 initials cover 0.243 of middles (seq_all 0.260, IM77 0.270, NAME-only 0.234) and top-10 finals 0.267 (0.298 / 0.335 / 0.284), against 0.257 / 0.261 for frequency-matched random strings; interior top-10 0.35 (random 0.28). Every name list has a concentrated edge: Ur III initials 0.484 (ur-, lu2-, a-...), finals 0.332; OB 0.403 / 0.393 (DN heads, -DN tails); Linear B initials 0.466, finals 0.628 (-jo, -ta, -wo...); Latin initials 0.291 (praenomina), finals 0.111 (open cognomina). Proto-Elamite finals 0.388 (commodity sign), HTS 0.69, ICD 0.83-0.95. Normalised entropy of the Indus initial / final slot 0.87 / 0.87 = random 0.87 / 0.87; name lists 0.77-0.89 / 0.80-0.85; Linear B final 0.805. | **The 'not names' conclusion survives the fair comparator, but on a different statistic than before.** Uniqueness and element-frequency arguments (S321, S-DARK-18, S-DARK-53's Gini/Zipf/Heaps) are retired as name tests: they measured homonymy and the Zipfian sign inventory. What a one-per-person name list has and the Indus middle lacks is a CLOSED EDGE SET: every attested name system (Sumerian, Akkadian, Mycenaean Greek, Latin) builds names from an open stem plus a small set of recurring heads or tails (theophoric element, patronymic/adjectival ending, praenomen), so 10 elements cover 30-63% of one edge; in the Indus middle the edges are as open as the interior and as open as random strings over the same alphabet (0.24-0.30), replicated at all three merge levels, IM77, NAME-only, and not an artefact of frame stripping (whole texts (a)). The order structure the middle does have (slot-bound elements, MI 0.4, transferable across sites, z 5-8) is weaker than in Proto-Elamite entries and far weaker than in names, i.e. it is the structure of a short formulaic string with soft positional habits, not of a morphology. **Fair statement of what remains possible:** a name system written with a large open sign set and no recurring onomastic elements (no theophoric heads, no suffixes) would look like this; no such attested system is in our library, and the three with the biggest sign inventories (Ur III, OB: 440-480 signs) still show the closed edge. Predictions of a positive 'names' reading: (1) a closed suffix / head set emerging on more data: FAILS now at n=1,431 and at IM77; (2) positional preferences transferring to held-out sites: PASSES weakly (ratio 1.3 vs 1.5-1.9 for names), but Proto-Elamite also passes, so it tests structure, not names; (3) same-site seal-sealing element sharing: untestable (9 sealings), needs the S-DARK-23 rebuild and findspot-level data; (4) patronymic pairs (two texts from one findspot sharing a stem): untestable without co-find data. S-DARK-26's 'free combination' stands; 'middle = name' stays C, now with a specific killer: a recurring edge set covering >= 0.35 of initials or finals would revive it. |
| S-DARK-3.4 | Pipeline screen: 20 random two-step compositions of the 15 transformations x 4 tests (74 arrows, train, P = 20), then held-out confirmation of the six non-trivial survivors on seq_raw/seq_strong/seq_all with the bigram-generated corpus and Ur III names/legends as controls (BPE arrows at reduced size P = 20, 10 parameterisations, machine shared) | 47 of 74 reach |z| >= 3; 41 are the frame (slot test), class-count collapse (family/randfamily/emblem/objtype), generic middle-out or an order-invariant test re-run; bar as cycle 2 plus 'null corpus must not reproduce it' | dropsign>reverse lifts the uniqueness ratio to 0.98 on train (z +20) but fires on the null corpus (+4.2) and the held-out base is already 1.05: additive re-statement of cycle 2. sort>nonnum (-4.1 to -4.7) is larger on the null corpus (-9.5): test artefact. bpe>reverse/namecalib super-additive on train (+10 to +14) but held-out +0.1/+3.7/+0.9. bpe>reverse/repeat is the one arrow that holds everywhere: train +5.1/+6.9/+8.0, held-out +5.0/+5.5/+3.7, null corpus +1.3, Ur III names go the other way (1.13 -> 1.66). Reversal only changes the null (slot shuffle aligned from the END), so the result reads: with compound terms fused and slots counted from the end, within-text repetition is 0.5-0.9 of chance, not 0.23 -- S88's repetition avoidance is mostly sign-level counting plus a start-aligned null on end-anchored texts. Not a new system; predicts nothing outside the corpus. | 1 of 6 pipeline survivors holds; it re-states end anchoring + compound terms and corrects S88's size |
| S-DARK-58.1 | **The minimal text, part 1: which slot survives when one or two signs are written?** 302 clean 1-sign and 467 clean 2-sign texts (complete, no lost sign, one copy per die; current inscriptions.csv rebuilt), tabulated by object type, site and emblem; every sign classed by its majority slot role in the 1,399 complete texts of >= 4 signs (S310/S331 parser: opener, marker, numeral, title, closer, suffix, middle; UNSEEN = never in a text >= 4). Role mix vs signs drawn by overall frequency, by frequency in texts >= 4, and from the same object type's own texts, 1,000x each; seals, tablets, sealings, pots, bangles separately; seq_raw/strong/all | **1-sign SEALS (57) are drawn from the middle inventory, not from the heads: 33 middle + 16 UNSEEN = 86%, head 4 (7%; W740 x3 at Mohenjo-daro, Bhirrana, Daimabad; W226 x1), opener 1, numeral 3, title 0.** UNSEEN is 16 vs 1.0 expected (P < 0.001; all three nulls), markers 0 vs 4-5 (P = 0.01-0.03); closers 4 vs 8.3-8.6 expected (P = 0.11, under but not significant at n = 57); 9 of the 57 signs are hapax in the whole corpus (16% vs 2-3% of tokens in 2-sign and 4+-sign seals). **1-sign TABLETS (31): the same, 13 middle + 13 UNSEEN (84%), head 3 (W156 x2, W740), numeral 1.** **1-sign POTS (159) are numerals: 90 (57%) vs 19-21 expected (P < 0.001), tall 2 x39, 3 x19, 4 x9; openers 0 vs 10, closers 7 vs 24, titles 0 vs 7, middles 36 vs 78 (all P <= 0.002).** Bangles (19): middle 68%, nothing significant. **2-sign texts are the frame in miniature: closers 187 vs 133-142 expected (P < 0.001), numerals 189 vs 109-123 (P < 0.001), openers 25 vs 58-67 and markers 13 vs 65-81 (both P < 0.001), titles 27 vs 43-46 (P = 0.002-0.02).** On SEALS (246) closers are 135 vs 70-75 (27% of tokens, 1.9x; P < 0.001 under all three nulls) and openers 12 vs 31-37; on TABLETS (108) nothing but UNSEEN departs from frequency; on POTS (73) numerals 51 vs 17-19 (P < 0.001). Identical arrows and P values at seq_raw, seq_strong and seq_all (closer set gains W158 at raw) | **The head is not what survives in a one-sign seal; the individual element is.** A lone sign on a seal or tablet is a middle-inventory sign, over-represented among signs that never appear in long texts and among hapaxes, which is the signature of a name-like (open-class) element, not of a title (closed class of 8) or an issuer mark (openers 1 of 57). The jar alone is 3 of 57 seals. Pots are a different medium: the one mark is a tall numeral, i.e. a quantity or lot label, never an opener. The two-sign text, by contrast, keeps the head: X + closer is the dominant seal pattern and the closer is doubled against frequency. So the compression order is opener/marker first to go (gone already at two signs), then the head (gone at one sign), the middle element last. Caveats: 57 and 31 are small; 1-sign seals are 49% Mohenjo-daro; the "UNSEEN" excess partly reflects that any sign confined to short texts is unseen by construction, so the hapax share (16% vs 2-3%) is the cleaner number |
| S-DARK-58.2 | **The minimal text, part 2: are 2-sign texts frame-ordered pairs, and are lone-head seals the used ones?** 467 clean 2-sign texts (one per die): share whose two signs stand in frame order (role rank from cycle 1) and share attested as an adjacent bigram in texts >= 4, vs random pairs drawn by overall and by long-text frequency (1,000x) and vs the same pairs read backwards; recurring pairs listed against the frozen pairs of GRAMMAR.md / S-DARK-26; exact-text matches of clean seals on sealings (TAG) and tablets by seal length (1-head, 1-other, 2, 3, 4+), same site and any site; seq_raw/strong/all | **Frame order kept in 89% of 2-sign texts (seals 93%, tablets 87%, pots 79%) vs 65% [61, 70] for frequency-drawn pairs and 35% read backwards; 51% are attested bigrams of long texts vs 29-32% [25, 37] null (seals 60%).** Seal patterns: middle + closer 80, numeral + middle 41, numeral + closer 28 (3-156 x7, 31-156 x5), middle + middle 27, title + closer 12, UNSEEN + closer 9; 130 of 246 seals end in a closer-class sign, 6 start with an opener, 37 are two middles. Recurrence: 339 distinct 2-sign texts, 69 recur on >= 2 dies (197 dies), 270 are singletons; 66 of the 197 recurring dies are the frozen numeral + item and pre-jar pairs already listed (3-390 x9, 3-156 x9, 33-700 x6, 16-390 x6, 31-156 x6, 142-617 x5, 176-740 x5, 4-390, 240-520, 550-527, 32/34-700, 100-740), the rest are opener + marker alone (861-2 x5, at Banawali 3) and X + jar pairs with a fresh X (61-740 x5, 416-740 x4, 910-740 x3, 803-740 x3). **Impressions: 1-sign seals with a head sign 4, of which 1 of 2 at a sealing site matches a sealing (W740 at Mohenjo-daro; CI 0.10-0.91); other 1-sign seals 2 of 45 (4%), 2-sign 7 of 213 (3%), 3-sign 0 of 227 (< 1.3%), 4+ 0 of 959 (< 0.3%).** Any-site matches fall the same way: 1-other 19%, 2-sign 8%, 3-sign 2%, 4+ 0.6%; tablet copies 25%, 32%, 13%, 2%. Same at all three levels (raw: 67 of 190 frozen; order 87%) | **Two-sign texts are not free pairs: they are the frame with the middle cut to one sign, "X + head" on seals and "count + item" on pots and tablets, and a third of their recurrence is the frozen-term list of S-DARK-26; the rest is X + jar with X varying, i.e. the same construction with a new individual element.** The impression test cannot separate "lone head" from "short text": the jar-alone seal that matches a sealing is one object, and every match rate is a monotone function of length (short texts recur because a 1-2 sign string is easy to duplicate by chance across 2,600 texts), so S-DARK-16's "closer-bearing short texts get copied" is a length effect first (S-DARK-30.4 agreed). Nothing here says a lone-head seal was used more than a long one; the upper bound for long seals (< 0.3% matched on site) is the firm number |
| S-DARK-58.3 | **The minimal text, part 3: calibration.** Same code on every corpus: a token type's position class in its own long texts (FIRST / LAST / INTERIOR / RARE = < 3 long-text tokens), then the class mix and hapax share of 1- and 2-token texts vs frequency-drawn tokens (1,000x). Ur III seal legends (2-word legends; no 1-word legends in the set), Linear B tablet lines (1-token = NUM and ideograms), Latin EDH (1-word = names), Indus Wells seals/tablets/pots at seq_raw/strong/all, IM77 seals | **Indus 1-sign seals have the name-only signature: RARE 0.47 vs 0.05-0.06 expected and hapax 0.16 vs 0.02, with the LAST (head) class at its frequency rate (0.16 vs 0.19-0.20) and FIRST (opener) below it (0.04 vs 0.09).** That is the Latin 1-word profile (RARE 0.56, hapax 0.32, LAST 0.00) and the Ur III 2-word profile (RARE 0.30 over, LAST 0.06 under, hapax 0.18), not the Linear B 1-token profile, which is LAST-class over-represented (ideograms and NUM, 0.03 vs 0.01-0.02) with RARE inflated by fragments. **Indus 2-sign seals are the only minimal class with the LAST class doubled (0.33 vs 0.19-0.20; IM77 0.27 vs 0.18): a name + title legend, like the Ur III 2-word legend (name + dumu/dub-sar) but with the title in the final slot.** Indus 1-sign pots match the Linear B single-entry profile instead (INTERIOR/numeral class 0.82, FIRST 0.00, LAST 0.07 under): commodity or count marks. Tablets 1-sign: RARE 0.58, hapax 0.29, i.e. one-off signs, not the voucher unit. IM77 reproduces the seal results in its own numbering (RARE 0.30 vs 0.02-0.03; 2-sign LAST 0.27 over) | **Minimal Indus seal texts behave like name-only legends, not title-only legends and not commodity ideograms; the one-mark pot behaves like a count entry.** The hapax excess (8x the frequency null on seals, 15x on tablets) is the same statistic that marks the name slot in Latin and Ur III, and it is the opposite of what a closed title inventory would give. With 57 and 31 texts the class shares carry +-0.13 intervals, and "RARE" in Indus includes signs confined to short texts for whatever reason (e.g. the zebu label W930, S223), so the comparison is at the level of profile shape, not of a fitted model. Transcription-robust (Wells and IM77 agree), not replicated on new material |
| S-DARK-52.1 | **How many signs? Part 1: a merge graph from four independent evidence types** on the 713 Wells signs (seq_raw; 10,725 pairs with both signs >= 8 tokens, 4,067 rare-sign pairs). Vetoes: adjacent in >= 2 texts, both stroke numerals. Rules compared: any k of 4; and 'principled' = one non-distributional witness (E3 or E4) AND one distributional (E1 or E2). Control: the same edge count drawn at random from the candidate universe, 200x. First pass (loop52_cycle1a.txt) used MI alone for E1 and fine >= 0.667 for E3 (`tools/dark_loop52_c1.py`, rescored `dark_loop52_c1b.py`; `loop52_merges.json`) | **First pass: E1 (neighbour predicts the form) is positive for 57% of ALL pairs and fine >= 0.667 for 31%, so 'two types agree' chained 212 signs (jar, marked jars, numerals) into one class: the S268 lesson again.** Rescored: E1 2,314, E2 295, E3 571, E4 81 positive pairs; >= 2 types 92 pairs, >= 3 types 7, all four 1 (154/156). Agreement between types: E3 & E4 36x chance, E2 & E4 9x, E2 & E3 2.8x; **E1 & E2 at or below chance (0.7x), and every E1+E2-only edge joins same-slot paradigm-mates** (100/760/923/482/222 before the jar; 154/520/161/241 after 33 and 806; 240/175/72 after 798), i.e. two distributional witnesses agree on 'same slot', not 'same sign'. Principled rule: 32 pairs, 10 of them S268 canonical, 22 new (697/700, 840/844, 900/906, 61/63, 595/597, 552/555, 226/232/241, 32/315, 176/388, 70/72, 97/142, 460/463, 467/468, 904/927, 151/154/156, 803/812/831/838, 806/831/838, 831/832); the graph alone re-finds 24 of the 27 S268 merges at k >= 1 and 13 at k >= 2. **Ladder: raw 713; canonical strong 695; canonical all 689; + principled graph 669 (44 signs merged, largest class 7, Chao1 900); + any 2 of 4 types 626 (largest class 26, Chao1 859); + any 1 type 288 (one class of 404 signs, Chao1 429).** Random edge sets of the k>=2 size give 598 +/- 1 classes (real 626: real edges cluster on hubs). Single-type routes: context only 488 classes (largest 226), shape only 430 (largest 59), free interchange only 569 (largest 114), transcriber only 645 (largest 7) | **Principled merging removes 44 of 713 Wells numbers (6%), not hundreds: 713 -> 669 signs in use, Chao1 total about 900.** No defensible evidence rule gets near a syllabary: the only way to reach 288 'signs' is to accept any single witness, which fuses the jar, the openers, the closers and the numerals into one class of 404 (cycle 3 shows that destroys the frame). The 'contextual bound form' effect of S-DARK-46 is real but is not an allograph detector: neighbour-conditioned form choice is also what two different signs in one slot look like, so it can only confirm a shape or reader link. Caveats: 22 of the 58 Mahadevan lumps and all 219 once-only signs are untestable by usage (< 8 tokens) and stay separate, so 669 is an upper bound on the used inventory and the lower bound from shape-only merging of rare signs is not estimable here; fine similarity is a weak witness (strong merges span 0.49-0.99); the bridge-derived E4 shares objects with the IM77 data used in cycle 3, so cycle 3 tests use E1-E3 only |

Rules out: a syllabary (60-120) at any merge level, including the indefensible maximal one (288 seen, 450 total); a small closed code (khipu/ECO-like spectra). | S-DARK-59.1 | **How much of the script is explained, part 1: every model as a predictive distribution P(next sign or END \| text so far, object class, site), scored in bits.** Ladder: uniform over the inventory; unigram per object class (with END and an UNK mass); Kneser-Ney Markov-1/2/3 (type token in the context); the STRUCTURAL model = EM-interpolated mixture (weights per frame zone and active-component set, fitted by 5-fold deleted interpolation) of (i) a slot-state automaton (zone START/opener/marker/middle/closer/suffix x previous sign class x opener flag x middle length, Witten-Bell backoff) that predicts a sign CLASS then the sign, with middle elements drawn from one pan-Indus shared pool (S-DARK-45/53), (ii) RULES = the 139-149 fit-set bigram continuations with count >= 4, P >= 0.12 and O/E >= 3 (frozen pairs, qualifier -> head, numeral -> item, opener -> W2/W60, closer -> END), (iii) a site-local whole-text prefix cache and (iv) a pan-Indus prefix cache, both adaptive (texts already scored at a held-out site enter its cache: mould duplicates and stock texts), (v) the unigram; ablations frame-only, frame+rules; COMBINED = structural + KN2 + KN3 (EM weights per zone); the S366 generator (M* = textreuse+closerdep+type+open+site) as a Monte-Carlo predictive distribution (KN-3 with site + type tokens fitted on 60 synthetic corpora of the evaluation set's object mix); the loop 33 ALERGIA automaton (all types + type symbol, class alphabet; class bits + P(sign \| class) from the fit set). Per-sign cross-entropy, 95% bootstrap CI over texts, per-text perplexity, gap closed = 1 - bits/unigram bits (oracle = 0 bits), per slot of the S310 parse (OPENER, MARKER, COUNT, NAME, TITLE, CLOSER, SUFFIX, END). Gap closed vs unigram: KN2 14%, structural 13%, combined 17%; S366 and loop 33 1-2%. IM77-only: unigram 6.45, KN2 5.15 (20%), structural 5.27 (18%), combined 4.92 [4.76-5.08] (24%), S366 5.67 (12%), loop 33 6.45 (0%).
